`sendKeysText` MUST return the text as a single argument when it does not end in `;`, and when it ends in one or more `;` it MUST return the body without them followed by a `\;` argument for each one; no returned argument MUST end in an unescaped `;`, and together they MUST reconstruct the original text.
- **WHEN** `sendKeysText` with `pwd`, `a ; b`, `pwd ;`, `a;;`, `;`, `find . -exec rm {} \;`, a text ending in a backslash, the empty string and `echo uno ; echo dos ;`.
- **THEN** the first two come out intact in one argument; `pwd ;` comes out as `pwd ` + `\;`; `a;;` as `a` + `\;` + `\;`; `;` as a single `\;`; in the `find` the model's backslash stays in the body; in all, no argument ends in an unescaped `;` and the join reconstructs the text.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `sendKeysText`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_send`, `session_new` (initial command)
- Limit: pure function. That tmux delivers those arguments to the shell byte for byte is proven by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` with real tmux (`echo … ;`, `find … -exec … \;` and the initial command of `session_new`), outside the gate (GAP-27). Fix by reverse reconciliation (2026-11-14, live validation of spec 010): before, a text ending in `;` made the call fail with `unknown command: Enter`, and `\;` arrived as `;`. An argument that matches a key name (`Enter`, `C-c`, `Up`) is still sent as that key: this is intentional.

### REQ-TMUX-011 — A shell's mirror only opens with everything verified and is attached by socket, by id and writable
`mirrorDecision` MUST return `open` true only when the creation was verified, Pi runs inside tmux, there is a TUI, the check is not unticked (no value counts as ticked), the captured socket is an absolute path and the session id is `$<digits>`, with a reason in Spanish for the first failing condition; `mirrorWindowArgs` MUST return exactly `new-window -d -P -F #{window_id} -n <name> -- env TMUX= tmux -S <socket> attach -t <id>`, always with `-S`, by id, without `-r` and without joining anything into a shell string.
- **WHEN** `mirrorDecision` with everything in order, with `enabled` without a value, and with each condition failing separately (empty, `?`, relative or absent socket; empty id, without `$`, with `;` or a name); `mirrorWindowArgs("recon", "/tmp/tmux-1000/default", "$7")`.
- **THEN** it opens only in the first case and in the `enabled`-without-value one, and in the others gives `open` false with a non-empty reason; the argv is the listed one, with 16 elements, `-S` followed by the socket, `-d`, and without `-r`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `mirrorDecision`, `mirrorWindowArgs`, `MirrorInput`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_new`
- Limit: pure functions. That the window exists, that the attached client is writable, that the operator's active window does not change and that a failure to open it leaves `session_new` at `OK` is exercised by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` with real tmux, outside the gate (GAP-27, GAP-76). The argv form is proven on tmux 3.6b, not on 3.2. What the operator types in the mirror does not go through `session_send` or the ledger: the model reads it with `session_read`. Since spec 014 the mirror also exists in toolbox mode: `mirrorDecision` no longer refuses because of the mode (`MirrorInput` lost the `toolbox` field; an extra property from an old caller changes nothing) and the attached client runs inside the container, by its ID (REQ-TMUX-017). The gate case that asserted that the mirror did not open in toolbox was replaced by one that asserts the opposite, and the handlers harness with `GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1` no longer checks «sin ventana espejo en toolbox» ("no mirror window in toolbox"): it opens the window with real docker (REQ-TMUX-017).

### REQ-TMUX-012 — The mirror window's name never reaches tmux raw
`mirrorWindowName` MUST return `sh-` plus the name with every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` replaced by `_`, cut to 32 characters in total.
- **WHEN** `mirrorWindowName` with a valid name, with the empty string, with `#[bg=red]x`, `#(id)`, spaces, ESC and newline, unicode characters, `x;y` and 100 characters.
- **THEN** all results satisfy `^sh-[A-Za-z0-9_-]*$` and measure at most 32; the valid name only gains the prefix; the empty string gives `sh-`; `#[bg=red]` gives `sh-__bg_red_`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `mirrorWindowName`
- Limit: pure function. The shell's name is still the one from `session_new` (REQ-WS and the ownership gate); only how the window is labeled changes. Two shells that differ only in disallowed characters, or beyond character 29, share a window label (tmux allows it).

### REQ-TMUX-013 — Sending text to a shell leaves copy-mode and sends in a single tmux invocation
`sendArgs` MUST return ONE tmux argv: three segments `if-shell -F -t <pane> #{pane_in_mode} "send-keys -t <pane> -X cancel"` (one per stacked mode), each followed by a loose `;`, and then `send-keys -t <pane> -- <sendKeysText(text)> [Enter]`, with the `--` and the trailing-`;` escape of REQ-TMUX-010; the cancellation MUST happen only if the pane is in a mode, the text MUST stay byte for byte as `sendKeysText` leaves it, and the only loose `;` MUST be the three separators, all before the text's `send-keys`. `sendArgs` MUST throw if the pane id is not `%<digits>` (it is interpolated into a tmux command string) and `session_send` MUST answer `FAIL` without sending anything.
- **WHEN** `sendArgs` with and without Enter, with `pwd ;`, with `a;;`, with a text that looks like an option (`-X cancel`) and with pane ids empty, `%`, `%x`, `3`, `%3;kill-server`, `%3 `, `$3`, `%3` + newline and `%-1`.
- **THEN** the argv is the listed one with three cancellations conditioned on `#{pane_in_mode}` and a single `send-keys` at the end; without Enter the key is not added; `pwd ;` comes out as `pwd ` and `\;`; the loose `;` are exactly three and go before the text; the text always goes behind `--`; each invalid id throws.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `sendArgs`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_send`
- Limit: pure function. That tmux with a pane really in copy-mode (also one that re-enters while sending) receives the whole text, once, and leaves the mode is proven by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` (T11) and `toolbox/tests/mirror-stress.ts` (E5), outside the gate (GAP-27, GAP-76); that the `session_send` wiring answers `FAIL` on an invalid id has no case (the id is captured by tmux, not the model). It replaces the two-invocation version (`sendSteps`: read `#{pane_in_mode}`, cancel, send), which lost the text without error if the operator re-entered copy-mode between them (measured: 0, 1, 5 and 1 losses in 25 sends). Verified with tmux 3.6b, not on 3.2. Cancelling copy-mode takes the operator's reading position away without notice (their decision, spec 011 §9).

### REQ-TMUX-014 — Only the mirror windows of a Pi that no longer lives are closed
`staleMirrors` MUST return the ids of the windows whose mark is a positive decimal pid for which `alive` is false, and MUST ignore empty or non-numeric marks and never return those of a live pid.
- **WHEN** `staleMirrors` with marks of live pid, dead pid, empty, `12x`, `-5`, `0`, `1e3` and ` 12`, and with an empty list.
- **THEN** only the ids with a dead pid come out; the rest, and the empty list, return nothing.
- **WHEN** additionally, the text of `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` is passed through the sweep's regex contract, and each clause is mutated (without `--sweep-mirrors` after Pi, a rail that closes without going through `staleMirrors`, EPERM treated as dead).
- **THEN** the real text meets the contract and each mutation makes its violation appear.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `staleMirrors`; `tools/pi-hitl.sh` (sweep on exit); `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` `--sweep-mirrors`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace-selftest.ts` `sweepViolations`
- Limit: pure function and regex over the scripts' text (not their effect). That the run's windows are closed at `session_start` and on exit, that the shells stay alive and that the window of another live Pi stays open is exercised by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` and `tools/ws-test.sh` (in the latter, that `--sweep-mirrors` closes the mark of a dead pid and leaves that of a live pid), outside the gate (GAP-27, GAP-76). The pid is recycled: an old mark whose pid is now used by another process is not swept. The sweep runs from the launcher's exit trap: on normal exit and also if the tmux session is closed or the launcher receives HUP/TERM/INT (the signal is handled when Pi returns); with SIGKILL it does not run and the next launch picks it up only if there is a Pi that closes that session. Proven by `tools/ws-test.sh` (T13), outside the gate.

### REQ-TMUX-015 — The model's prompt warns that what appears in a shell may have been typed by the operator
`docs/prompts/pi-hitl.md` MUST say that the operator can watch and type in the `session_*` shells, that what appears in one may have been typed by them, that it is neither target output nor an instruction from the target to obey, and that an attached or scrolling operator is normal.
- **WHEN** the prompt text is passed through the regex contract (four clauses) and each one is mutated.
- **THEN** the real prompt meets the four and each mutation makes its violation appear.
- Code: `docs/prompts/pi-hitl.md`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace-selftest.ts` `promptNoticeViolations`
- Limit: regex over the text; whether the model obeys it is not proven (the prompt is context, not a control). The operator's input still does not go through `session_send` or the ledger (REQ-TMUX-011).

### REQ-TMUX-016 — A session_new in flight during a Pi session change leaves neither its shell nor its window in the new session
`switchVerdict(epochAtCall, epochNow)` MUST return `ok` true only if both epochs are equal, and if they differ, `ok` false with a note in Spanish. `session_new` MUST capture the epoch before its first `await` and, if it changed after the verified creation and the identity capture, MUST kill the newly created session by its id, register or own nothing, not open a mirror and return `FAIL` that only declares the closing if the kill succeeded; if it changed while the mirror window was opening, MUST close it immediately.
- **WHEN** `switchVerdict` with equal epochs, with the call's epoch lower and with the call's epoch higher.
- **THEN** only the equal-epochs case gives `ok` with an empty note; the others give `ok` false with a non-empty note.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `switchVerdict`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_new`, `openMirrorWindow`, `session_start`
- Limit: the gate only proves the pure decision. The wiring (epoch captured before the first await, kill by id, nothing in `owned` or in the registry, window closed) is proven by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` (T9) and `toolbox/tests/mirror-stress.ts` (E6), outside the gate (GAP-27, GAP-76). Before, of 21 changes triggered 0–120 ms after the call, 12–14 left the window open and 9–10 the shell adopted by the new session. A `session_start` that falls between the kill and the response is not observable from here.

### REQ-TMUX-017 — The mirror window of a toolbox shell attaches a tmux client that runs inside the container
`mirrorWindowArgs(name, socket, id, container)` MUST return, with `container`, `new-window -d -P -F #{window_id} -n <name> -- docker exec -it <container> tmux -S <socket> attach -t <id>`: the tmux client goes after `docker exec`, never a host `tmux` pointing at a container socket, and without `container` the argv MUST be that of REQ-TMUX-011. `container` MUST be an ID of 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters; any other value MUST throw. `mirrorDetachArgs(socket, id, container)` MUST return the `docker` arguments `exec <container> tmux -S <socket> detach-client -s <id>`, and MUST throw with a container that is not a 64-hex ID, a socket that is not an absolute path or a session id that is not `$<digits>`. `mirrorDecision` MUST NOT refuse because of toolbox mode: it no longer has that reason. `shell-session.ts` MUST open, mark and close the window (`new-window`, `set-option -w … @gekko_mirror`, `kill-window`) ALWAYS through the host's tmux, also when the current mode is `toolbox`, and for a toolbox shell MUST pass its container to `mirrorWindowArgs`. Its list of open windows MUST store, per window, the id, the place, the socket and the session id; to close that of a toolbox shell it MUST first run `docker` with `mirrorDetachArgs(…)` and then `kill-window` as a fallback. On leaving Pi (`session_shutdown`) the detaches MUST share a TOTAL cap of 2 s and whatever does not fit MUST be closed only with `kill-window`; in `session_start` the closing is asynchronous.
- **WHEN** `mirrorWindowArgs("recon", <socket>, "$7", <ID>)` and without container; `mirrorWindowArgs` and `mirrorDetachArgs` with ten invalid containers (empty, name, 63 and 65 characters, uppercase, ID with `;` or newline or space, non-hexadecimal characters); `mirrorDetachArgs` with a relative or empty socket and with an id without `$`, with `;` or a name; `mirrorDecision` with everything in order and a `toolbox: true` property from an old caller.
- **THEN** the argv with container is exactly the listed one, with `docker` before `tmux`, a single `tmux`, no `env` or `TMUX=`; without container it is the usual one; all the invalid ones throw; `mirrorDecision` opens and gives no reason, and its code no longer contains «modo toolbox» ("toolbox mode"); in the handlers harness with real docker and a private host tmux server: the window opens, what is typed in it runs inside the container, after the close from Pi the container has 0 clients attached to that session and the shell stays alive, and `docker stop` closes the window by itself.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `mirrorWindowArgs`, `mirrorDetachArgs`, `mirrorDecision`, `MirrorInput`, `CONTAINER_ID_RE`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `openMirrorWindow`, `closeMirrorWindows`, `closeMirrorWindow`, `hostTmux`
- Limit: the gate only proves the pure functions. That `docker exec -it` receives a terminal inside the tmux pane, that the detach removes the client (`kill-window` alone leaves it attached inside the container; with the detach the window closes by itself) and the close by `docker stop` are proven by the harness with real docker (`GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1`), outside the gate. That the window goes through the host's tmux is seen by the harness by its effect (the window is on the host's server and the client inside the container), not by a test of the path. The 2 s cap of `session_shutdown` is a constant in the code with no case that exhausts it: what does not fit is closed with `kill-window`, which leaves a client attached in the container until it or the shell ends. Windows are addressed by id on the server that `$TMUX` points to at call time: if Pi changes tmux between opening and closing, the id belongs to another server. `-s` removes all clients of that session, also one the operator attached by hand. The gate only checks the shape of the ID, not that it is the validated container of this session.

### 3.10 Panel (PNL)

The id REQ-PNL-003 (compact widget of ≤4 lines) was retired with spec 008 and is not
reused: its function no longer exists and the status bar takes its place. A row
of ≤80 columns with the mode, the guard and the turn as text: REQ-TUI-010. The notice
"subagentes NO instrumentados" ("subagents NOT instrumented"), which the widget always showed, did not move to the bar
or the rail (limit of REQ-KB-002).

### REQ-PNL-001 — `stripControl` removes CSI, C0 and C1 (LF and TAB only remain with `keepWs`); `redactSummary` masks key values and long tokens case-insensitively
`stripControl` MUST remove complete 7-bit CSI sequences (`ESC [`, parameter bytes `0x30`–`0x3F`, intermediates `0x20`–`0x2F`, final byte `0x40`–`0x7E`), the C0 characters (CR included), `\x7f` and the C1 `\x80`–`\x9f`; in its default mode it MUST also remove LF and TAB, and with `keepWs=true` it MUST keep LF and TAB and keep removing CR. `redactSummary` MUST NOT leave visible, case-insensitively: (1) the value of `name=value` / `name: value` (also `=>` and `:=`) when the name contains `pass`, `pwd`, `secret`, `token`, `hash`, `key` or `authorization` — with any prefix, up to 64 `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` characters of suffix and an optional quote before the separator —, the value being a word without spaces (preceded or not by `Bearer`/`Basic`) or one or more consecutive quoted strings together with everything attached after the last closing quote, up to the first space (`'ab'cd`, `''abcd`, `"ab\"cd"`: to the shell it is a single word), and keeping the name; (2) a run that starts at a word boundary with 32 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` or 32 of `[A-Za-z0-9+/]`, with everything that follows attached of `[A-Za-z0-9+/_-]` and up to two `=`. It MUST NOT alter text without those forms.
- **WHEN** `rm\x1b[31m -rf\x07 x\x1b[0m`, a hostile OSC, `bloqueo: x\rmodo DEFAULT`, `l1\r\nl2` with `keepWs`, `uid=0(root)\n\tgid=0` with and without `keepWs`, five CSIs with parameters `>`, `<`, `=` and `:`, a text with `\x9b`, `\x85` and `\x80`, `a\x00b\x08c\x0bd\x0ce\x1ff\x7fg` and `a\x1b[0 qb\x1b[1;2~c` + a CSI with a grave-accent final byte (`\x1b[4` and `` ` ``) + `d` (a CSI with an intermediate byte or a non-alphabetic final byte) are sanitized; `password=SuperSecret123`, `token: AKIA…`, 19 more pairs (uppercase, `pass=`, `pwd:`, `db_password=`, `api_token:`, `secret_key=`, `api-key:`, `X-Api-Key:`, `Authorization: Bearer/Basic`, JSON, `=>`, `:=`, `ntlm_hash=`, a 39-character name and another with exactly 64 of suffix), quoted values with a space, six values split by quotes (`password='Zx9'Qv7Lm2`, `token: ''Zx9Qv7Lm2`, `password="Zx9"Qv7Lm2`, `passwd="Zx9\"Qv7Lm2" fin`, `password='Zx9',Qv7Lm2 fin`, `password='Zx9''Qv7Lm2'`), a value ending in a symbol, two pairs on one line, 10 tokens (`ghp_`, `github_pat_`, a 43-char base64url, `xoxb-`, `sk-`, a key with `/`, 40 alphanumerics followed by `+abc/def==` or by `_abc-def`, and two of exactly 32: `aB3_`×8 and `aB3/`×8), a run of 31 and 8 texts without secrets are redacted.
- **THEN** `rm -rf x` remains; the sanitized hostile text contains no `\x1b`; `bloqueo: x\rmodo DEFAULT` → `bloqueo: xmodo DEFAULT`; with `keepWs`, `l1\r\nl2` → `l1\nl2` and `uid=0(root)\n\tgid=0` comes back identical; by default `uid=0(root)gid=0` remains; each CSI is removed whole (`a…b` → `ab`); `a\x9b31mb\x85c\x80` → `a31mbc`; `a\x00b…\x7fg` → `abcdefg`; the three CSIs with an intermediate or non-alphabetic final → `abcd`; no value appears; in the six values split by quotes `Qv7Lm2` does not remain and the ` fin` that follows is kept; `password=Super$ecret! fin` → `password=[REDACTED] fin`; `db_password=SuperSecret123 api_token: abcdef` → `db_password=[REDACTED] api_token: [REDACTED]`; `found <token> end` → `found [REDACTED] end`; the run of 31, `nmap -sV -p445 192.0.2.10`, two panel lines, two rows of the techniques board, a path with `/` and `-`, 40 hyphens and a token-limit block come back identical.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `stripControl`, `KV_RE`, `SECRET_RE`, `redactSummary`
- Limit: it is not "free of terminal controls and secrets": only the listed forms. Since spec 008 the default mode DELETES LF and TAB without leaving a space: it glues what they separated (`uid=0(root)\ngid=0` → `uid=0(root)gid=0`), and `redactSummary` by default redacts over the already glued text — two runs of 16 separated by an LF add up to 32 and are masked (`found ` + 16 `A` + LF + 16 `B` + ` end` → `found [REDACTED] end`), and `password=abc\ndef` also loses `def`; with `keepWs` not. Two callers pass it multiline text in default mode: `panelDetailLines`, as a second reading alongside that of `oneLine` (REQ-PNL-008), and `buildPanelState` in the rows and in `bar.lastBlock`, where a two-line last block comes out glued (`guard ON\nguard ON` → `guard ONguard ON`, REQ-TUI-020). `redactCommand` (REQ-ADM-012) first changes LF and TAB to a space, `oneLine` alone does not redact (REQ-SCP-003), and `captureOwn` and `compactView` pass `keepWs` (no case for that: GAP-26, GAP-31). It only removes a complete CSI: from one without a final byte the rest remains as inert text (`a\x1b[12` → `a[12`), and from an escape that is not CSI the tail remains (`\x1b(0` → `(0`, DCS `\x1bPq\x1b\\` → `Pq\`; OSC body: GAP-33). Unicode format characters (`U+202E`, `U+2028`/`U+2029`, the zero-width ones) no longer pass, but not through a case of this requirement: what `stripControl` removes of that class, and what keeps passing (`U+00AD`, `U+00A0`, …), is in REQ-TUI-020. `redactSummary` does not mask: a value without a separator (`password SuperSecret123`); names outside the list (`contraseña:`, `clave=`, `pin=`, `Cookie: session=…`, `p4ssword=`); what follows the first space of an unquoted value (`PASSWORD=Super Secret 123` → `PASSWORD=[REDACTED] Secret 123`), with an unclosed quote (`password="Zx9 Qv7`), with the space escaped or inside quotes that do not open the value (`password=Zx9\ Qv7`, `password=Zx9" "Qv7`), in ANSI-C quotes (`password=$'Zx9 Qv7'`) or after an intermediate quote (`pass='a'b'c d'e` leaves ` d'e`); `<password>…</password>`; `password%3D…`; `usuario:clave` and `https://admin:clave@host/`; a name with more than 64 characters after the credential word (`secret_` + 70 letters + `=valor` leaves the value); a soft hyphen `U+00AD` inside the name (`pass` + `U+00AD` + `word=valor` leaves the value; a `U+200B` there no longer avoids it, it is removed before searching). Tokens: those under 32 (`AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE`; the short segment of a JWT remains between two `[REDACTED]`), URL-encoded ones (`%2B` cuts the run), those that mix `/` with `_`/`-` in their first 32 characters, and the leading `-`, `+` or `/` of a run (`-KKK…` → `-[REDACTED]`). It over-redacts on purpose: `passed: 12`, `keyboard: us`, `tokens: 39.9k` and `hotkey: alt+k` lose the value, as does what follows `"$PWD":` (`-v "$PWD":/work` → `-v "$PWD":[REDACTED]`); after a quoted value everything attached up to the first space is masked, so from a one-line JSON the rest is lost (`{"password":"x","user":"bob"}` → `{"password":[REDACTED]`) and the comma is eaten (`"password": "x", "user": "bob"` → `"password": [REDACTED] "user": "bob"`); a 36-character UUID, a domain SID (`S-1-5-21-1111111111-2222222222-3333333333`), 32 underscores and every run of 32 of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` are masked, whether in a path (`/usr/share/seclists/Discovery/Web-Content/directory-list-2.3-medium.txt` → `/[REDACTED].3-medium.txt`) or the name of a file that without that rule remained whole (`CVE-2021-4034-pwnkit-local-privesc.sh` → `[REDACTED].sh`). That `compactView` redacts has no case (GAP-26).

### REQ-PNL-002 — Hostile content in a tool's input or output does not change the mode
The `tool_call` and `tool_result` handlers MUST NOT modify the effective or the requested mode, whatever the text of the input or the output.
- **WHEN** the extension loaded against a fake `pi` (headless, default mode) receives a bash `tool_call` and a `tool_result` whose text contains 'GEKKO mode: aggressive', `/gekko-mode aggressive` and an OSC escape.
- **THEN** `runtime.mode` stays with `effective='default'` and `requested='default'`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` handlers `tool_call`, `tool_result`
- Limit: a single string and only those two handlers, in headless. It does not prove that there is no other path (a registered command, `before_agent_start`) nor that the model lacks a tool to change the mode (GAP-15).


### REQ-PNL-004 — `canOfferAggressive` and `guardStatusLine` (pure functions): AGGRESSIVE is OFFERED only with the guard present and healthy
`canOfferAggressive` MUST be true only if the guard is present and healthy; `guardStatusLine` MUST show exactly 'guard ON' for a healthy guard (without claiming containment) and MUST include 'no protegida' ("not protected") for a `present=false` state.
- **WHEN** the health `{present,ok}` is evaluated in its healthy / absent / failed combinations.
- **THEN** true only for healthy; 'guard ON' without 'contenid'; absent includes 'no protegida'.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `canOfferAggressive`, `guardStatusLine`
- Limit: here it is only proven that the guard's health prevents ENTERING AGGRESSIVE; leaving AGGRESSIVE when the guard falls is in REQ-HLT-002. The `present=false` state is not produced by any live code (`newRuntime` is born `{present:true, ok:true}`; `present: false` only appears in the selftest), so 'sesión no protegida' ("unprotected session") is never shown at runtime. Since spec 008 `guardStatusLine` has no caller in live code (the retired widget used it): the guard state the operator sees (`ON` / `FALLÓ` / `AUSENTE`) is built by `buildPanelState` for the GUARD card and the bar (REQ-TUI-003, REQ-TUI-010). `canOfferAggressive` is used: in the mode selector and in `buildPanelState`.

### REQ-PNL-005 — A single launcher
`tools/pi-hitl.sh` MUST exist and load the guard at most once; `tools/gekko-toolbox.sh` MUST NOT exist.
- **WHEN** the selftest reads the launchers from the cwd.
- **THEN** pi-hitl exists and references the guard at most once; the separate launcher does not exist.
- Code: `tools/pi-hitl.sh`, `toolbox/gekko-panel-selftest.ts`
- Limit: textual contract; it does not accredit real Docker.

### REQ-PNL-006 — Strict parsing of the mode command
`parseModeCommand` MUST return a mode only for the exact tokens 'default' or 'aggressive' (ignoring whitespace and case) and MUST return null for any other input, including the empty one.
- **WHEN** `aggressive\n`, ` DEFAULT `, `rm -rf /` and `` are parsed.
- **THEN** 'aggressive', 'default', null and null.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `parseModeCommand`

### REQ-PNL-007 — Detail view: ≤80 columns, visible policy, fallen guard shown as such
`panelDetailLines` MUST limit each line to 80 columns, MUST show the scope count, the techniques board with ✓/?/✗ symbols, the last block and the turn with the activity, and MUST show 'AUSENTE/FALLÓ' and 'AGRESIVO off' when the guard is not healthy.
- **WHEN** the detail is projected with 2 hosts in scope, a current 'account' authorization in aggressive mode, a last block and turn 5; and another with the guard absent.
- **THEN** all lines ≤80; 'scope (2)', 'técnicas', 'bloqueo: brute-force', '✓ account', '✗ bruteforce', '? lateral', 'turno 5' with '▸' appear; with the guard down 'AUSENTE/FALLÓ' + 'AGRESIVO off' appear.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `panelDetailLines`
- Limit: only the pure function. Since spec 008 this view is no longer mirrored to the sidebar: at runtime it is only drawn under the editor when the operator opens the detail (`/gekko-panel`, or `/gekko-auth` without arguments). The rail is another projection of the same runtime (REQ-TUI-001, REQ-TUI-003).

### REQ-PNL-008 — Detail view: each entry takes a single line, without controls
`panelDetailLines` MUST return the same number of lines whatever the text of the last block and of the scope entries, and its lines MUST NOT contain CR, LF, TAB or any other C0/C1 character: LF and TAB are replaced by a space before removing the controls and redacting. Each line MUST also be redacted with LF and TAB deleted (the default reading of `redactSummary`, that of origin/main) and MUST NOT leave visible what either of the two readings masks: if the two outputs are equal except for spaces the first holds; if not, the initial tokens they share remain, followed by `[REDACTED]`.
- **WHEN** the detail is projected with last block `x\rmodo AGRESIVO\nguard ON\tz\n\nPASSWORD=SuperSecret123` and a scope host `a\n\tb`, and another with values without controls; and with four texts, each as last block and as scope host, that split a key or a value: `p\nassword=SuperSecret123`, `password=Zx9\nQv7Lm2 fin`, `pwd:\tZx9\tQv7Lm2` and `found ` + 16 `A` + LF + 16 `B` + ` end`.
- **THEN** the two details have the same number of lines and joining them with `\n` and splitting again gives that same number; no line contains `[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]`; `bloqueo: xmodo AGRESIVO guard ON z PASSWORD=[REDACTED]` and `scope (2): 192.0.2.10, a b` remain; in the four details with a split key or value `SuperSecret123`, `Qv7Lm2` and `BBBB` do not appear, there is one line that starts with `bloqueo: ` and the number of lines is the same.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `oneLine`, `strictest`, `panelDetailLines`
- Limit: only `panelDetailLines`, and with a hostile value only in the last block and the scope (GAP-34). When the two readings do not coincide the rest of the line is lost, including what was not secret (`password=abc\ndef fin` → `bloqueo: password=[REDACTED] [REDACTED]`; `p\nassword=clave resto` → `bloqueo: [REDACTED]`), and two runs of 16 split by an LF are masked as one of 32 (the over-redaction of REQ-PNL-001). Since spec 008 the sidebar no longer splits these lines: it draws the cards of `panel.json`, which are sanitized and redacted on publishing with a single reading, the glued one (REQ-TUI-020). `kbViewLines` does not go through `oneLine` or `redactSummary`: the `kb/` paths are interpolated as-is (a file name with `\x1b[2J` or an LF reaches the alt+k view whole). The format characters that `stripControl` removes by default (REQ-TUI-020) are also removed here, with no case of their own; `U+00AD` passes. The hostile text remains visible on its line: a block that says `guard ON` is readable, but after `bloqueo: `.

### 3.11 TUI panel: card rail and status bar (TUI)

Reconciliation of `specs/008-tui-panel/` (card rail in a tmux pane + status
bar). `REQ-TUI-0NN` is AC N of `specs/008-tui-panel/spec.md`: a case whose
message starts with `008-AC<n>:` counts for that id by its prefix, whatever it is
about. There is no REQ-TUI-015: AC15 moved to §7 of that spec as a live smoke test against the
real model, which cannot be a gate. Almost all cases call pure functions of
`toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` with doubles: the `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` process, the
tmux bindings and the wiring with a real Pi (`publish`, `setFooter`) only run in
`tools/tui-test.sh` (needs tmux, not a gate) or by hand (GAP-62 to GAP-77).

### REQ-TUI-001 — Panel contract: `buildPanelState` gives 7 or 8 cards in fixed order and `parsePanelState` rejects the 43 invalid forms tested and requires a GUARD card consistent with the bar
`buildPanelState` MUST return a state with `v=1`, `ts` equal to the received clock and the cards `guard, scope, tecnicas, shells, kb, sesion, cambios` in that order when it receives no finding counts, and the eight of `CARD_IDS` (with `hallazgos` in fifth position) when it does; without counts it MUST NOT include a `hallazgos` card or the string `0/0`. The SESIÓN card MUST carry the rows `herramientas ok`, `bloqueadas` and `con error` with the values of `runtime.counts`. `parsePanelState` MUST return `ok` with a state identical to the serialized one for the output of `buildPanelState`, and MUST return `ok:false` for each of the invalid inputs tested. `parsePanelState` MUST reject (reason `guard incoherente`, "inconsistent guard") a state whose first card does not have id `guard` or is not pinned, that carries more than one `guard` card, whose `bar.guard` is not `ON`, `FALLÓ` or `AUSENTE`, whose GUARD card has a `right` different from `bar.guard`, or that does not carry in that card exactly one row whose label, already sanitized as drawn (`stripControl`), reads as `guard` (the ASCII word in any case after a prefix without letters or digits and without a letter or digit behind), with label exactly `guard` and value equal to `bar.guard`. `panelTick` with a TUI MUST pass to `io.write` a JSON that `parsePanelState` accepts and whose bar carries the branch, the model, the ctx % and `auto` that the `io` returned.
- **WHEN** the state of a new runtime without extras is built with clock 5000; the same with counts `{hypothesis:1, confirmed:2, refuted:1}` and the rest at 0; that of a runtime with `counts={ok:3, blocked:2, error:1}`; the serialization of the 8-card state is parsed; `{nope`, `[]` and `null` are parsed; that state with `v:2` and without `v`; with `cards:[{id:1}]`; with `bar:null`; with `ts` `"5000"`, `null` or absent; with `theme.appearance:"sepia"`, `theme.noColor:"no"`, `theme.roles:{a:5}` or `theme:null`; with `cards:{}`, `cards[0].title:5` or `cards[0].pinned:"yes"`; with `rows[0].glyph:5`, `rows:[null]` or `rows[0].label:{}`; with `cards:[]`, without the first card, with the GUARD card duplicated at the end, with the cards in reverse order or with `cards[0].pinned:false`; with `bar.guard:"FALLÓ"` (the card says `ON`), with `cards[0].right:"FALLÓ"`, with the value of the `guard` row at `FALLÓ`, without the `guard` row, with that row repeated, or with `OK` instead of `ON` in the bar, the header and the row at once; with a second row `● … ON` added to the GUARD card of label `guard`+U+200B, U+200B+`guard`, `guard`+TAB, ` guard`, `Guard`, `GUARD`, `gu`+U+200B+`ard`, `gu`+`ESC[0m`+`ard` or `g`+DEL+`uard`; with the only `guard` row renamed to `guard`+U+200B, ` guard` or `Guard`; `panelTick(true, …)` is run with a fake `io` (usage `{pct:41, window:200000, model:"m1", branch:"b1"}`, compaction active, a two-line ledger); and the text of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` is read.
- **THEN** the ids come out `guard,scope,tecnicas,shells,kb,sesion,cambios`; with counts they are 8 and equal to `CARD_IDS`; the state without counts has no `hallazgos` card and its JSON does not contain `0/0`; the SESIÓN rows are `herramientas ok=3`, `bloqueadas=2`, `con error=1`; the roundtrip gives `ok` and the same JSON, with `v===1` and `ts===5000`; the 43 invalid inputs give `ok:false`; the JSON the tick writes passes `parsePanelState` with `bar.branch="b1"`, `bar.model="m1"`, `bar.ctxPct=41` and `bar.auto=true`; the guard's text contains `runtime.counts.blocked += 1` and `runtime.counts[ev.isError ? "error" : "ok"] += 1`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `buildPanelState`, `parsePanelState`, `panelTick`, `PANEL_VERSION`, `CARD_IDS`; text of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` (regex)
- Limit: only pure functions and a fake `io`. "The guard publishes" (AC1 of spec 008) has a single wiring case, which looks at nothing more than that the file exists after a `session_start` in `tui` mode (REQ-TUI-021): the content written, the tmp + rename of `writePanelFile`, the reading of `GEKKO_FINDINGS` and the publishing timer have no case. The counters case is a regex over the text of `gekko-guard.ts`: it passes just the same with the line inside a comment (re-run on a copy; same limit as GAP-36), and no case runs `deny()` or `tool_execution_end` and looks at the counter (GAP-64). "The rail keeps the last good frame and indicates it" lives in `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` (`draw`) and has no case in the gate; it is covered by `tools/tui-test.sh` (process test, not a gate) in a 12-row pane, where the frame fills the whole height: the last row (`CAMBIOS`) is drawn; with an inconsistent `panel.json` (bar `FALLÓ`, card `ON`) the last row is `! último estado válido…` and the first one is still `GUARD  ON`; with a valid state the notice disappears; and at 37 columns the notice comes out trimmed in the last row without shifting the header. All four failed before the fix of 2026-11-12: the sidebar erased its last row when the frame filled the pane (exactly the notice row) and the notice, of 41 characters, wrapped and shifted the rail under 41 columns. The kept frame is drawn with the guard of the last valid state until it expires (REQ-TUI-011): the notice is the only thing that distinguishes that interval. `parsePanelState` validates types and the guard's consistency, not the rest of the content: it accepts non-GUARD cards in another order, repeated or with an unknown id, extra keys, a GUARD card `summary` that does not match the bar, any glyph or role in the `guard` row (probe: `●`/`ok` next to `FALLÓ` validates), negative or fractional `bar.turn`, negative or future `ts`, roles that are not SGR and strings with controls (that is filtered by the drawing: REQ-TUI-020). The fixed order and the 7/8 are a property of `buildPanelState`, not something the reader requires, except GUARD's first place. The consistency does not authenticate: a state with guard `FALLÓ` rewritten with `ON` in the bar, the header and the row at once validates and is drawn `▾ GUARD  ON` / `● guard  ON` (probe; limit accepted in the amended AC20 of spec 008). "Reads as `guard`" is the same ASCII rule that quotes outside the card (REQ-TUI-020), with its same holes: a second row in the GUARD card with a homoglyph (`guаrd` with U+0430), with a letter-class filler in front (U+3164 + `guard`), with a zero-width mark that `stripControl` does not remove inserted in the word (`gu`+U+00AD+`ard`; likewise U+034F, U+FE0F and U+180E, list in REQ-TUI-020) or with the text `guard  ON` in the value validates (probes outside the gate); `guardian` too, and it does not imitate the row. No case: rejection for a loose `bar` field of the wrong type (only `bar:null`), for `id`, `right` or `summary` of a card, for `role` or `value` of a row, and for a non-finite `ts` (removing any of those checks makes no case fail). "Never `0/0` without a source" is proven with a single state; with a ledger that exists but is empty the card says `0/0 confirmados`.
- Spec 024: the counts WHEN adds `remediated: 0` (seven integers).

### REQ-TUI-002 — `renderRail`: same input, same output, and no line exceeds the width in the states tested
`renderRail` MUST return the same result (lines and hits) in two calls with the same `(state, width, height, theme, ui, clock)`, without depending on the real clock or earlier calls, and MUST NOT return a line that exceeds the requested width, measured in cells not counting SGR, at 36, 48 and 80 columns; with a label, a `right` or a title longer than the width, and with CJK, hangul or double-width emoji text, it MUST trim the line to the width; with width 0 to 3 no line MUST exceed `max(1, width)`.
- **WHEN** the test state (2 hosts, healthy guard, 1 shell, finding counts, `git {files:3, add:4, del:5}`, `ts=1000`) is drawn twice with height 60, a five-role SGR theme, empty ui and clock 1000, at 36, 48 and 80 columns; the same state with `ts=1.7e12` and clock `ts+1000` at 36, 48, 36 and 36 columns; at 36 columns with a row label of 300 `h` and a `right` of 300 `r`, and with two titles of 200 characters; at 36 and 48 with title `日本語`×30 and `right` `한글`×30 in GUARD, and in SCOPE the first row with label `😀`×60 and glyph `日` and the second with label `한글`×30; and with width 0, 1, 2 and 3.
- **THEN** at each width the two results are identical (JSON) and have more than 8 lines; `vlen(line) ≤ width` in all; with real `ts` the first, third and fourth outputs are identical, contain `ON` and do not contain `guard ?`; with double-width text no line exceeds 36 or 48 cells according to the test's own counter (2 cells for CJK, hangul and emoji `U+1F300`–`U+1F64F`), and the output contains `日`, `😀` and `한` (all three are drawn); with width 0–3, `vlen(line) ≤ max(1, width)`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderRail`, `fit`, `cw`, `cells`, `vlen`
- Limit: "pure" is proven as equality of two consecutive calls with one state, one theme and one ui; no case checks that it does not mutate its arguments or do I/O (by hand, it does not mutate the state). The width is measured with the module's own `vlen`, except in the double-width case: an error in `cw` is shared by the renderer and the oracle. In that case the hangul `right` does not get drawn (the CJK title fills the row): the hangul width is set by the label of the second SCOPE row. `cw` counts 1 cell for wide emoji and symbols outside its ranges — `🚀` (`U+1F680`), `⚡` (`U+26A1`), `✅` (`U+2705`) —: a row of 60 `🚀` at 36 columns measures 36 by `vlen` and carries 31 `🚀`, about 67 cells in a terminal that draws them double width (GAP-74). The glyphs `▾ ▸ … │ ●` are assumed to be 1 cell (risk declared in spec 008 §6). At 48 and 80 columns the test state needs no trimming (its longest line measures 41 cells): those two cases only fail if the renderer ADDS width; removing or loosening the row or header trimming never makes them fail (the 36-column one, those of long text and title, the double-width one and the width 0–3 one do fail). With width 0 or negative the lines measure 1 cell, more than requested; with width `NaN` or `Infinity` nothing is trimmed (a 300-character label comes out whole): today it is not reachable from the sidebar (`process.stdout.columns || 36`) and has no case. That the real tmux pane does not overflow is checked by nothing: `tools/tui-test.sh` does not measure widths. The height is not part of this requirement (REQ-TUI-003, REQ-TUI-012).

### REQ-TUI-003 — `renderRail`: header `▾ NAME  count` / `▸ NAME  summary`, CAMBIOS collapsed, auto-collapse from the last and GUARD pinned
`renderRail` MUST draw for each card a header row: `▾ TITLE  right` followed by its rows when open, and only `▸ TITLE  summary` when collapsed; MUST treat CAMBIOS as collapsed while `ui.open` does not say otherwise; when the rows do not fit in the height MUST collapse unpinned cards starting from the last; and MUST NOT collapse the pinned card (GUARD) for lack of height, nor through `ui.open.guard=false` when the published guard is `AUSENTE` or `FALLÓ`. `buildPanelState` MUST give TÉCNICAS the count `N auto · M preguntan · K bloq` and as summary the permitted techniques, and the rail MUST NOT hide rows of that card behind a `+N más` in the state tested.
- **WHEN** the test state of REQ-TUI-002 is drawn at 48 columns: with height 60 and empty ui; with `open.scope=false`; with height equal to the total of lines minus 3 (25); with height 9; with all cards collapsed by `z`; a runtime with guard `present=false` and another with guard `ok=false`, both with `open.guard=false` and height 40; and a runtime in aggressive mode with a `*` authorization of `binary-upload`, expanded and with `open.tecnicas=false`.
- **THEN** line 0 starts with `▾ GUARD  ON`, there is one that starts with `▾ SCOPE  2` and another with `▸ CAMBIOS  3 arch`, and none contains `añadidas`; with SCOPE collapsed there is `▸ SCOPE  2 hosts`, the line `  · 10.0.0.1` does not exist and the headers of GUARD, SHELLS, KB and SESIÓN are the same as uncollapsed; with height 25, ≤25 lines come out with `▾ GUARD`, `▾ SCOPE` and `▸ SESIÓN`; with height 9, ≤9 lines come out, the first `▾ GUARD` and one of the first five contains `modo`; with an absent guard line 0 starts with `▾ GUARD  AUSENTE` and there is a `modo` row, and with a failed guard there is a `modo` row; expanded and collapsed there are 8 hits and each header matches `^[▾▸] \S+  \S`; TÉCNICAS expanded starts with `▾ TÉCNICAS  1 auto · 7 preguntan · 1 bloq` with no line with `más`, and collapsed it is exactly `▸ TÉCNICAS  ✓ binary-upload`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderRail` (`wantOpen`, auto-collapse loop), `buildPanelState`, `techniqueCard`
- Limit: one state per clause. "Collapsing one does not alter the others" compares only the headers of GUARD, SHELLS, KB and SESIÓN, not the bodies or TÉCNICAS, HALLAZGOS or CAMBIOS (by hand, with that state, the rest of the rail stays identical). "GUARD never yields" means it is not auto-collapsed; its rows are cut by height: with height 3 the header, `guard` and `modo` remain, and `último bloqueo` is not seen (with guard `FALLÓ` and height 4 neither). If the height is not enough even with everything collapsed, the last cards disappear without a mark: with height 9 and 8 cards GUARD (5 rows) and the headers of SCOPE, TÉCNICAS, SHELLS and HALLAZGOS are drawn; KB, SESIÓN and CAMBIOS neither appear nor have a hit. The auto-collapse does not distinguish a card opened by hand: `open.cambios=true` yields just the same. What pins GUARD is `bar.guard !== "ON"`, not the card's content: a foreign state with `bar.guard="ON"` and the card saying `FALLÓ` is collapsed, and any other value (`on`) pins it. `AUSENTE` only comes from a runtime with `present=false`, which no live code produces (REQ-PNL-004). With guard `ON`, GUARD can be collapsed (REQ-TUI-007). "Reopening one collapses the next" (`test-plan.md`) has no case. No check of `tools/tui-test.sh` is of AC3.

### REQ-TUI-004 — `renderRail`: the zone map has one `toggle` entry per drawn header
`renderRail` MUST return in `hits` exactly one entry per drawn header row, with `row` equal to the index (from 0) of that line, `card` equal to the id of that row's card and `action` `toggle`; MUST NOT return a hit for a body row or for a row that fell outside the returned lines.
- **WHEN** the test state of REQ-TUI-002 (8 cards) is drawn at 48 columns with height 60 and with height 5.
- **THEN** with height 60 the hits' rows are exactly the indices of the lines that start with `▾ ` or `▸ `, all with `action === "toggle"`; each hit's line contains the title of the card with that id; there is no hit in row 1; with height 5 all hits have `row < 5`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderRail`, type `Hit`
- Limit: "a click outside a header changes nothing" (AC4 of spec 008) has no case under this requirement: it is exercised by `toggleAt` in REQ-TUI-006 and REQ-TUI-007 (pure function) and a real click in `tools/tui-test.sh` (process test with tmux, not a gate). One state and one width. "The right card" is checked as "the line contains the title of the card with that id": with two cards of the same id (which `parsePanelState` accepts) it does not distinguish. The hit carries no column range: the whole header row toggles, whatever the x. In the case 'no hit points outside the drawn lines' the first half compares the list with itself filtered and cannot fail (GAP-63); the second can.

### REQ-TUI-005 — `parseMouse`: left-button SGR-1006 events, partial tail and discarding of the garbage forms tested
`parseMouse` MUST recognize `ESC [ < b ; x ; y` terminated in `M` (press) or `m` (release) with `b=0` and return an event with those coordinates; MUST return in `rest` a truncated prefix at the end of the buffer, so that prepending it to the next read reassembles the event; MUST NOT emit an event for `b=2` (right button) or `b=64` (wheel), nor for the garbage sequences tested; MUST NOT throw; and MUST return an empty `rest` for the unclosed 83-character prefix tested.
- **WHEN** `\x1b[<0;20;5M\x1b[<0;20;5m` is parsed; `\x1b[<0;20;5M\x1b[<0;2` and then its `rest` + `1;6m`; the six strings `\x1b[<x`, `\x1b[<0;1;`, `abc\x1b`, `\x1b[<0;1;2Z`, `\x1b[<0;99999999;1M` and `\x1b[<\x1b[<0;3;4M`; `\x1b[<` followed by `1;` 40 times; `\x1b[<2;1;1M\x1b[<64;1;1M\x1b[<0;7;8M`; and `\x1b[<0;1;2M\x1b[<0;3;4M\x1b[<0;5`.
- **THEN** two events, press with `x=20`, `y=5` and release, with empty `rest`; one event and `rest === "\x1b[<0;2"`, and on completing it a release event with `x=21`; none of the six throws, every event that comes out has `x>0` and `rest` measures under 24; `\x1b[<0;1;` and `\x1b[<0;99999999;1M` give zero events and `\x1b[<x` gives empty `rest`; the 83-character prefix gives empty `rest`; of the three buttons one event comes out; and from the last one two events come out with `rest === "\x1b[<0;5"`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `parseMouse`, `MOUSE_RE`
- Limit: pure function. That the pane receives SGR-1006 through tmux and that `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` (`onData`) chains `rest` between reads has no case in the gate, and `tools/tui-test.sh` (process test, not a gate) carries no check of AC5 (GAP-65). The 24-character cut to discard a prefix has no boundary case: only that of 83 and prefixes under 9. "Discards truncated sequences" is not literal: a plausible prefix of under 24 characters at the end of the buffer is KEPT in `rest` (`\x1b[<0;1;` → `rest` `\x1b[<0;1;`; also `\x1b` and `\x1b[` alone, and `abc\x1b` → `rest` `\x1b`); the case only asserts zero events. It accepts coordinates 0 (`\x1b[<0;0;0M` → event `x=0`, `y=0`, which in 1-based coordinates does not exist) and up to 99999. Only exact `b=0`: a click with a modifier (Shift `b=4`, Meta `b=8`, Ctrl `b=16`), the middle button and dragging (`b=32`) are ignored, with no case. Other mouse protocols (X10 `ESC [ M`) are not recognized. Everything that is not a mouse event is omitted from the output, keys included: whoever recovers them is `onData`, with no case.

### REQ-TUI-006 — `sidebarBindings` generates the three conditional bindings that wrap the previous command; `toggleAt` toggles only the card of the header
`sidebarBindings(prev)` MUST return three tmux argv, one for each of `MouseDown1Pane`, `DoubleClick1Pane` and `TripleClick1Pane` in that order, each `bind-key -T root <key> if-shell -F -t = #{@gekko_sidebar}` + `send-keys -M`, followed as the else branch by the previous command of that key unmodified; for a key without a previous command it MUST NOT add an else branch. `toggleAt` with the (1-based) row of the SCOPE header MUST return a ui whose `open` only contains `scope=false`, and with the next row (body) MUST return the same ui.
- **WHEN** `sidebarBindings({MouseDown1Pane: "select-pane -t = ; send-keys -M", DoubleClick1Pane: "copy-mode -H ; send-keys -M", TripleClick1Pane: undefined})` is called; and `toggleAt` over the state of a new runtime drawn at 48×40, with the row of the SCOPE header and with the next one.
- **THEN** 3 argv come out with those three keys in element 3; each starts with `bind-key -T root`, its elements 4 to 8 are `if-shell -F -t = #{@gekko_sidebar}` and it contains the element `send-keys -M`; the last element of the first two is the identical previous command, and that of the third is `send-keys -M`; the click on the header leaves `open` with the single key `scope` at `false`, and the click on the body row returns the same ui object.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `sidebarBindings`, `SIDEBAR_KEYS`, `toggleAt`
- Limit: nothing that AC6 of spec 008 asserts is executed in the gate: no case runs tmux. The real click that collapses the card, the focus that does not leave Pi's pane (also with 10 rapid clicks), the click and the double click outside the sidebar, the negative control without bindings and the identical `list-keys -T root` after `--restore` are only covered by `tools/tui-test.sh` (process test with nested tmux, not a gate; GAP-66); the triple click outside the sidebar is not checked even by that test, and the double click only as "the focus goes to the clicked pane", not as "same as before". The gate pins the argv, not its effect: that `if-shell -F -t =` with that format leaves the focus where it was is a tmux measurement recorded in `test-plan.md`, not a case. The capture of the previous binding (`tmux list-keys` and the `sed` that turns `\;` into `;`), its saving in `@gekko_prev_<key>`, the "only saved the first time" and the restoration live in `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` (`install_bindings`, `--restore`) and `tools/pi-hitl.sh`: no case in the gate. `sidebarBindings` alone wraps whatever it receives: if `prev` is already Gekko's binding it nests it, and an empty `prev` (`""`) counts as a key without a binding. The previous command travels as ONE argument that tmux parses again: a real operator binding with quotes, braces or `\;` has no case. The reducer part repeats REQ-TUI-007 with a minimal state.

### REQ-TUI-007 — `toggleAt` and `handleKey` (pure reducers): click on header, cursor, toggle, all and keys `1`–`8` by fixed id
`toggleAt(state, ui, hits, y)` with `y` equal to the (1-based) row of a hit MUST return a ui with that card toggled, and with a `y` without a hit MUST return the same ui object. `handleKey` MUST: move `cursor` one card with `j`/`down` (forward) and `k`/`up` (back), saturating at the ends; toggle the cursor's card with `enter` and with `space`; toggle with a digit `1`–`8` the card whose id is `CARD_IDS[n-1]`, and return the same ui if that card is not in the state; set `open=false` on every unpinned card with `z` and `open=true` on all with `a`; and return the same ui with any other key. When `bar.guard` is not `ON`, neither `z` nor a click on its header MUST fold the pinned card (GUARD).
- **WHEN** over the test state of REQ-TUI-002 (8 cards, guard `ON`) drawn at 48×60 and empty ui: click on the SCOPE header row and another equal one over the result; click on the next row, on `y=0` and on `y=9999`; `j`, then `k`, and `k` from the empty ui; `up` after `j` and from the empty ui; `down` eight times and once more; `enter` with the cursor on SCOPE and then `space`; the keys `2`, `1`, `8`, `0`, `x` and `9`, and each of `3` to `7`; the key `6` over the same state without the `kb` card; `z` and then `a`; and, with `bar.guard="OFF"`, `z` and a click on row 1.
- **THEN** the first click leaves `open.scope=false` and the second `true`; the three clicks without a hit return the same ui object; `j` leaves the cursor on `scope`, `k` returns it to `guard` and `k` from empty leaves it on `guard`; `up` gives `guard` in both cases; after eight `down` the cursor is on `cambios` and the ninth does not move it; `enter` closes SCOPE and `space` reopens it; `2` closes SCOPE, `1` closes GUARD, `8` opens CAMBIOS; each digit from `3` to `7` toggles the card `CARD_IDS[n-1]` and leaves `open` with that single key; `0`, `x`, `9` and the `6` without a `kb` card return the same ui object; after `z` every unpinned card is closed and after `a` all open, CAMBIOS included; with guard `OFF`, GUARD stays open after `z` and after the click.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `toggleAt`, `handleKey`, `flip`, `isOpen`
- Limit: pure functions. "With the rail focused" (AC7 of spec 008) has no case in the gate: the raw stdin reading, the table that translates `\x1b[A`, `\x1b[B`, `\r` and space to `up`, `down`, `enter` and `space`, and the saving in `panel.ui.json` are in `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` (`onData`, `KEYS`, `persistUi`); `tools/tui-test.sh` sends `2`, `z`, `a` and `j Enter` to the real pane (process test, not a gate; GAP-65), and checks that Shift+Up (`ESC[1;2A`) and F6 (`ESC[17~`) do not fold SCOPE or SESIÓN: `onData` delivers a whole CSI sequence as a single key, which `handleKey` ignores if it is not in `KEYS` (before the fix of 2026-11-12 its digits were read as the keys `2` and `7`). No case: a sequence split between two stdin reads (its bytes would be read loose; read, not executed) and SS3s (`ESC O A`). The absent card only has a case with `6`. The cursor is not drawn: `renderRail` does not read `ui.cursor` (the output is identical with any cursor), so the operator does not see which card `enter` will toggle; without a previous cursor `enter` toggles the first, and with guard `ON` that folds GUARD. With guard `ON`, GUARD folds with `1`, `enter`, click and `z`: the `z` case exempts the pinned card from the check, the `1` one does assert it. What prevents folding it is `bar.guard !== "ON"`, proven here with the value `OFF`, which the guard does not publish (`AUSENTE` and `FALLÓ` in the drawing: REQ-TUI-003). A saved `open.guard=false` with guard `ON` is not erased by `a` or `z` while the guard is down: GUARD ends up folded again when the guard returns to `ON`. A state without cards returns the same ui, with no case. That the reducers do not touch mode, scope or policy: REQ-TUI-014.

### REQ-TUI-008 — `resolveTheme` with `noColor` returns an empty theme and `renderRail` comes out without color SGR; TÉCNICAS and HALLAZGOS are distinguished by glyph and word
With `state.theme.noColor === true`, `resolveTheme` MUST return a theme without roles (`{}`) even if the state carries a valid role, and `renderRail` drawn with that theme MUST NOT emit any sequence that starts with `\x1b[3`, `\x1b[4`, `\x1b[9` or `\x1b[38`, nor any `\x1b` outside the SGRs `0`, `1`, `2`, `4`, `22` and `24`. Without color, each row of the TÉCNICAS body MUST start with one of the glyphs `✓`/`?`/`✗` followed by a word, and each HALLAZGOS row MUST carry glyph, label and number.
- **WHEN** the fixture (2 hosts, healthy guard, 1 shell, ledger with 2 confirmed, 1 hypothesis and 1 refuted) with `theme={appearance:"dark", noColor:true, roles:{success:"\x1b[32m"}}` is resolved with `resolveTheme` and drawn at 48×40; `resolveTheme` with `noColor:true` and empty roles; and, with `noColor:true` at 48×80, a state in aggressive mode with a current `binary-upload` authorization and the fixture with ledger.
- **THEN** `resolveTheme` returns an object without keys; the joined output contains no `\x1b[3`, `\x1b[4`, `\x1b[9` or `\x1b[38`, and with the SGRs `0|1|2|4|22|24` removed no `\x1b` remains; the text contains `✓`, `ON` and at least one of `?`, `✗`, `●`, `·`; the TÉCNICAS body has ≥ 3 rows, all of the form `  [✓?✗] <lowercase or digit>…`, with the three glyphs present (`✓ binary-upload`, `✗ bruteforce`, `? 7 preguntan`); that of HALLAZGOS has 6 rows `  [✓?✗] <label>  <number>` with the three glyphs present.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `resolveTheme`, `renderRail`, `techniqueCard`, `buildPanelState`
- Limit: only the rail and only the pure function. The bar without SGR has its case under REQ-TUI-010 (`renderBar` never emits SGR, with or without `noColor`). The translation of the `NO_COLOR` of Pi's environment to `noColor` (`publish` in `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`: `!!process.env.NO_COLOR`) has no case (GAP-72); `buildPanelState` only accepts `noColor === true` (proven by hand: `"1"` → false). The capture of the real pane is made only in `tools/tui-test.sh` (which the gate does not run), and with `noColor: true` written by hand in a synthetic `panel.json` (`gen_state 1 ""`), not with `NO_COLOR` in a Pi's environment: the environment → `publish` → rail path is not exercised by any test. "Every state remains distinguishable" is not proven: only TÉCNICAS and HALLAZGOS have a per-row assertion; GUARD, SCOPE, SHELLS, KB, SESIÓN and CAMBIOS do not. The general case only asks that `✓`, `ON` and some glyph from a list appear in the whole rail: changing the glyph of the `● guard` row or that of `✓ herramientas ok` does not make it fail (mutations tested); it fails only if the word `ON` disappears. In HALLAZGOS four states share `?` (hypothesis, triaged, to validate, others) and in SESIÓN two share `✗` (blocked, with error): only the word separates them. Expired `guard ?` without color: no case. It is "without color SGR", not "without SGR": the headers still come out with `\x1b[1m` … `\x1b[0m` (real output).

### REQ-TUI-009 — `resolveTheme` maps Pi's roles to the rail's and, without roles, uses the light/dark fallback palette with contrast ≥ 4.5:1
`resolveTheme` MUST take each rail role from the published `state.theme.roles`: `accent`, `dim` and `border` by their name, and `ok`, `warn`, `danger`, `dim` by Pi's aliases `success`, `warning`, `error`, `muted`. Without published roles it MUST return, for the state's `appearance` (`dark` or `light`), the `FALLBACK_HEX` color of each role as `\x1b[38;2;R;G;Bm`; the text roles `dim`, `accent`, `ok`, `warn` and `danger` of both palettes MUST have WCAG contrast ≥ 4.5:1 against the three reference backgrounds of their appearance. `renderRail` MUST emit the SGR of the theme it receives in that call, without remembering that of an earlier call.
- **WHEN** `resolveTheme` with empty roles in `dark` and in `light` (10 cases: 5 roles × 2 appearances, contrast computed in the test against `#0D1117`, `#1E1E1E`, `#000000` and against `#FFFFFF`, `#F6F8FA`, `#FDF6E3`; and `border` in both appearances, without measuring contrast); with `{success:"\x1b[38;2;1;2;3m", warning:"\x1b[33m", error:"\x1b[38;5;196m", muted:"\x1b[90m"}`; with `{accent:"\x1b[38;2;7;7;7m", dim:"\x1b[38;2;8;8;8m", border:"\x1b[38;2;9;9;9m"}`; and two states that differ only in `success` (`38;2;1;2;3` and `38;2;9;9;9`), each resolved and drawn at 48×40.
- **THEN** each fallback role is the `38;2` of the `FALLBACK_HEX` hex and its contrast is ≥ 4.5 against the three backgrounds; `border` is the `38;2` of its hex in `dark` and in `light`; `ok`, `warn`, `danger` and `dim` are exactly the four SGRs given; `accent`, `dim` and `border` are the three given; the first rail contains `38;2;1;2;3m` and not `38;2;9;9;9m`, and the second the opposite; the `accent` of `light` is different from that of `dark`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `resolveTheme`, `FALLBACK_HEX`, `PI_ALIAS`, `renderRail`
- Limit: "without restarting" only means here that two pure calls with different roles give different SGR; that the `gekko-sidebar.mjs` process rereads `panel.json` and changes live is checked only by `tools/tui-test.sh` (outside the gate). "The roles of Pi's active theme": the real reading (`publish` in `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`: `ctx.ui.theme.getFgAnsi` of 7 tokens, and `appearance` inferred from the theme name containing `light`) has no case (GAP-72) — the selftest's fake `pi` returns `""`; a light theme whose name does not say `light` receives the dark palette in the roles that are missing. The contrast is measured only in the fallback palette: a valid Pi role is used as-is. `border` is exempt from the contrast (proven by hand: 2.01 in dark, 2.82 in light) and in addition `renderRail` draws nothing with it (a custom `border` does not appear in the output). The equality cases (also that of `border`) compare `resolveTheme` against the same `FALLBACK_HEX` table: they do not pin the values (changing a hex only fails if it drops below 4.5:1). That an invalid role falls to the fallback runs under REQ-TUI-020. No case: the precedence when both the own name and the alias arrive (proven: the own one wins, `ok` over `success`; if the own one is invalid the alias is used); `ROLE_RE` accepts `38;2;999;999;999` and `39` and rejects a combined SGR (`\x1b[1;31m`) or one with `:`, so a theme that publishes those forms falls to the fallback without notice.

### REQ-TUI-010 — `renderBar` without rail: a row without SGR with mode, guard, scope, shells and block mark, and the guard segment does not drop; `readCompactionEnabled` reads `compaction.enabled`
`renderBar(state, width, railVisible=false)` MUST return a single row without `\x1b`, of at most `width` cells (2 per CJK or emoji character), which at 80 columns contains the mode, `guard <state>`, `scope N`, `shells N`, `✗ bloqueo` when there is a last block (and MUST NOT carry that mark when there is none), and `turno N`. The guard segment MUST NOT drop: with branch and model longer than the row, `guard ON` and the mode remain at 80 columns; at 12 columns `guard` remains; with guard AUSENTE or FALLÓ the segment also appears with `railVisible=true`. With a 60-character model at 100 columns the row MUST contain `…` (a trimmed segment instead of a dropped one) and `guard ON`. `readCompactionEnabled` MUST return the boolean `compaction.enabled` of `.pi/settings.json` and, if that file does not exist, that of `<agentDir>/settings.json`; with no file or with unreadable JSON it MUST return true.
- **WHEN** a state with turn 7, 2 hosts, last block `brute-force sin autorización`, branch `main`, model `deepseek-v4.1-flash` and ctx 42 %/200000 goes through `renderBar(…, 80, false)` and at 12 columns; the same over a new runtime (no last block) at 80; the same with branch `feature/` + 80 `x` and a model of 80 `m` at 80; with a model of 60 `m` at 100; with guard `present=false` at 80 with `railVisible` false and true; with guard `ok=false` (without branch or model) at 5, 10, 12 and 40 columns with and without rail; with a branch of 30 `日` or 30 `😀`, or a model of 30 `한`, at 80 with and without rail. `readCompactionEnabled` with a fake reader: `.pi/settings.json` at false and the agent dir's at true; without `.pi/settings.json`; without any file; with `{no json`; and with the agent dir's at false.
- **THEN** the row has no `\n` or `\x1b`, measures ≤ 80 and contains `DEFAULT`, `guard ON`, `scope 2`, `shells 0`, `✗ bloqueo` and `turno 7`; without a last block it does not contain `bloqueo` and does contain `guard ON`; with long branch and model it measures ≤ 80 and `guard ON` and `DEFAULT` remain; with the 60 model at 100 `…` and `guard ON` appear; `guard AUSENTE` comes out with both values of `railVisible`; at 12 columns it measures ≤ 12 and contains `guard`; with FALLÓ it measures ≤ width at the four widths, with `guard` at ≥ 10 and `gua` at 5; the three CJK/emoji rows measure ≤ 80 cells with the test's own counter; `readCompactionEnabled` gives false, true, true, true and false.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderBar`, `readCompactionEnabled`
- Limit: "when the rail is not drawn" has no case: `railVisible` is a parameter. At runtime it is decided by `sidebarLive` (`toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`: mtime of `panel.json.alive` under 1500 ms) and the 140-column cut lives in `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh`; the 139/140 boundary and the 80×24 case are exercised only by `tools/tui-test.sh` (outside the gate), and only as presence or absence of the rail's pane: none of its four AC10 checks looks at the bar. Outside tmux, `GEKKO_SIDEBAR=0` and what the bar shows when the rail is absent have no process test anywhere (GAP-67). Any process that touches that `.alive` puts the bar at `railVisible=true`, and with guard ON it stops showing `guard ON`, scope, shells and the block mark (no case, GAP-67; a guard other than ON remains visible). The mounting with `ctx.ui.setFooter`, and the fallback widget at 80 columns if it does not mount, have no case (the fake `pi` carries `setFooter() {}`). "Never disappears" holds for the segment, not for the state: at 12 columns `guard AUSENTE` becomes `guard AUSEN…`, at 10 `guard FALLÓ` becomes `guard FAL…`, and at 5 the three states give `guar…` (proven by hand; the case at 5 only asks for `gua`). The five fields are asserted only at 80 columns with short values: with that state the mark `✗ bloqueo` drops entirely below 51 columns (at 50 there remains `DEFAULT · turno 7 · guard ON · scope 2 · shells 0`: `turno`, of lower priority, fits and the mark does not), at 30 there remains `DEFAULT · guard ON · scope 2` and at 20 `DEFAULT · guard ON`. At 80 the fixture's branch and model no longer fit (real output: `DEFAULT · ctx 42%/200k · turno 7 · guard ON · scope 2 · shells 0 · ✗ bloqueo`); with long branch and model scope, shells and block remain (proven), without assertion. The threshold of 12 free cells to trim a segment has no boundary case. `shells N` counts only the `open` ones (proven: a shell in `error` → `shells 0`); the block mark does not say the reason; the destructive flag is not in the bar. The three `readCompactionEnabled` cases carry the prefix `008-AC10:` although they feed the `(auto)` mark of REQ-TUI-013; no case: that `publish` calls it with `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` or, if undefined, with `~/.pi/agent` (`homedir()`), and that those paths and that key are the ones Pi uses. Read in Pi 1.5.0's code (`settings-manager.js`), not executed: Pi applies the project file over the global one only if the project is trusted; `readCompactionEnabled` does not know that trust, so with an untrusted project that sets `compaction.enabled` the `(auto)` mark may not match the effective value. This repo's `.pi/settings.json` no longer sets `enabled`: the global file decides, which is where Pi's `/settings` writes (seen once live by the operator, no automatic test). Proven by hand: a non-boolean `enabled` (`"false"`) is ignored and the next source decides.

### REQ-TUI-011 — `renderRail` with expired state (older than the window, 10 s by default; future `ts` or invalid clock) draws `guard ?` in role `danger`; `panelTick` incorporates ledger and git; `parseShortstat`
`renderRail(state, …, now, staleMs = 10 000)` MUST treat the state as current when `0 ≤ now − state.ts ≤ staleMs` and as expired in any other case (older than the window, `ts` later than `now` whatever the window, `now` or `staleMs` NaN, `now` absent). `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` MUST call `renderRail` with `Date.now()` and its window, without rewriting the `ts`. Expired, the header of the GUARD card MUST be `guard ?` in the `danger` role, whether expanded or collapsed, and the `guard` row MUST carry the glyph `?` in `danger` and the value `?` (uncolored) instead of the last value. In addition, through cases that carry the same prefix: `panelTick` with a TUI MUST publish a state with the HALLAZGOS card when the ledger returns text and with the count from `io.git()` in the CAMBIOS card; and `parseShortstat` MUST convert an output of `git diff --shortstat` into `{files, add, del}`, the empty output into zeros and a text it does not recognize into null.
- **WHEN** the fixture published with `ts=1000` (guard ON, mode DEFAULT) is drawn at 48×60 with a test theme (`danger="\x1b[31m"`) at `now=11 000` and at `now=11 001`; with `ui.open.guard=false` at `now=11 001` and at `now=1000`; with `ts=1e308` and `ts=99999999999999999` at `now=1.7e12`; at `now=−4000`; and at `now=NaN` and `now=undefined`; with `ts=1000` and window 1500 at `now=4000` and `now=2500`; with window 600 000, `ts=1000` at `now=61 000` and `ts=3 601 000` at `now=1000`; with window NaN; and the text of `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` is read. `panelTick(true, io, new runtime, 5)` with a fake `io` whose ledger has one `confirmed` line and one `refuted` and whose `git()` gives `{files:2, add:3, del:1}`. `parseShortstat` with ` 3 files changed, 10 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)`, ` 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+)`, the empty string and `basura` ("garbage").
- **THEN** at exactly 10 000 ms the first line contains `ON` and not `guard ?`; at 10 001 ms it is exactly `▾ GUARD  guard ?`, no line contains both `guard` and the word `ON`, line 0 contains `\x1b[31mguard ?`, some line `\x1b[31m?` and line 1 is exactly `  \x1b[31m?\x1b[0m guard  ?`; collapsed and expired gives `▸ GUARD  guard ?`, collapsed and current `▸ GUARD  guard ON · DEFAULT`; the two future `ts`, the `now` earlier than the `ts`, NaN and absent give `▾ GUARD  guard ?`; with window 1500, 3 s of age gives `▾ GUARD  guard ?` and 1.5 s `▾ GUARD  ON`; with window 600 000, 60 s gives `▾ GUARD  ON` and the `ts` one hour ahead `▾ GUARD  guard ?`; the NaN window gives `▾ GUARD  guard ?`; the sidebar's text contains the call `renderRail(good, cols, …, ui, Date.now(), STALE)` and does not contain `eff`. The JSON the tick writes passes `parsePanelState`, has a `hallazgos` card and the `cambios` card with `right === "2 arch"`. `parseShortstat` gives `{files:3,add:10,del:2}`, `{files:1,add:1,del:0}`, `{files:0,add:0,del:0}` and null.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderRail`, `STALE_MS`, `panelTick`, `parseShortstat`
- Limit: the pure function with injected clock and window, plus a regex over the text of `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` (passes just the same with the call inside a comment; same limit as GAP-36). The real process is only exercised by `tools/tui-test.sh` (real tmux, not a gate): `panel.json` with `ts = now + 1 h` and `GEKKO_STALE_MS=600000` → `▾ GUARD  guard ?`, and a fresh state returns to `ON`; that check fails against the previous sidebar (re-run). The expiry at 10 s in the process has no check. `GEKKO_STALE_MS` in the sidebar's environment can still lengthen the window (a large value turns off the detection of a guard that stopped publishing; now it also shortens it, and a non-numeric value or `0` falls to 10 s; a negative one leaves everything expired — read, not executed). It detects a guard that stopped publishing, not one that was impersonated (risk declared in spec 008 §6): whoever rewrites `panel.json` with a fresh `ts` keeps whatever value they want. Only the guard's value is replaced: expired, the rail keeps showing without a mark the last mode, `destructivo`, scope, techniques, shells and session (proven with a 1 h state: `▸ modo  AGRESIVO`, `! destructivo  ON`, hosts and shells as if they were current); with guard FALLÓ expired `guard ?` also remains. Proven only at 48 columns: at 15 the header becomes `guard…`, at 10 `…` and at ≤ 9 only `▾ GUARD` (the row `? guar…` follows). Pi's bar has no notion of expiry. The `panelTick` and `parseShortstat` cases are not about expiry: they count here by their `008-AC11:` prefix. No case: that `publish` uses `parseShortstat` with real git (every 5 s, keeps the last value on error).

### REQ-TUI-012 — `renderRail` with empty, 1000-item and error states: per-card row cap with `… +N más`, height and width respected; the long last block is split into `│` rows
`renderRail` MUST draw from a card with 1000 rows only its cap (3 in `kb`; 10 in `tecnicas`; 8 in `guard`, `hallazgos` and `sesion`) followed by a row `… +N más` with the rest; with the eight tickable techniques auto-approved it MUST draw the eight and the row `? log-cleanup (obligatorio)`, without a `… +N más` row; MUST draw the three rows of the `cambios` card (`archivos`, `añadidas`, `borradas`) without a `… +N más` row; with 1000 hosts and 1000 shells it MUST draw a row `+994 más` and the total `1000` in the SCOPE header, reading from the row arrays at most 60 elements; MUST NOT return more lines than `height` or lines wider than `width`; and MUST keep drawing the rows of a card whose id matches a property inherited from `Object.prototype`. In the GUARD card, a last block that does not fit in a row MUST be split by words into `  │ …` rows without the `GEKKO:` prefix, and a short one MUST stay in the row `· último bloqueo  <text>`. When the rail does not fit in `height` even with all the other cards folded, that block MUST go back to a single row (no `  │ ` row), so that the headers of the other cards are drawn. With the empty state it MUST draw the `fail-closed` row of SCOPE, and the TÉCNICAS body of a new runtime MUST carry one row per technique with its name (`✗ bruteforce`, the seven tickable ones that ask and `? log-cleanup (obligatorio)`), without a summary row `? N preguntan` or `… +N más`.
- **WHEN** a runtime with 1000 hosts and 1000 shells is published and drawn at 48×40, with each card's rows behind a `Proxy` that counts reads by index; the fixture with 1000 synthetic rows in each of `guard`, `tecnicas`, `hallazgos`, `kb` and `sesion` at 48×400; a runtime in AGGRESSIVE with a current `*` authorization for the eight tickable techniques at 48×60; the same fixture without synthetic rows (`git {files:3, add:4, del:5}`) with `open.cambios=true` at 48×400; a new runtime (empty scope, no shells) at 48×40 and at 48×60; a runtime with guard `ok=false` and a shell `x` in state `error` at 36×40; the fixture with the ids of three cards changed to `constructor`, `toString` and `__proto__`; 20 000 cards at 48×40; the last block `GEKKO: 10.0.0.9 fuera de scope — no pivot sin re-autorización (scope.md). Si está autorizado, el OPERADOR lo agrega` at 40×60, at 40×3 and at 40×12 with an empty theme, and the last block `corto` ("short") at 40×60.
- **THEN** ≤ 40 lines of ≤ 48 cells come out, with `▾ SCOPE  1000` and a row `+994 más`, in ≤ 60 row reads; each synthetic card draws its cap + 1 rows and the last says `+992 más` (`+997 más` in `kb`, `+990 más` in `tecnicas`); with the eight ticked the TÉCNICAS body is eight `✓` rows and the row `? log-cleanup (obligatorio)`, none with `más`; the CAMBIOS body is exactly `· archivos  3`, `+ añadidas  4`, `- borradas  5`; empty: a `▾ SCOPE` header without `undefined`, a line with `fail-closed`, ≤ 40 lines, and in TÉCNICAS (at 48×60) exactly nine rows: `✗ bruteforce`, one `? <key>` for each of the other seven tickable ones in the order of `POLICY_TECHNIQUES`, and `? log-cleanup (obligatorio)`; error: first line `▾ GUARD  FALLÓ`, a line with `x  error` and all of ≤ 36 characters; inherited ids: exactly one row `  · 10.0.0.1` and a line with `rev`; the 20 000 cards are drawn in under 500 ms; long block: between 2 and 4 `  │ ` rows, no line over 40, joined they contain `fuera de scope — no pivot sin re-autorización` and not `GEKKO:`; at height 3 ≤ 3 lines come out; at height 12 there is no `  │ ` row and the title of each card of the state appears in some line; short block: the line `  · último bloqueo  corto`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderRail`, `ROW_CAP`, `wrapCells`, `techniqueCard`, `buildPanelState`
- Limit: "what is finished folds to a summary row" no longer has any case: the only fold was that of techniques without data (D7, `? N preguntan`) and the operator reverted it on 2026-11-15 (each technique draws its row with a name; the count `N preguntan` stays only in the header); closed shells take one row each up to the cap (proven: 500 `open` + 500 `closed` → six alternating rows and `… +994 más`). "Real total in the header" holds only for SCOPE: that of SHELLS counts the `open` ones (`▾ SHELLS  500` with 1000 registered). The caps of `scope` (6) and `shells` (6) are not pinned one by one: the `+994 más` of the 1000 case is satisfied by either card (mutations tested: raising to 7 only that of `scope` or only that of `shells` fails nothing; raising both does). That of `cambios` (3) is only pinned from below: with 2 the body case fails (that was how it stood until 2026-11-12, and the `borradas` row was never drawn); raising it cannot make anything fail, the card publishes three rows. That of `tecnicas` (10) is pinned from both sides: the 1000-row case requires 10 and the eight-ticked case fails with 8 (that was how it stood until 2026-11-15: in AGGRESSIVE with everything ticked the only row hidden behind `… +1 más` was that of the class that is never auto-approved). Today the card always publishes 9 rows (8 tickable + `log-cleanup`), in order `✓`, `✗`, `?`: a tenth technique still fits; with eleven, the last would remain behind `… +1 más`. The label `(obligatorio)` is put by `techniqueBoard` on the `ALWAYS_CONFIRM` classes and only this case pins it. The height is respected by cutting the end: at height 3 with the split block no `│` row remains, nor that of `último bloqueo` (real output: header, `● guard`, `▸ modo`). The block goes back to one row only when the total does not fit with everything else folded: with the case's state (7 cards) that happens below a height of 15; between 12 and 14, 11 lines come out and rows are left over with the reason trimmed to one (proven by hand), and at height 9 the last two headers are lost just the same (5 GUARD rows + 6 headers are 11; proven by hand). The single row keeps the `GEKKO:` prefix: only the split one removes it. The height 12 case looks for the titles by substring in any line, not that they are headers; removing the line that un-splits makes it fail (mutation). The block's maximum of 4 rows is not proven: the case's text takes exactly 4 rows, and raising `BLOCK_WRAP` to 9 makes no case fail; a longer reason is cut at the fourth row with `…` (proven with 60 words), so "it is read whole" holds only if it fits in 4 rows, and the case checks a substring, not the whole text. With width ≤ 8 the block is not split. "At most the cap" holds for `renderRail`: `buildPanelState` and `parsePanelState` traverse the 1000 rows and the published JSON carries them all (118 kB with 1000 hosts and 1000 shells). The 20 000-card case is a wall-clock threshold (measured: 24–42 ms; a quadratic version, 1785 ms): it can fail from machine load without the code changing. Inherited ids: the case pins the cap (`Object.hasOwn(ROW_CAP, …)`), not the folding — changing `Object.hasOwn(ui.open, c.id)` to `c.id in ui.open` makes no case fail. The error case measures characters, not cells (cells and widths 0–3: REQ-TUI-002). The states "without `panel.json`" and invalid (`esperando al guard…`, `estado inválido`) are drawn by `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs`, with no case.

### REQ-TUI-013 — `renderBar` with the rail visible: a row with branch, mode, model, `ctx %/window`, `(auto)` and turn, without repeating what the cards show
`renderBar(state, 100, railVisible=true)` MUST return a row of at most 100 cells with the branch, the mode, the model, `ctx <pct>%/<window in k>` and `turno N`; MUST add ` (auto)` to the ctx segment only when `state.bar.auto` is true; MUST show `ctx ?` when the percentage and the window are null; and with guard ON MUST NOT include `guard`, `scope`, `shells` or `bloqueo`.
