An `extStatuses` that is not an array of strings MUST NOT make it throw.
- **WHEN** a state with branch `main`, model `deepseek-v4.1-flash`, ctx 42 %/200 000, turn 7, 2 hosts, last block and guard ON goes through `renderBar` at 100 columns with the rail visible and `auto` true and false; with `ctxPct` and `ctxWindow` null; and, without rail, with `extStatuses` equal to `[undefined, 5, null, {}]`, to `null` and to `undefined`.
- **THEN** with `auto` true the row contains `ctx 42%/200k (auto)`, `main`, `DEFAULT`, `deepseek-v4.1-flash` and `turno 7`, measures ≤ 100 and does not contain `scope`, `shells`, `guard` or `bloqueo`; with `auto` false it contains `ctx 42%/200k` and not `(auto)`; the unknown ctx comes out as `ctx ?`; the three calls with invalid `extStatuses` return a string.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderBar`
- Limit: "except a guard that is not ON" has no case under this requirement: `guard AUSENTE`/`FALLÓ` with the rail visible runs under REQ-TUI-010. Only at 100 columns and with short values: narrower or with long values the bar drops whole segments (proven with the rail visible: at 60 the model drops, at 40 the branch too, at 30 there remains `DEFAULT · ctx 42%/200k (auto)` without turn and at 20 `DEFAULT · turno 7` without ctx; an 88-character branch at 100 comes out trimmed with `…`; with long branch and model the branch disappears). Where the data comes from has no case: branch (`footerData.getGitBranch`), model (`ctx.model.id`), ctx (`ctx.getContextUsage`), statuses of other extensions and the mounting with `ctx.ui.setFooter` live in `publish` of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`; the smoke test with a real Pi is manual (GAP-67). That `(auto)` reflects Pi's compaction depends on `readCompactionEnabled` (cases under REQ-TUI-010) and on `publish` passing it, with no case. The branch, the model and the extension statuses are interpolated with no sanitizing beyond removing controls (that: REQ-TUI-020): proven by hand, an extension status `guard ON` is drawn next to the real one (`… · guard FALLÓ · guard ON`) and a branch `x · guard ON · scope 99` reads as three segments. That a valid extension status appears in the bar is asserted only in one case of REQ-TUI-020. No case: null percentage with known window (`ctx ?/200k`), window under 1000 (comes out without `k`), window of 1 000 000 (`1000k`) and percentage over 100 (comes out as-is).

### REQ-TUI-014 — `toggleAt` and `handleKey` return only `{open, cursor}` and do not mutate the state, the UI or the shared `runtime`
`toggleAt` and `handleKey`, called with a UI that only has `open` and `cursor`, MUST return an object whose only keys are `open` (with boolean values) and `cursor`, and MUST NOT mutate the `PanelState` or the `PanelUi` they receive nor the `runtime` singleton.
- **WHEN** over the fixture and `ui={open:{}}` `handleKey` is called with `j`, `k`, `enter`, `space`, `a`, `z`, `1`, `5` and `x` and `toggleAt` at rows 0, 1, 5 and 99; and, with the state and the UI deeply frozen (`Object.freeze`) — a state with guard ON and another with `bar.guard="FALLÓ"` —, `handleKey` with 20 keys (`j`, `k`, `up`, `down`, `enter`, `space`, `a`, `z`, `1`–`9`, `x`, `m`, `g`) and `toggleAt` at rows 0 to 79.
- **THEN** the state's JSON does not change; each result has only the keys `open` and `cursor`, and `open` only boolean values; no call throws on the frozen objects; and `JSON.stringify(runtime)` equals the one taken at the start of the panel selftest.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `toggleAt`, `handleKey`, `flip`
- Limit: only the two pure reducers. "No sidebar event can change mode, scope or policy" as a property of the `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` process (stdin → `parseMouse`/`toggleAt`/`handleKey` → `panel.ui.json`) has no case in the gate: the sha256 of `panel.json` and `scope.txt` after a burst of clicks and keys is measured only by `tools/tui-test.sh` (GAP-65). "The only thing the guard reads from the sidebar is the modification date of its heartbeat" has no case: it is a reading of the code (`sidebarLive` in `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` does a `statSync` of `panel.json.alive`, and the guard does not open `panel.ui.json`). That mtime decides the bar's `railVisible` (REQ-TUI-010): it does not change mode, scope or policy, but it does change what the operator sees in the bar, and any process can touch it (GAP-67). "Only `{open, cursor}`" depends on the input UI: the reducers copy with `{...ui}` (proven by hand: a UI with `mode:"aggressive"` and `open.evil:"x"` comes back with both); the filter is `parseUi` (REQ-TUI-019). The `runtime` comparison is by `JSON.stringify`, which does not see `activity.inflight` (a `Map`): mutating it from `handleKey` does not make the case fail (mutation tested). The reducers do not receive the runtime: the case only fails if one imports the singleton and touches it. In the first case the state snapshot is taken after the AC7 cases (a mutation of the state with the `z` key is detected only by the frozen-objects case), and its "runtime" comparison is that of the fixture's runtime, which the reducers never receive: that sub-assertion cannot fail (GAP-63).

### REQ-TUI-016 — `setup.sh` in check-only mode: exits 1 if tmux < 3.2, node < 22.19, one is missing or node does not run TypeScript
With `GEKKO_SETUP_CHECK_ONLY=1`, `setup.sh` MUST finish with code 0 when `tmux -V` prints `tmux 3.2a` or `tmux next-3.5` and `node --version` prints `v22.19.0`, and MUST finish with code 1 when tmux prints `tmux 3.1c` (with `tmux` in the output), when node prints `v22.18.0` (with `node` in the output) or `v21.9.0`, when there is no `tmux` binary or no `node` binary in the `PATH`, and when a node of sufficient version (`v24.21.0`) answers `false` to `node -p 'process.features.typescript'` (with `TypeScript` in the output).
- **WHEN** the selftest runs `/bin/bash setup.sh` with `GEKKO_SETUP_CHECK_ONLY=1` and with `HOME` and `PATH` pointing to a temporary directory that only contains fake scripts `npm`, `git` and `python3`, a link to `dirname` and, depending on the case, a `tmux` and a `node` that print a fixed string: (`tmux 3.2a`, `v22.19.0`), (`tmux next-3.5`, `v22.19.0`), (`tmux 3.1c`, `v22.19.0`), (`tmux 3.2a`, `v22.18.0`), (`tmux 3.2a`, `v21.9.0`), without `tmux`, without `node`, and a `node` that prints `v24.21.0` on `--version` and `false` on any other argument.
- **THEN** code 0 in the first two; code 1 in all the others; stdout+stderr contains `tmux` with `3.1c`, `node` with `v22.18.0` and `TypeScript` with the node that answers `false`.
- Code: `setup.sh` `ver_ge` and the prerequisites block (`command -v` loop, version checks, `process.features.typescript` check, `GEKKO_SETUP_CHECK_ONLY` output)
- Limit: only check-only mode. "With valid versions it continues" (AC16) has no case here: none of these runs `setup.sh` beyond the prerequisites (that of REQ-CFG-001 does run it whole, with fake binaries), nor asserts that check-only does not install or write (probe outside the gate: the temporary `HOME` stays empty). "Says what to install" is measured only as the output containing `tmux`, `node` or `TypeScript`: removing the install hint from the tmux message makes no case fail (mutation), and the text `detectado 'tmux 3.1c'` ("detected") already satisfies the pattern. For an absent binary only code 1 is asserted, not the message: removing the `command -v` loop also fails nothing (the version check cuts anyway). No case for absent `npm`, `git` or `python3` (probe: without `npm` it exits 1 with `falta npm` ("npm missing")). **The comparison of the major number is not proven:** replacing the body of `ver_ge` with "minor ≥ threshold" passes the 7 cases (GAP-70); the case titled "node 21.9 fails even though its minor is greater" does not distinguish it, because 9 < 19. No case: lower major with higher minor (`tmux 2.9`, `v21.20.0`) and higher major with lower minor (`tmux 4.0`, `v23.0.0`); probes outside the gate over the current code: the first two exit 1, the last two 0, `tmux 3.10` exits 0 and a version without `N.N` (`tmux master`) exits 1. The TypeScript check only cuts on the literal `false`: a node that prints `undefined` passes (probe). The message for an absent node suggests `apt install nodejs`, the package that the TypeScript check later rejects. The case depends on the host's `/bin/bash`, `sh` and `dirname`. It does not prove that the rail works with those versions. Outside check-only, with stdin on a terminal and `curl` present, a node that answers `false` no longer cuts: `setup.sh` asks and, on a yes, downloads and runs the nvm installer, installs node 22 and continues (GAP-75); in check-only, without a terminal or without `curl` it exits 1 with the same message.

### REQ-TUI-017 — `missingAcs` returns the expected ACs that have no cases
`missingAcs(counts, expected)` MUST return the numbers in `expected` that have no count in `counts`, and MUST return an empty list when all the expected ones have at least one case.
- **WHEN** `missingAcs(new Map([[1, 3]]), [1, 2])` and `missingAcs(new Map([[1, 1]]), [1])`.
- **THEN** `[2]` and `[]`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel-selftest.ts` `missingAcs`
- Limit: only the pure function with those two inputs (an AC absent from the map; an explicit count of `0` has no case). What AC17 asserts — that the selftest prints how many `008-ACn:` cases it ran per AC and fails if an expected one has zero — is the wiring at the end of `runPanelSelftest` (`acCounts`, `EXPECTED_008_ACS`, the `bad++` and the `console.log`) and **has no case** (GAP-71): removing the `bad++`, emptying `EXPECTED_008_ACS`, leaving the counter fixed at 1 or deleting the printed line makes no case fail (mutations). Probe outside the gate over the current code: with `15` added to the list, the gate prints `FAIL [panel]: ACs 008 esperados sin casos: AC15` (expected 008 ACs without cases), the count line carries `AC15=0` and it exits 1. The expected list is maintained by hand in the same file. The count is by message prefix: a case counts even if it fails and even if it is `ok(true)` (GAP-53). Second net, independent of this wiring: each `008-ACn:` is attributed to `REQ-TUI-0nn`, so an AC reconciled here that is left without cases makes the trace fail (§2c).

### REQ-TUI-018 — FINDINGS: `countFindings` reduces the ledger to seven integers and no field of a row reaches the published state
`countFindings` MUST return exactly seven keys with integer values (`hypothesis`, `triaged`, `confirmed`, `needs_validation`, `refuted`, `remediated`, `otros`): each non-empty line adds 1 to the key of its `status` field if it is one of those six, and to `otros` if it is not JSON, carries no `status` or carries another value; MUST NOT throw with empty text or with text that is not JSON. The state that `buildPanelState` builds with those counts MUST NOT contain the id, the title, the target, the evidence command or the unknown `status` of any row. `panelTick` MUST NOT publish the `hallazgos` card when `io.ledger()` returns `null`. The real reader (the guard's `io.ledger`) MUST be `readPanelLedger`, which MUST return `null` —without following, blocking or throwing— on a symbolic link, a FIFO, a file that does not exist or one over `PANEL_LEDGER_MAX_BYTES` (64 MiB): it runs on Pi's thread and in toolbox the container's root writes that directory. Spec 024: the card carries the «remediado» ("remediated") row and ` remediados N` in the summary only with N > 0; its seven states and «visor» ("viewer") (8 rows) fit in DEFAULT_ROW_CAP without trimming.
- **WHEN** `countFindings` receives an 11-line ledger — one row for each of the six states (the `confirmed` one with id `CANARY-ID`, title `CANARY-TITLE`, target `CANARY-TARGET` and `evidence.command` `CANARY-EVID`; the `refuted` one with a canary title and target), a row with status `CANARY-STATUS`, the non-JSON line `{línea CANARY rota`, an empty line, the line `42` and a row without `status` —; `countFindings("")`; `countFindings("\x00\xff basura\n[")`; `buildPanelState(rt, { findings: <those counts> }, 1)`; and `panelTick(true, io, rt, 6)` with `ledger: () => null`; `readPanelLedger` over a regular ledger, a link to a foreign JSONL, a FIFO, a 65-byte file with cap 64 and a nonexistent path; and the text of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`.
- **THEN** 1 in each of the six states and `otros === 4`; seven keys, all `Number.isInteger`; `otros` 0 and 2 for the empty text and the garbage; neither the state's JSON nor the title, the right, the summary and the rows of its cards contain `CANARY`; the JSON the tick writes carries no `hallazgos` card; `readPanelLedger` returns the regular one's text and `null` in the other four, the FIFO in under 1 s; the guard's `io.ledger` calls `readPanelLedger`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `countFindings`, `FINDING_STATUSES`, `buildPanelState`, `panelTick`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `readPanelLedger`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `io.ledger`
- Limit: pure functions. That `io.ledger` uses `readPanelLedger` is checked by reading the guard's text, not by running the guard (GAP-73): that the path is never published and that an unreadable file does not break hold by construction (the path does not enter `panelTick`; a read failure is treated as "no source" and the card disappears), not by a case, and no fixture carries a path. "Unreadable" in the cases is garbage text passed as a string. `FINDING_STATUSES` is a hand copy of `STATUSES` in `.claude/tools/findings.py`: no case compares the two lists. That the card appears with a ledger, in its slot and with its values: REQ-TUI-001 and REQ-TUI-011. Probes outside the gate: these go to `otros`: a status with other capitalization or with a space (`Confirmed`, ` confirmed`), the key `Status`, a status that is not a string, the first line of a file with a BOM and each line of an indented JSON (one row over 4 lines → `otros` 4). `buildPanelState` does not require integers in the card's right or summary (only the rows pass through `int`): with `findings: { confirmed: "CANARY", … }` it publishes `CANARY/… confirmados` and `✓ CANARY`; today its only caller passes it the output of `countFindings`. A `panel.json` written by a third party can put any text in that card (`parsePanelState` validates the shape, not the content): REQ-TUI-020.

### REQ-TUI-019 — `parseUi` / `serializeUi`: folding roundtrips and a corrupt JSON falls back to defaults
`parseUi` MUST return an `open` without keys, without throwing, for text that is not JSON, for the empty string, for `null` and for an `open` that is not an object; MUST keep in `open` only the ids of `CARD_IDS` whose value is boolean and `cursor` only if it is an id of `CARD_IDS`, and MUST NOT return any other key from the file. `parseUi(serializeUi(ui))` MUST preserve the values of `open` and the `cursor`.
- **WHEN** `parseUi("{")`, `parseUi("null")`, `parseUi('{"open":5}')` and `parseUi("")`; `parseUi(serializeUi({ open: { scope: false, cambios: true }, cursor: "kb" }))`; and `parseUi` of a JSON with `open: { scope: "no", evil: true, kb: false }`, a `cursor` that is the sequence `\x1b[2J` and a key `extra`.
- **THEN** `open` without keys in the first four; `open.scope === false`, `open.cambios === true` and `cursor === "kb"` after the roundtrip; in the last one `open` carries neither `scope` nor `evil`, `open.kb === false`, `cursor` is `undefined` and the result carries no `extra`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `parseUi`, `serializeUi`
- Limit: only the two pure functions. "Folding survives closing and reopening the rail" (AC19) is I/O of `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` (`persistUi` with tmp+rename, a single read at startup) and has no case in the gate: it is covered only by checks of `tools/tui-test.sh` (real tmux, not a gate; GAP-65), as is the corrupt file on disk. The folding reset on each launch (D12, `tools/pi-hitl.sh`) neither: the launcher deletes `<state without .json>.ui.json` and the sidebar reads `panel.ui.json` in the state directory, paths that coincide only if the state file is called `panel.json` (read, not executed). The roundtrip is proven with two ids and the cursor `kb`. `serializeUi` does not filter: it writes unknown ids and an invalid cursor as-is; the filter is read-only (probe outside the gate: `{ open: { evil: true, scope: false }, cursor: "nope" }` is reread as `{ open: { scope: false } }`). `parseUi` accepts as `cursor` the id of a card that is not drawn (`hallazgos` without a ledger; probe).

### REQ-TUI-020 — Hostile content: `buildPanelState`, `renderRail`, `renderBar` and `resolveTheme` let through no controls, C1, the listed format characters or a role that is not a color SGR; the published rows are redacted
`stripControl` in its default mode MUST remove, in addition to what is in REQ-PNL-001, TAB, CR and LF and the characters U+061C, U+200B, U+202E, U+2028, U+2029, U+2060, U+2066, U+2069, U+FEFF, U+FFF9–U+FFFB and the tag U+E0041; with `keepWs=true` it MUST keep TAB and LF. `buildPanelState` MUST publish without `[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]`, without U+202A–U+202E and without U+200B–U+200F the scope host, the shell name, the last block, the branch, the model and the role keys, and MUST publish as a role only a value that matches `ROLE_RE` (a single foreground color SGR). `buildPanelState` MUST pass through `redactSummary` the label and the value of each card row and `bar.lastBlock`: the value of a credential key or a 32+ token placed in the last block, in a scope host or in a shell name MUST NOT reach the published state (the `panel.json` JSON) nor the rail and bar drawn from it; it MUST NOT redact `bar.branch` or `bar.model`. `renderRail`, over a state that did not go through `buildPanelState`, MUST NOT emit any `\x1b` outside `\x1b[0m`, `\x1b[1m`, `\x1b[2m`, `\x1b[4m`, `\x1b[22m`, `\x1b[24m` and the sequences that match `ROLE_RE`, nor CR, nor an LF inside a row, nor C1, nor those format characters, whether they come in the title, the right, the summary, the glyph, the label or the value of a row, or in a theme role. `renderRail` MUST draw between double quotes the title of a card whose id is not `guard` and the label of a row of that card when, after `stripControl`, they start with the word `guard` (case-insensitive, preceded only by characters that are not letters or digits and not followed by a letter or digit); it MUST NOT quote the row or the title of the `guard` card. `resolveTheme` MUST replace with the fallback palette's color a published role that does not match `ROLE_RE`. `renderBar` MUST NOT emit `[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]` or the listed format characters from the branch or from the statuses of other extensions.
- **WHEN** (publish) `buildPanelState` with scope host `10.0.0.1\r\x1b[2J\x1b]0;pwn\x07host`, last block `guard ON\nguard ON` + U+202E + ` falso\x9b31m`, a shell `sh\x1b[2Jell\n`, branch `main\r\n\x1b[31m`, model `m` + U+200B + `x` and roles `accent: \x1b[38;2;1;2;3m`, `danger: \x1b[2J`, `warn: \x1b[31m\x1b[2J` and a key `k\x1b[2J` with value `\x1b[32m`; (draw) the copy of a valid state with `E` = `\x1b]0;pwn\x07\x1b[2J\x9b31m` + U+202E + `\r\n` placed in the title, the right and the summary of the GUARD card, in the glyph, the label and the value of two SHELLS rows (one with role `\x1b[2J`, another with role `__proto__` and label `h\rguard ON`) and in the label of the first SCOPE row (`10.0.0.1` + `E` + `guard ON`), drawn with `renderRail` at 48×60 with a theme whose `danger`, `warn` and `dim` are `\x1b[2J`, `\x1b[31m\x1b[2J` and an OSC, once with everything open and once with guard, shells and scope folded; four rows whose glyph is `\x1b`, `\r`, `\x9b` and `\x07`; the string `a` U+2066 `b` U+2069 `c` U+061C `d` U+2060 `e` U+FEFF `F` U+2028 `g` U+2029 `h` U+E0041 `i` U+FFF9 `j` U+FFFA `k` U+FFFB `l` passed to `stripControl` and placed in a title, a row label, `bar.branch` and an extension status; `renderBar` at 160 columns with branch `x\x1b[2J\x9b31m\r\ny` and the extension status `ext\x1b]0;p\x07 ok`; `resolveTheme` with valid roles `success`, `warning`, `error` and `muted` and `accent: \x1b[2J`; a last block `x ` × 30 + `● guard  ON` drawn at 20 columns; `stripControl` of `a\r\nb\tc\x85d` + U+202E + `e` + U+200B + `f` and, with `keepWs`, of `a\nb\tc\x85`; (redact) `buildPanelState` of a runtime with healthy guard, hosts `10.0.0.1` and `10.0.0.2 password=Winter2026!`, last block `GEKKO: ampliación de scope RECHAZADA (10.0.0.9 api_token=S3cr3tTokenValue) — sigue vigente el scope anterior` and an open shell `psexec-` + 32 hex, serialized to JSON, drawn with `renderRail` at 48×60 and with `renderBar` at 200; and the same runtime with a model of 60 `m` and branch `feature/` + 40 `x`; (imitate) `buildPanelState` of a runtime with guard FALLÓ, hosts `guard  ON` and `10.0.0.1` and an open shell `guard  ON`, drawn at 48×60; and that state with an added card of id `x`, title `GUARD`, right `ON` and rows `●` U+00A0 + `Guard` = `ON` and `guardian`.
- **THEN** (publish) `accent` keeps its value, `danger` and `warn` are not among the roles, and every published role value matches `^\x1b\[[0-9;]+m$`; no string or key of the state, outside the role values, contains those characters; the value of the `último bloqueo` row contains `guard ONguard ON`, a single string. (draw) after removing the permitted sequences, the lines contain no `\x1b`, C0, `\x7f`, C1, U+202A–U+202E or U+200B–U+200F; joining the lines with LF and splitting again gives the same number of lines; no line is exactly `guard ON` or `ON`; the output does not contain `\x1b[2J`; the four control-glyph rows come out without `[\x00-\x09\x0b-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]`; `stripControl` of the format string gives `abcdeFghijkl`, and neither the rail nor the bar emit U+061C, U+2028, U+2029, U+2060–U+2069, U+FEFF, U+FFF9–U+FFFB or U+E0000–U+E007F; the bar comes out without `[\x00-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]` and keeps `ext`; the resolved `accent` is that of the dark fallback palette; exactly one line starts with `  ● guard`; `stripControl` gives `abcdef` and, with `keepWs`, `a\nb\tc`. (redact) neither the JSON nor the rail nor the bar contain `Winter2026`, `S3cr3tTokenValue` or `aad3b435`; the rail carries `  │ api_token=[REDACTED] — sigue`, `  · 10.0.0.2 password=[REDACTED]`, `  ● [REDACTED]  open` and `  · 10.0.0.1`, and `bar.lastBlock` contains `api_token=[REDACTED]`; `bar.model` and `bar.branch` come back whole. (imitate) the rail carries `  · "guard  ON"`, `  ● "guard  ON"  open` and `  · 10.0.0.1`, and the only line that matches `^  \S guard\b` is `  ✗ guard  FALLÓ`; with the added card it carries `▾ "GUARD"  ON`, `  ● "` U+00A0 `Guard"  ON` and `  · guardian`, and a single line starts with `▾ GUARD` or `▸ GUARD`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `stripControl`, `ROLE_RE`, `buildPanelState` (`row`, `san`), `redactSummary`, `renderRail`, `resolveTheme`, `renderBar`
- Limit: it is not "hostile content does not reach the terminal": it is those pure functions with those fixtures. The redaction is that of `redactSummary`, with its limits (REQ-PNL-001), and is applied only on publishing and only to the rows and to `bar.lastBlock`. The cards' right and summary do not go through it (on the legitimate path: numbers, fixed words and technique names), nor `bar.branch`, `bar.model` or the statuses of other extensions that `renderBar` receives (probe: a branch `token=abc` and a status `password=ext` come out whole in the bar), and `renderRail` does not redact: a `panel.json` written by a third party is drawn with whatever it carries (probe: a row `password=ajeno` ("foreign")). LF and TAB are deleted before redacting, not changed to a space: a single reading, the glued one (the detail view uses two: REQ-PNL-008). The `)` attached to the value is lost (`api_token=…)` → `api_token=[REDACTED]`), and a shell name with a run of 32+ ends up whole as `[REDACTED]`: the operator does not see which shell it is. The target of an authorization (TÉCNICAS row) goes through the same function, with no case (probe: `account (token=abc)` → `account (token=[REDACTED]`). Of 32 secret templates placed in the last block, a host and a shell name, 13 remain visible: the same ones that the detail view of 1875528 did not mask whole either (`usuario:clave@host`, `-p clave`, tokens under 32…; probe outside the gate). The process that writes to the terminal (`toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs`: read `panel.json`, `parsePanelState`, position and write each line) and the footer that `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` mounts with `setFooter` have no case in the gate, and `tools/tui-test.sh` carries no AC20 check (GAP-65). **"Nor can it draw a false `guard ON` row" holds only for what is not the GUARD card, and only for the ASCII word:** a host, a shell or a foreign card called `guard…` come out between quotes (cases above), and `parsePanelState` rejects an inconsistent guard (REQ-TUI-001); a `panel.json` rewritten whole and coherently by a third party still draws `▾ GUARD  ON` / `  ● guard  ON`, and the bar `guard ON`. This is not a defect against spec 008: its AC20 (Amendment 2, 2026-11-12) no longer promises authenticity and §6 declares the risk (mirror, not authority); any process of the same user, also an agent command, can write the file, and a live guard overwrites it at its next publication (every 2 s; read, not executed). The quotes do not cover (probes outside the gate): a homoglyph (`guаrd` with U+0430 → `  ● guаrd  ON`), a letter-class filler in front (U+3164 + `guard`), the text `guard  ON` in the value of a row (`  ● x  guard  ON`) nor in the right or summary of another card (`▾ SCOPE  guard ON`); on the legitimate path those last two are numbers and fixed words. A legitimate host that starts with `guard` followed by a dot or a hyphen comes out quoted (`"guard.corp"`); `guardian` does not. `renderBar` quotes nothing: a model named `x · guard ON` has no case. What the "false row" cases measure is that no row carries an LF and that no line is only `guard ON` or `ON`, a one-space pattern that does not describe the genuine row either (`● guard  ON`, with glyph and two spaces). The case `a hostile block split into rows produces no second ● guard row` cannot fail by the path it names: changing the `│` glyph of the split rows to `●`, raising `BLOCK_WRAP` to 6 or disabling the split does not make it fail (mutations; the hostile text of its fixture falls in the fifth row, which is trimmed to `● g…`); it fails only if the genuine row disappears (GAP-62). Probe: with `aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa ● guard  ON` the split row comes out `  │ ● guard ON`, always behind `│`. No "file name" fixture: CAMBIOS carries only three integers, and the AC20 test column no longer asks for it (it returns if CAMBIOS comes to publish file rows). On publishing, the cards' right and summary have no hostile fixture (removing their sanitizing makes no case fail: on the legitimate path they are numbers and fixed words; the drawing does cover them), nor `bar.mode` or `bar.guard`. Removing a whole CSI is REQ-PNL-001's: taking it out of `stripControl` makes no `008-AC20` case fail. Of the format class only the listed code points have a case: U+200C–U+200F, U+202A–U+202D, U+2061–U+2065, U+2067, U+2068 and the rest of the tags do not. `stripControl` does not remove (probe): U+00AD, U+034F, U+115F, U+180E, U+2800, U+3164, U+00A0, U+2000, U+3000, U+FE0F, U+E0100, U+1D173 or the combining characters (U+0338): invisible filler and overlay remain possible inside a row. From an escape that is not CSI the tail remains as inert text (`]0;pwn`, GAP-33). `ROLE_RE` accepts any foreground color: probe, `danger: \x1b[30m` and `dim: \x1b[38;2;13;17;23m` (the dark reference background) are accepted and `FALLÓ` is drawn in that color — a hostile role with a valid SGR can make an alert unreadable; the contrast is only proven for the fallback palette (REQ-TUI-009). `\x1b[38;2;999;999;999m` also matches.

### REQ-TUI-021 — `panelTick` with `hasUI=false` invokes no member of the `io` (pure function), and the guard only publishes in `tui` mode
`panelTick(false, io, rt, now)` MUST return `null` without invoking any of the nine members of the `io` (`theme`, `ledger`, `gitRefresh`, `git`, `compaction`, `usage`, `workspace`, `write`, `mountBar`; `workspace` since spec 010); with `hasUI=true` it MUST invoke all nine (`write` with the state's JSON) and MUST return the state. The guard MUST pass it `true` only when `ctx.hasUI` and `ctx.mode === "tui"`: in rpc (`hasUI` true, no terminal) it MUST NOT write `panel.json` and MUST keep delivering to the client the guard's status row as the `gekko` widget.
- **WHEN** `panelTick(false, io, newRuntime(0), 1)` with a double `io` that records the calls to its nine members; then `panelTick(true, io, rt, 5)` with the same double and a two-line ledger; and the real extension is loaded with `GEKKO_PANEL_STATE` in a new file and its `session_start` is called with a fake ctx `hasUI:true, mode:"rpc"` and then with `mode:"tui"`.
- **THEN** `null` and zero recorded calls; with `hasUI=true`, non-null result and the calls to `theme`, `gitRefresh`, `git`, `ledger`, `compaction`, `usage`, `mountBar` and one `write` are recorded; after the `session_start` in rpc the file does not exist and `setWidget("gekko", …)` received a line with `guard ON`; after the tui one the file exists.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `panelTick`, `PanelIo`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `publish`, `render`
- Limit: the pure function with a double `io`, and one wiring case with a fake `pi` and ctx: it is not "the guard without a TUI" with a real Pi. The wiring case pins `publish`'s decision (passing it `ctx.hasUI` alone, as until 2026-11-12, makes it fail: in rpc it wrote the file); `render` and `refreshPanelLive` still cut on `ctx.hasUI`, and that with `hasUI=false` they do not publish has no case of its own (the harness's headless ctx carries only `hasUI` and `cwd`: a `publish` that reached `io.usage` without a TUI would throw). The case looks at the file and the widget; that in rpc git is not launched or the ledger read follows from `panelTick(false, …)` (first clause), not from direct observation. In rpc the widget row comes from `buildPanelState(runtime, {})`, without `io`: no branch, model, ctx % or counts (`DEFAULT · ctx ? · turno 0 · guard ON · scope 0 · shells 0`). No gate case starts Pi. Run by hand with Pi 1.5.0 on 2026-11-12, with a dead local endpoint and no model: `--mode rpc` gives `ctx.hasUI=true` and `ctx.mode="rpc"` (before the fix it wrote `panel.json`; after, it does not, and emits `setWidget gekko` with that row and no `setFooter`); `-p` and `--mode json -p` give `hasUI=false` and do not write; the TUI in tmux gives `mode="tui"`, writes `panel.json` and mounts the bar. A Pi whose ctx carries no `mode` would not publish anything in TUI either: the rail would stay at `esperando al guard…` ("waiting for the guard…") and the status row would come out as a widget (read, not executed; Pi 1.2.0's documentation already names `ctx.mode`). An exception from `io.write` propagates from `panelTick` (probe; in the guard `writePanelFile` swallows its errors). The 2 s timer of `refreshPanelLive` and git's `execFile` have no case (GAP-69).

### REQ-TUI-022 — The workspace is seen in the rail with the SESIÓN card open and folded, unredacted
`buildPanelState` MUST put the workspace name as the first row of the SESIÓN card and at the start of its summary (what the rail draws with the card folded); without a workspace, both MUST say `sin workspace` ("no workspace"). The name MUST be shown sanitized, trimmed to 31 characters and without turning into `[REDACTED]`.
- **WHEN** `buildPanelState` with `workspace` = a 31-character name, and with `workspace` null; the state is drawn with `renderRail` at 48 columns with the SESIÓN card open and with it folded.
- **THEN** with a workspace: the full name appears in a SESIÓN row (open) and in the header line (folded), and no line contains `[REDACTED]`; without a workspace: `sin workspace` appears in both cases.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `buildPanelState`, `PanelExtras`, `PanelIo.workspace`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `io.workspace`
- Limit: pure function. The name comes from `GEKKO_WORKSPACE` in Pi's process: it is a mirror of the environment, not an authority (same limit as the rest of `panel.json`, REQ-TUI-020). At 36 columns a long name is trimmed at the end. With a short rail the SESIÓN card is the first to fold: that is why the name is also in the summary.

### REQ-TUI-023 — Without a rail, the status bar carries the workspace
`renderBar` with `railVisible=false` MUST include the workspace (`ws <name>`, or `sin workspace`) with priority immediately after guard and mode; with the rail visible it MUST NOT repeat it.
- **WHEN** `renderBar` at 80 columns with `railVisible=false`, a 31-character workspace, branch and model of realistic length; the same state with `railVisible=true`; and a state without a workspace with `railVisible=false`.
- **THEN** the 80-column bar contains the full `ws <name>` next to guard and mode; with the rail visible it does not contain `ws `; without a workspace it contains `sin workspace`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `renderBar`, `PanelBar`
- Limit: pure function; below the width at which guard, mode and workspace fit, the workspace is trimmed or dropped before guard and mode.

### REQ-TUI-024 — The mirror check lives in `/gekko-policy` in its own block and is not an authorization
`viewRows` MUST return a `mirror` row ticked unless the value is `false` (no value counts as ticked); `policyRows` MUST NOT contain any mirror row; `policyOverlayBody` MUST draw that row after a blank line and a header «vista (no es una autorización)» ("view (not an authorization)"), with the selection mark and the hint of the selected element; toggling the check MUST NOT touch `runtime.auth` or the mode, and `session_start` MUST NOT reset it.
- **WHEN** `viewRows` with `true`, `false` and without a value; `policyRows`; `policyOverlayBody` with a technique selected and with the view row selected, ticked and unticked; the toggle over a runtime with an authorization; and a real `session_start` of the guard with `runtime.mirror = false`.
- **THEN** the row is ticked with `true` and without a value, and unticked with `false`; `policyRows` has no `mirror`; the row goes in its own block after the techniques; the `›` mark and the hint are those of the selected one; after toggling, `runtime.auth` is the same array and the mode does not change; after `session_start` the value stays `false`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `viewRows`, `policyOverlayBody`, `PanelRuntime.mirror`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `openPolicyView`
- Limit: the drawing and the pure toggle are in the gate; that the real overlay's keys (↑/↓, space, `c`) do what is described is not exercised: the overlay needs a Pi TUI. The value lives in the `globalThis` singleton, does not persist between processes and only affects new shells; unticking it does not close windows already open. It is not a pre-authorization: it changes nothing of what the guard blocks or confirms.

### REQ-TUI-025 — The window shortcuts only act in Pi's tmux session
`navBindings` MUST generate, for `S-Right`, `S-Left`, `F3` and `F2`, a `bind-key -T root <key> if-shell -F '#{@gekko_pi}' <next-window | previous-window> <else>` in argv form, where `<else>` is the operator's previous binding as-is or, if there was none, `send-keys <key>`.
- **WHEN** `navBindings` with a previous binding for `S-Right`, another for `F3` and none for `S-Left` or `F2`.
- **THEN** four bindings come out in that order; the condition is `#{@gekko_pi}`; right and `F3` go to `next-window`, left and `F2` to `previous-window`; the `else` of `S-Right` is the literal previous one and that of `S-Left` is `send-keys S-Left`; each binding has 9 arguments, all strings, none joined into a shell string.
- **WHEN** additionally, the text of `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` is passed through the shortcuts' regex contract, and each clause is mutated (without marking the session with `@gekko_pi` = the launcher's pid, without `--keys` before Pi, without `--keys-restore` or without removing the mark after Pi, a rail that does not take the argv from `navBindings`, without clearing the marks of dead pids before installing or before returning the shortcuts).
- **THEN** the real text meets the contract and each mutation makes its violation appear.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `navBindings`, `NAV_KEYS`; `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` `--keys`, `--keys-restore`; `tools/pi-hitl.sh`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace-selftest.ts` `keysViolations`
- Limit: the gate pins the argv and, by regex, the scripts' wiring, not their effect. The `@gekko_pi` mark is the launcher's pid (any non-empty value other than 0 is true for `if-shell -F`); `--keys` and `--keys-restore` first clear the mark of every session whose pid no longer lives (`staleMirrors`, same criterion as the mirror windows; EPERM counts as alive) and `--keys-restore` returns the shortcuts only if no session keeps a mark. The cleanup (unmarking and returning the shortcuts) is registered in an exit trap of the launcher: it runs on normal exit and also if the tmux session is closed or it receives HUP/TERM/INT (exits with 128+signal; the signal is handled when Pi returns), proven by `tools/ws-test.sh` (T13), outside the gate. SIGKILL cannot be trapped: the dead launcher leaves its mark until the next start or the next exit of a launcher, and in between the shortcuts stay installed. The installation, the cleanup of orphan marks, the restoration of the operator's bindings on exit and that outside the marked session the key reaches the application intact live in `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` and `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and are exercised with `tools/ws-test.sh`, outside the gate (GAP-77). A pid reused by another live process keeps the mark (no closing on pid reuse). `Shift+arrow` reaches tmux only if the terminal sends it distinguishable (proven in kitty + Herdr); if not, `F3`/`F2` remain.

### REQ-TUI-026 — `--fit` derives the session from the window id and does not stack rails
`tools/gekko-sidebar.sh --fit <window>` MUST take the session from that window (`display-message -p -t "$win" '#{session_name}'`, not from a `display-message` without context) and MUST serialize the find-and-open per window with `tmux wait-for -L`, released in an exit trap (`wait-for -U`).
- **WHEN** the selftest reads `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` and applies regular expressions over its code without comments, and each clause is mutated (session without context, without the lock, lock not released on exit).
- **THEN** the real text meets the contract and each mutation makes its violation appear.
- Code: `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` mode `--fit`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace-selftest.ts` `fitViolations`
- Limit: the gate only pins the text; the effect is proven by `tools/tui-test.sh` with real tmux (two sessions, one client each, latest activity in the other: four resizes leave one rail, and six simultaneous `--fit` leave one), outside the gate (GAP-77). Origin: a defect of spec 008 seen in the real run: the hook runs without a client, the "current" session was the one with the most activity, `find_sb` did not see the rail and opened one more per resize. The modes that run inside a pane (open, `--toggle`, `--restore`, `--keys`) still resolve the session by context. The lock is tmux's, lives in the server: a `--fit` that dies with SIGKILL before releasing it blocks the following ones of that window until the server restarts.
### REQ-TUI-027 — FINDINGS: the report module row only with an exact loopback URL
`validReportUrl` MUST return the string only if it is exactly `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` with a port from 1 to 65535, and `null` for any other value. `buildPanelState` MUST add to the `hallazgos` card a last row `visor <URL>` ("viewer <URL>") only when the card exists and `validReportUrl(reportUrl)` is not `null`; in any other case the card MUST keep its six rows. `panelTick` MUST NOT invoke `io.reportUrl` with `hasUI=false`.
- **WHEN** `buildPanelState` with counts and `reportUrl` = `http://127.0.0.1:65535/`, and the state is drawn with `renderRail` at 36 columns; with `reportUrl` absent, `null` and `""`; with each of the invalid forms tested (`localhost`, `https`, no trailing slash, with a path, port `99999`, port `0`, another host, leading space, a control or ANSI escape inside, and values that are not strings); with the canary ledger of REQ-TUI-018 and a valid URL; with `findings: null` and a valid URL; and `panelTick(false, …)` with an `io` whose members throw.
- **THEN** the card carries the row and a line of the rail contains the full URL; without a URL or with an invalid form the card has exactly six rows and `validReportUrl` returns `null`; the state still does not contain `CANARY`; without a ledger there is no card; the tick without UI does not throw.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `validReportUrl`, `buildPanelState`, `PanelExtras.reportUrl`, `PanelIo.reportUrl`, `panelTick`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `io.reportUrl`
- Limit: pure functions. The URL comes from `GEKKO_REPORT_URL` in Pi's process, which `tools/pi-hitl.sh` exports only if `findings_web.py ensure` exited 0 (REQ-RPT-013): it says where the module was available when the session started, not that it is still alive or that whoever answers on that port is the module. With the card folded the URL is not seen. A `panel.json` written by a third party can put any text in that row (REQ-TUI-020).

### REQ-TUI-028 — The «ejecución» ("execution") block of `/gekko-policy`: a row without a checkbox, a one-key confirmation inside the overlay
`policyOverlayBody` MUST accept an optional fourth argument (the row from `execRow`); without it it MUST return exactly the previous body, and with it it MUST add, after the «vista» block, a blank line, the header `ejecución (dónde nacen las shells · no es una autorización)` and a single row without a checkbox, selectable after those of techniques and view, with its hint when selected. `execRow` MUST give `os`, or `toolbox · levantando|lista|caída` (in `caída`, the reason, sanitized and cut to the width), with the action: `os` → switch to toolbox, `toolbox` `lista` → go back to `os`, `toolbox` `caída` → retry (the hint sends to `/gekko-toolbox off` to go to `os`), `levantando` → none (the hint says to wait) and without a workspace → none (the hint says to leave Pi and choose or create one in the launcher's menu). `execConfirmLines` MUST, toward toolbox or on retry, name the workspace, the container and the real path of the directory being mounted (whole, in several rows), say whether it carries a VPN (with `GEKKO_OVPN`, a warning line against two tunnels with the same `.ovpn`), whether the container will be CREATED, STARTED or ADOPTED (CREARÁ, ARRANCARÁ or ADOPTARÁ) and, with shells open on the host (`hostShells` > 0), how many remain and that they stay active and can reach the target (the change neither moves nor closes them; finding 5 of the 013/014 review), without removing `s`; while that datum is unknown, with an unresolved real path or with a directory that is a symbolic link it MUST say so and `canConfirm` MUST be false. Toward `os` it MUST say that new shells are born on the host and that `bash`'s block stops applying, and `canConfirm` MUST be true. `execStepKey` MUST return `confirm` only for `s`/`S` with `canConfirm`, `wait` for `s`/`S` without it and `close` for any other key. All text coming from docker or from disk MUST come out without control characters, without tmux styling (`#[`, `#(`) and bounded to the width.
- **WHEN** `policyOverlayBody` with and without the argument (compared, in addition, against the body of the file's previous version in a probe outside the gate), with the row selected and with another; `execRow` for each mode and state, with and without a workspace, and with a hostile reason (escapes, `#[bg=red]`, line breaks, 400 characters) at 40 and 70 columns; `execConfirmLines` for create, start and adopt, with and without VPN, with unknown plan, with symbolic link, without real path, with a 100-character path at 50 columns, for retry, toward `os`, with 0, 1, 2 and 3 shells on the host, and with hostile workspace, container and path; `execStepKey` with `s`, `S`, thirteen different keys and `s` without being able to confirm.
- **THEN** the body without the argument does not mention «ejecución» and has its 22 lines; with it, removing the block's three lines gives the previous body; the row has no `[ ]` or `[x]`; the `›` mark and the hint are those of the selected row; `policyRows` and `viewRows` have no execution row; the actions are those listed and the hints say what was asked; the hostile reason comes out without controls or `#[` and fits the width; each plan says its verb and only that one; with a VPN there is one warning line and without it there is no mention of `.ovpn`; unknown plan, link and unresolved path give `canConfirm` false and do not offer `s`; the long path is seen whole and every row fits; toward `os` it says what was asked and accepts `s`; with shells on the host toward toolbox or on retry there is a single line «quedan N shell(s) en el host … siguen activas» ("N shell(s) remain on the host … still active") and `s` is accepted, not with 0 nor toward `os`; only `s`/`S` confirm.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `policyOverlayBody`, `execRow`, `execConfirmLines`, `execStepKey`, `ExecInput`, `ExecConfirmInput`, `ExecRow`, `ContainerPlan`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `openExecDialog` (counts `hostShells`)
- Limit: pure functions. That `c` does not touch the row holds because the row is not a `PolicyKey` and does not enter `toggleTechnique` (there is no case on the overlay's keys: it needs a Pi TUI, same limit as REQ-TUI-024); the wiring (opening the confirmation, the real path and the asynchronous `docker inspect` with a deadline that say create/start/adopt, that `s` is not accepted until it arrives, bringing up and saving the mode) belongs to `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` and is covered by the next paragraph. The count of `hostShells` in `openExecDialog` (registry shells not closed with place `os`) has no wiring case: only the pure function; a host shell that the registry does not know is not counted. The confirmation does not authenticate whoever presses the key: the model can type in Pi's TUI (spec 014 §6). The text «ADOPTARÁ» describes what the `inspect` saw at an instant; a `docker run` by the model between the confirmation and the start is adopted under a text that said something else (the adoption check applies just the same: REQ-TBX-003). The trimming to the width counts cells with `gekko-panel.ts`'s short table (without the full `wcwidth` one).

Wiring in the real overlay (spec 014 T4, D7). The `/gekko-policy` component MUST traverse `[...techniques, ...view, execution]`; Space/Enter on the execution row MUST open the confirmation INSIDE the overlay (without a nested `ctx.ui.confirm`) and `execStepKey` MUST decide each key while it is open (`s` confirms only with `canConfirm`; `wait` does not close; any other closes it without changing anything). On opening a confirmation toward toolbox or a retry it MUST resolve the real path of the workspace directory and whether it is a symbolic link, and launch an asynchronous `docker inspect --type container` BY NAME `gekko-tb-<ws>` with a 3 s deadline: until the plan arrives it is `unknown` and `s` is not accepted; when it arrives (create if it does not exist, start if stopped, adopt if running) it MUST request a redraw; an error that is not «no such container» MUST be shown as a reason and `s` is not offered. `c` MUST NOT touch the block (neither the mode nor the state); outside `ctx.mode === "tui"` the block MUST NOT be actionable; without a workspace the row says how to create one and opens no confirmation or invokes docker.
- **WHEN** the real guard mounted against a fake `pi` and the component delivered by `ctx.ui.custom` driven from the selftest with a fake docker/CLI: from `os` with the `inspect` pending (keys `s` and another) and then resolved as nonexistent, stopped, running and with a permission error; the CLI bringing up in flight and resolved; from `lista` toward `os` with a foreign key and with `s`; from `caída` retrying; `c` with an authorization; an `rpc` `ctx`; without a workspace; with a workspace that is a symbolic link.
- **THEN** the confirmation opens inside the overlay with «comprobando…» ("checking…") and `s` does nothing until the `inspect` arrives (a redraw is requested and then it says CREARÁ, ARRANCARÁ or ADOPTARÁ with the container and the real path); the `inspect` is a call by name with deadline 3000; `s` leaves the state `levantando` immediately and `lista` when the ID arrives, with the mode saved; toward `os` it changes memory and file without calling docker; another key or Esc closes without changing anything; in `caída` it offers «Reintentar» ("Retry") and goes back to `lista`; a permission error is shown and `s` is not accepted; `c` leaves the block intact; with `rpc` and without a workspace nothing opens and docker is not called; the symbolic link is stated and cannot be confirmed.
- Code (wiring): `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `openPolicyView`, `execDialogView`, `openExecDialog`, `lookupContainer`
- Limit (wiring): proven with the component delivered to `ctx.ui.custom` and simulated keys, not with a real Pi TUI that delivers them (same limit as REQ-TUI-024). The create/start/adopt plan describes an instant: the container comes up after the key (GAP-78 and the `docker run` race above). Without a TUI (`ctx.mode` other than `tui`) there is no actionable overlay or periodic check.

### REQ-TUI-029 — The rail and the bar show the execution mode, the toolbox state and where each shell lives, and cannot change anything
`buildPanelState` MUST publish `exec` (mode, state, reason) and an `ejecución` row in the GUARD card only when `PanelExtras.execMode` is `os` or `toolbox`; in `toolbox` it MUST publish the state of `runtime.exec` and, if absent or unknown, `caída` with the reason «estado sin determinar» ("state undetermined") (never `lista` without knowing); in `caída` the row's value MUST carry the reason sanitized, redacted and bounded to 160 characters. `renderBar` MUST add the segment `exec os` or `exec toolbox <state>` (without the reason) with and without rail, with priority immediately after guard and mode. Each shell's row MUST state its place: with no addition in `os` or with the place absent, ` · toolbox` in toolbox, ` · lugar ?` ("place ?") if the place is malformed (`validPlace`); `registerShell` MUST keep `place`. `parsePanelState` MUST accept a file without `exec` and reject one whose `exec` is not `{mode: os|toolbox, state, reason: string}` with `state` null if and only if the mode is `os`. `PanelIo.execMode` MUST be optional: without it the published state carries no `exec`; `panelTick` with `hasUI=false` MUST NOT invoke it. The rail process (`toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs`) MUST NOT have any path that changes the mode, writes the `exec` file or invokes `docker`.
- **WHEN** `buildPanelState` without `execMode`, with an unknown one and with `null`, with `os` and with `toolbox` in each state, with `toolbox` without a state and with a hostile reason (escapes, `#[bg=red]`, line breaks, 300 characters and `password=Winter2026`) drawn at 36 columns; `renderBar` with and without rail and at 24 and 40 columns; `parsePanelState` over each valid form and over nine invalid forms of `exec`; `registerShell` with place `os`, `toolbox` with ID, `toolbox` with a name instead of the ID and without a place, and the resulting SHELLS card; `validPlace` with ten values; `panelTick` with an `io` without `execMode` and with one that carries it (with and without UI); the text of `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` passed through the contract (`railViolations`) and fourteen doctored copies that add a path to it (importing `child_process`, a workspace module, another node module or a disallowed `fs` function; dynamic `require` or `import()`; writing `exec` directly or through a variable, writing, renaming or deleting another file; naming `docker` or `GEKKO_EXEC`; moving the heartbeat path); and, in `tools/tui-test.sh`, the real rail with `os`, `toolbox · lista` and `toolbox · caída` and a click and keys on the row.
- **THEN** without `execMode` there is no `exec`, row or segment and the state is the former one; the row says `os`, `toolbox · lista`, `toolbox · levantando` or `toolbox · caída · <reason>`; `toolbox` without a state says `caída · estado sin determinar`; the row is added without moving the `guard` row; the bar carries the segment without the reason and yields to guard and mode; the hostile reason comes out without controls or `#[`, without `Winter2026` and trimmed to the width; the SHELLS card says `open`, `open`, `open · toolbox` and `open · lugar ?`; the reader accepts a file without `exec` and rejects the nine forms; the contract gives no violations on the real text and gives at least one for each doctored copy; the real rail shows the three rows, without escapes or more width than the pane, and after the click and the keys the rail, `panel.json` and `scope.txt` do not change and no mode file appears.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `buildPanelState` (`execView`), `PanelExtras.execMode`, `PanelIo.execMode`, `PanelState.exec`, `parsePanelState`, `renderBar`, `registerShell`, `Shell.place`, `validPlace`, `ShellPlace`, `PanelRuntime.exec`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `publish` (`io.execMode`); `toolbox/gekko-panel-selftest.ts` `railViolations`; `tools/tui-test.sh`
- Limit: pure functions, a text contract and a process test with real tmux (`tools/tui-test.sh`, outside the gate). The contract over the rail is a regex over the text without comments: it does not see a dynamically assembled path (concatenated strings, `eval`); its real guarantee is that the rail only imports `fs`, `path` and `gekko-panel.ts`, and none writes `exec` or calls docker. «No path» holds for that process, not for the `exec` file or for docker in general: the model has `bash` on the host (spec 014 §6). `PanelRuntime.exec` is written by the guard (REQ-TBX-009, REQ-TBX-010); here the gate proves its initial value (`{ state: null, reason: "" }`) and that an old singleton without the field is read without throwing. The guard's `io.execMode` (`parseExecMode(GEKKO_EXEC)`, reread on every tick) is covered by the two cases of this requirement with the real guard mounted: the published `panel.json` carries `exec` with mode and state in `toolbox`/`lista` and `exec.mode` `os` after going back to `os`; `session_start` does not reset `runtime.exec` (REQ-TBX-009). The reason comes from docker: `panel.json` can be rewritten by whoever has `bash` on the host (REQ-TUI-020). A `caída` with a long reason is trimmed with `…` at 36 columns: the whole reason is not seen in the rail. No case: the rail with a hostile `place` inside a SHELLS row (it goes through the same `row()` as the rest, REQ-TUI-020).

### 3.12 Workspace (WS)

### REQ-WS-001 — Valid workspace name confined to `engagements/`
`validWorkspaceName` MUST accept only `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,30}` without `..` and different from `none`; `confined` MUST return false when the workspace's real path does not fall inside the real path of `<root>/engagements/`.
- **WHEN** valid names of 1 and 31 characters; names with `/`, `..`, space, leading dot, leading hyphen, 32 characters, empty and `none`; `confined` with a double `realpath` that returns one path inside and another outside (a symbolic link that escapes).
- **THEN** only the first ones are valid; `confined` is true inside and false outside.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `validWorkspaceName`, `workspaceDir`, `confined`
- Limit: pure function. The cap of 31 exists because `redactSummary` masks runs of 32+ characters in the panel. The confinement matters because the firewall treats what lives under `engagements/` as untrusted (`touchesLoot`).

### REQ-WS-002 — The launcher only writes targets the broker counts
`parseTargets` MUST accept IPv4 and CIDR, MUST keep as a label the non-IP token that follows an IP, MUST return as a name to resolve a non-IP token with no IP before it, and MUST reject `IP:port`, IPv6, octets >255 and octets with a leading zero. Every non-comment line of `scopeFileText` and of `replaceScopeText` MUST be accepted by the broker's `parseScopeLine`.
- **WHEN** `parseTargets` with IPs and CIDRs separated by space and by comma, `IP label`, a bare name, `IP:port`, an IPv6, `300.1.1.1` and `010.0.0.1`; and each entry of `scopeEntries(scopeFileText(…))` and of `scopeEntries(replaceScopeText(…))` is passed to the broker's real rule.
- **THEN** the expected targets, labels, names and rejections; all the written entries are accepted by the broker.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `parseTargets`, `scopeLine`, `scopeFileText`, `scopeEntries`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `parseScopeLine`
- Limit: the broker understands neither ports nor names (GAP-12): an authorized `host:port` target is written as an IP and the broker covers the whole IP.

### REQ-WS-003 — A workspace's scaffolding never overwrites
Creation MUST produce `scope.txt`, `notes.md`, `AUTHORIZATION.md`, `loot/creds.md`, `scripts/` and `canaries/`, and MUST NOT modify a file that already exists.
- **WHEN** `scaffoldPlan` for a name and two targets; and the CLI `new` over a temporary directory that already contains `notes.md` and `scope.txt` with their own content.
- **THEN** the plan lists the six paths; after `new`, the two previous files keep their content byte for byte and the ones that were missing exist.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `scaffoldPlan`, CLI `new`
- Limit: it does not verify permissions or owner of what is created. `AUTHORIZATION.md` is created as a template: nobody checks that it is filled in, nor the testing window.

### REQ-WS-004 — Replacing the scope keeps the previous entries as a dated comment
`replaceScopeText` MUST leave each previous entry as a comment line that includes the date and the original text, MUST keep the previous comments, and MUST add the new entries.
- **WHEN** a `scope.txt` with two entries and a comment is replaced by a new entry with date `2026-11-14`.
- **THEN** `scopeEntries` of the result is only the new entry; the text contains the two previous ones commented with `2026-11-14` and the original comment.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `replaceScopeText`, `scopeEntries`
- Limit: pure function; the replacement happens before starting Pi (a restart loads the file as-is, REQ-SCP-002), it does not go through the confirmation of hosts added live.

### REQ-WS-005 — Menu: last used by default, only real workspaces, name resolved only with confirmation
`pickFlow` MUST choose the last used with an empty line, MUST NOT offer it if it is no longer in the list, MUST create a workspace only with a valid name, MUST write the IP of a resolved name only if the operator confirms, and MUST return null (creating nothing) if the input runs out.
- **WHEN** `pickFlow` with a double `io`: (a) empty line with the last used present; (b) last used absent from the list; (c) `n`, an invalid name, a valid one and targets; (d) a host name that the double resolves, answered with `n` and then with `s`; (e) end of input; (f) choosing an existing one and replacing its scope; (g) `liveShells` returns null.
- **THEN** (a) returns the last used; (b) does not choose it by default and asks again; (c) rejects the invalid one and calls `create` once with the valid one; (d) without confirmation `create` does not receive that IP, with confirmation it does; (e) null and zero calls to `create`; (f) `replaceScope` once; (g) the menu is drawn without a count.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `pickFlow`, `PickIo`
- Limit: pure flow with injected I/O. Listing only directories with `scope.txt`, counting live shells by `#{session_path}` and resolving through the system live in the CLI's real `io`, covered by `tools/ws-test.sh`, not by the gate.

### REQ-WS-006 — A shell is born in the workspace or is not born
`newSessionArgs` without a workspace MUST return `new-session -d -s <name> -x 220 -y 50 -e IGNOREEOF=10` and with a workspace MUST add `-c <dir>` at the end; `cwdVerdict` MUST fail when the directory tmux reports is not the workspace's real path.
- **WHEN** `newSessionArgs("x", undefined)` and `newSessionArgs("x", "/ws")`; `cwdVerdict` with an equal path, with a different path (`$HOME`), and without a workspace.
- **THEN** `["new-session","-d","-s","x","-x","220","-y","50","-e","IGNOREEOF=10"]` and the same plus `-c /ws`; verdict ok, not ok, and ok.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `newSessionArgs`, `cwdVerdict`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_new`
- Limit: pure functions. That `session_new` uses them, checks the directory beforehand and kills the session if the verdict fails is proven by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` (real tmux), outside the gate (GAP-27): a deleted directory, without permission and a regular file give FAIL without a session; the killing branch is exercised with an intermediary `tmux` that strips the `-c` from `new-session` (deterministic injection); and two simultaneous calls with the workspace changed between one and the other are each born in their own (the variable is read on entering the call, before the first wait). A `.bashrc` that does a `cd` makes every creation with a workspace fail. It only affects `session_*`: the `bash` tool stays in Pi's directory.

### REQ-WS-007 — The launcher does not start Pi without a workspace and derives scope, findings and sessions from it
`tools/pi-hitl.sh` MUST resolve the workspace before any Pi start, MUST fail without an interactive terminal when `GEKKO_WORKSPACE` is not defined, MUST export `GEKKO_WORKSPACE`, `GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR`, `GEKKO_SCOPE`, `GEKKO_FINDINGS` and `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR` from the workspace, and MUST relaunch itself in tmux without the server capturing the workspace in its environment.
- **WHEN** the selftest reads `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and applies regular expressions over its text.
- **THEN** the invocation of `gekko-workspace.ts` appears before the first execution of `pi`; the error message naming `GEKKO_WORKSPACE`, the five exports and the `env -u GEKKO_WORKSPACE` of the relaunch exist.
- Code: `tools/pi-hitl.sh`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` CLI
- Limit: only regex over text (like REQ-PNL-005, GAP-36). The real behavior —menu, error without a terminal, the variables Pi receives, old value in the tmux server, warning of an overridden `GEKKO_SCOPE`, banner— is proven by `tools/ws-test.sh` with a fake `pi`, outside the gate. With `none` an old `GEKKO_SCOPE` inherited from the tmux server can still slip in. The other launchers (the autonomous experiment runner (not included in Lite)) do not use a workspace.

### REQ-WS-008 — The model's prompt names the real workspace and how to pass it to a subagent
`workspacePromptBlock` with a workspace MUST include the absolute path, that the `session_*` shells are born there, that `bash` runs at the repo root, that another tree must not be created, the name of the variable `GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR` and the order to pass the path when delegating to a subagent; without a workspace it MUST NOT name any path.
- **WHEN** `workspacePromptBlock("acme", "/r/engagements/acme")` and `workspacePromptBlock(null, null)`.
- **THEN** the first contains the path, `GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR`, and the mentions of `session_`, `bash` and subagents; the second does not contain `engagements/`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `workspacePromptBlock`; `tools/pi-hitl.sh` (generated prompt); `docs/prompts/pi-hitl.md`
- Limit: it is text for the model: it does not compel. Subagents inherit the environment because `@tintinweb/pi-subagents` 0.23.2 runs them inside Pi's process; if a future version moves them to another process, that inheritance must be proven again.

### REQ-WS-009 — The panel state belongs to a launch, not to a workspace
`tools/pi-hitl.sh` MUST publish the panel state in a file of its own per launch (the launcher's pid at the end of the name: `panel.<workspace>.<pid>.json`, or `panel.<pid>.json` with `none`), MUST delete on launch the state files whose pid no longer lives, and `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` MUST pass the path of that file inside the `window-resized` hook command.
- **WHEN** the selftest reads `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh` and applies regular expressions over their text.
- **THEN** the two `panel_default` assignments carry `$$` before `.json`, the cleanup `kill -0 "$pid" … || rm -f` exists, and the `set-hook … window-resized` includes `GEKKO_PANEL_STATE='$GEKKO_PANEL_STATE'`; removing any of the three things makes the contract fail.
- Code: `tools/pi-hitl.sh`; `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh`
- Limit: only regex over text (GAP-36). The behavior —two live Pis over the same workspace receive different paths, a dead pid's file is deleted and a live one's is not, the rail reopened by a resize reads its Pi's file— is proven by `tools/ws-test.sh` with a fake `pi`, outside the gate. A `GEKKO_PANEL_STATE` already present in the environment still takes precedence: two launches that inherit it share a file again. The pid is recycled: a dead launch's file survives while another process uses that pid. The folding of the cards (`panel.ui.json`) is still one per directory, shared between rails. Origin: a defect seen on 2026-11-15 (two Pis over the same workspace; one's rail showed `AGRESIVO` and `SHELLS 0` of the other).

### REQ-WS-010 — The shell is born with `IGNOREEOF=10`
`newSessionArgs` MUST include exactly one `-e IGNOREEOF=10` with and without a workspace, so that a reflexive `Ctrl+D` at the prompt does not close the agent's shell.
- **WHEN** `newSessionArgs("x", undefined)` and `newSessionArgs("x", "/ws")`.
- **THEN** both carry a single `-e` followed by `IGNOREEOF=10`, and keep `-d` and `-s x`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `newSessionArgs`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_new`
- Limit: pure function. That the shell survives a `Ctrl+D` is proven by `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` with real tmux, outside the gate (GAP-27). `IGNOREEOF` is only read by bash (zsh does not; unverified which shell the toolbox image uses) and does not protect a program running inside nor a typed `exit`. Each shell's `mouse on` is switched on by the `session_new` wiring (spec 011 §9), not by `newSessionArgs`, and has no case in the gate.

### REQ-WS-011 — A workspace whose directory is a symbolic link is refused
`checkWorkspace` and `createWorkspace` (and with them `check`, `mode`, `scope`, `prompt`, `toolbox`, `touch`, `new` and the menu) MUST refuse a workspace whose directory —its last component— is a symbolic link (`lstat`, live or dangling), before reading `scope.txt`, creating anything and touching a container, with a message that says to replace the link with the real directory. A link in `engagements/` or higher up MUST keep being accepted.
- **WHEN** `engagements/alfa -> beta` (a real workspace) goes through each CLI command and through `new` with and without `--exec`; a dangling link goes through `new`; `engagements` itself is a link to another real tree and `check` and `mode` are requested.
- **THEN** each command exits ≠ 0 with `enlace simbólico` ("symbolic link") and `directorio real` ("real directory"), without output and without creating or modifying anything (the target workspace keeps its content, the dangling link's target is not created); with the link in `engagements/` they exit 0.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `refuseLinkedWorkspace`, `checkWorkspace`, `scaffoldInto`; `tools/pi-hitl.sh` (uses `check` before Pi); `tools/ws-test.sh`
- Limit: without this `alfa -> beta` passed beta's scope, mode and container off as alfa's. `lstat` and the later use are not atomic: a link placed between the two is not detected. The link inside the workspace (`scope.txt`, `exec`) is covered by REQ-TBX-001. An existing workspace that is a link stops starting: the operator replaces it with the real directory.

### REQ-WS-012 — The launcher warns on exit if the container is still running
`tools/pi-hitl.sh` MUST print `[pi-hitl] contenedor activo: gekko-tb-<ws>; apagar: docker rm -f gekko-tb-<ws>` ("[pi-hitl] container active: gekko-tb-<ws>; shut down: docker rm -f gekko-tb-<ws>") when Pi ends, inside and outside tmux, when `docker inspect` says that container is running at that moment (not according to the launch's mode: it can be brought up with Pi open). There MUST be ONE exit path per branch (inside tmux, `cleanup_after_pi` prints the notice at the start, before silencing the output; outside, `trap … EXIT`), it MUST NOT replace the process with Pi via `exec`, MUST keep Pi's exit code, and the query MUST go behind `command -v docker` and `timeout`, with no query for no workspace (`none`). The banner MUST say that the mode it shows is that of the launch (`shells    os (al lanzar)`, "at launch").
- **WHEN** the selftest reads `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and applies regular expressions over its text, mutating one clause at a time; and `tools/ws-test.sh` launches with a fake `docker` that says running / stopped / absent, and without a `docker` binary, inside tmux and outside, with a fake `pi` that exits with code 7, and with a workspace saved in `toolbox` without asking.
- **THEN** the text contract is met and each mutation makes it fail; the notice comes out (with the container name) only when it is running, also if the launch's mode was `os`; without docker nothing comes out and it does not hang; Pi's code arrives intact; the banner says `(al lanzar)`.
- Code: `tools/pi-hitl.sh` `notify_running_toolbox`, `cleanup_after_pi`, banner
- Limit: only regex over text in the gate (GAP-36); the behavior is proven by `tools/ws-test.sh`, outside the gate. Inside tmux the exits by HUP/TERM/INT also warn (they go through the trap); outside tmux only Pi's normal exit triggers the notice, not a TERM to the launcher (as already happened in toolbox). The notice is information: it shuts nothing down. `docker inspect` with the daemon down prints nothing (indistinguishable from «not there»). A TERM signal to the launcher's pid outside tmux no longer reaches Pi (before, `exec` made it the same process).

### 3.13 Pi configuration written by `setup.sh` (CFG)

### REQ-CFG-001 — `setup.sh` leaves a single provider `provider` with the model `GEKKO_MODEL`, 256k context and 32k maxTokens
`setup.sh` MUST require the model id (`GEKKO_MODEL` exported, saved or typed): without it MUST exit with code 1 without writing Pi's config. With it MUST create the provider `provider` in `models.json` (key `${GEKKO_PROVIDER_KEY}`), write in `modealias.json` the alias `sub-128k` with destination `provider/<model>`, and write `GEKKO_PROVIDER="provider"` and `GEKKO_PROVIDER_KEY` in `.gekko-state/env` (the own key if it exists; if not, the same `GEKKO_LLM_KEY`). The chosen model MUST end up with `contextWindow` 262144 and `maxTokens` 32768 in `models.json` and in the alias.
- **WHEN** the selftest copies `setup.sh`, `toolbox/models.json` and `toolbox/scope.txt.example` to a temporary repo, saves in its `.gekko-state/env` a URL, a key and —depending on the case— the model `m-test` and/or `GEKKO_PROVIDER_KEY`, and runs `/bin/bash setup.sh` with a temporary `HOME` and a `PATH` that only has fake scripts `node`, `npm`, `git`, `tmux` and `pi` and links to `python3`, `dirname`, `grep`, `head`, `sed`, `mkdir`, `cat` and `chmod`, with stdin that answers empty to the model prompt and `n` to the Herdr one.
- **THEN** with the model saved it exits with code 0, the alias is `provider/m-test`, `models.json` has the provider `provider` with `m-test` at 262144/32768, the alias carries 262144/32768, and `.gekko-state/env` carries `GEKKO_PROVIDER="provider"` and the provider's key (the own one, or `GEKKO_LLM_KEY` if there is none); without a model it exits with code 1 and neither `models.json` nor `modealias.json` exist.
- Code: `setup.sh` (the `python3` invocation that writes `models.json`, `modealias.json` and `subagents.json`)
- Limit: it does not decide what the model can do, but with which provider and key the subagents authenticate. The other models of the catalog keep 131072/24576 and the id `sub-128k` is kept even though the window is 256k. It does not prove the server holds 256k or 32k of output (VRAM). Only "saved" and "absent" have a case; "exported in the environment" and "typed at the prompt" do not. It does not prove the rest of `models.json` (`subagents.json`: REQ-CFG-002), nor that the key is valid. An already written `modealias.json` is not corrected until `setup.sh` runs again. Without `curl` or `herdr` in the `PATH`: the Provider probe and the Herdr integration are not exercised. The case depends on the host's `/bin/bash`, `sh`, `python3` and linked utilities.

### REQ-CFG-002 — `setup.sh` leaves Pi's subagents one at a time, not nested and only in foreground
`setup.sh` MUST write to `subagents.json` `maxConcurrent: 1`, `maxConcurrentForeground: 1`, `maxSubagentDepth: 0` and `backgroundByDefault: false`, overriding the previous value of those four keys and keeping the file's other keys.
- **WHEN** the selftest runs `setup.sh` as in REQ-CFG-001, in one case without a previous `subagents.json` (with the model and the key saved) and in the other with one that carries `backgroundByDefault: true` and a foreign key `otro: 1`.
- **THEN** the two resulting files have the four keys with those values, and the second keeps `otro: 1`.
- Code: `setup.sh` (`python3` block, `subagents.update(...)`)
- Limit: it is configuration read by `@tintinweb/pi-subagents`: it does not prove that the extension respects it, nor the `run_in_background: false` in the frontmatter of `.pi/agents/*.md`, nor the repo's `.pi/subagents.json` (written by hand, with no case). `backgroundByDefault` only covers the types without an agent file, according to `.pi/agents/README.md`; it is not verified against the extension. An already written `subagents.json` is not corrected until `setup.sh` runs again. Same host dependencies as REQ-CFG-001.

### 3.14 Findings ledger (LED)

Cases in `python3 .claude/tools/findings.py selftest` (lines `ok REQ-LED-…`, §2a). All run over ledgers
in temporary directories.

### REQ-LED-001 — Narrative and classification fields: optional, with validated format and no write on an invalid value
`add` and `set` MUST accept `summary`, `impact`, `remediation`, `cvss`, `cwe` and `cve` and store them in the row only when passed. `cvss` MUST be a number from 0.0 to 10.0, `cwe` MUST have the form `CWE-<digits>` and `cve` the form `CVE-<4 digits>-<4 or more digits>`; on a value that does not comply, the command MUST exit ≠ 0 with a message that names the field and MUST leave the ledger byte for byte the same. A row without those fields MUST keep working in `list`, `render` and `set`. Since 019, `cvss_vector` and `cvss_justification` go through the same single rule `validate_field` (REQ-LED-008) used by the CLI, the chat and the web editing.
- **WHEN** `add` with the six fields and `set` that changes each one; `add` and `set` with none; and, for each validated field, values out of form or range, comparing the ledger's sha256 before and after.
- **THEN** the row carries the values (`cvss` as a number) and only the keys passed; each invalid value exits ≠ 0 naming its field and the sha256 does not change.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `cmd_add`, `cmd_set`, `validate_field`, `NARRATIVE_FIELDS`
- Limit: it validates the form, not that the value is true (a nonexistent CWE or CVE passes; the CVSS is whatever the agent wrote). The validation of `summary`, `impact`, `remediation`, `cwe` and `cve` lives in `validate_field`, which the report module also uses (REQ-RPT-031/032), so the web and the CLI accept and reject the same thing. Through the CLI an already written field cannot be deleted, only replaced; only `set_row(drop=)` (the chat's «undo», REQ-LED-007) removes a narrative key. A row written by hand in the JSONL skips the validation.

### REQ-LED-002 — `render`: confirmed and corroborated by default, six fields, two languages and fenced values
`render` MUST project by default only `confirmed` rows whose `corroboration` field is text with some visible character (the same `_blank` rule as `set`: Cc controls and Cf format characters, e.g. U+200B, count as nothing); with `--all` it MUST include all rows. It MUST show the six fields of REQ-LED-001, fence any value with backticks or line breaks with a fence longer than any run of backticks, and write on one line any value outside a fence. `id`, `title`, `severity`, `status`, `target`, each `repro` step and the `kb` list MUST each come out as a single inline code span, with a backtick run longer than any in the value and a padding space if the value is empty or starts or ends with a backtick, so that outside those spans the Markdown does not change with the value. The report (Markdown, web, `.html`, print view) MUST NOT show the `blocker` of a `confirmed` row; the ledger keeps it. With `cvss_vector` and `cvss_justification` present, it MUST show them right after `cvss`, each as a code span, in both languages (eight fields in total). `--lang en|es` MUST translate only the labels through `LABELS`, leaving values, severity tokens and status intact; the default MUST be `en`. Without `--tipo`, `render` produces `render_md`. `render --tipo <name> [--borrador F]` MUST resolve a valid built-in or added template, validate the draft against its keys and produce exactly `render_report` (REQ-RPT-046/047/049); `--tipo` is not limited to a fixed list. An unknown name, an invalid draft, `--borrador` without `--tipo` or `--all` with `--tipo` MUST exit ≠ 0 without printing a report. `validate_draft` requires a JSON object, offered keys, text of up to 4096 bytes per field and one line in single-line fields.
- **WHEN** `render` and `render --all` over varied states, including two confirmed rows without useful corroboration; a finding with the six fields, evidence with backticks/line breaks and hostile values in title/steps/KB; the same hostile value in the seven inline fields, in turn: HTML tag with a remote URL, image, `javascript:` link, `<https://…>` autolink, forged bold/emphasis, a run of backticks, backticks at the edge, trailing `\`, leading `#`, `|` and empty; distinct and contiguous hostile values in `severity` (`` `x ``) and `status` (``a `` <img …>``); a confirmed row with a corroboration of only U+200B; a `needs_validation` row with a blocker moved to `confirmed`; and both language options plus an unknown language.
- **THEN** only the corroborated confirmed rows appear by default, `--all` includes the other rows, the EN/ES outputs differ only in labels, the forged title creates no headings, the fences contain the value, the U+200B-only corroboration excludes the row and `set` rejects it, the confirmed row does not show its blocker in any language but the ledger keeps it, with the code spans removed (also with contiguous hostile values in different fields) the hostile report is the same skeleton as with a neutral value and carries no `<`, `![`, `](`, `\`, `_` or emphasis, each value remains legible, and an unknown language fails without printing a report.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `included`, `report_fields`, `render_md`, `render_report`, `validate_draft`, `LABELS`, `cmd_render`, `_field`, `_fence`, `_code` (the case's oracle is `_CODE_SPAN`; `_code`'s padding was tested by mutating it to «only if the value is empty»: the contiguous-fields case fails)
- Limit: a non-empty `corroboration` does not prove that a second independent signal exists.
