The span oracle is a regex of the CommonMark rule, not a parser: with `marked` (GFM) it was checked by hand that no `<a>`, `<img>` or bold comes out of those values and that URLs inside a span are not linked; that same `marked` loses the bold of the next label after a span with backticks or with a trailing `\` (cosmetic). A viewer that does not respect CommonMark code spans may interpret the value. The line breaks handled are `\r`, `\n`, U+000B, U+000C, U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029. The negatives of `validate_draft` name the draft's key with `repr`, so a hand-written file with a hostile key carries no control bytes (ESC, OSC) to stderr (T45-fix B1); `render` still prints raw to stdout the ledger's values and those of the draft (`--borrador`): `validate_draft` only rejects line breaks in single-line fields, not ESC or OSC (verification of 019b). `render --tipo` without `--workspace` puts the ledger folder's name in the eyebrow only if it hangs from `engagements/<codename>/`; if not, none (a client's real name does not reach the deliverable). A case compares each field line of `render_md` with the Technical one.
- UI-r3 (2026-11-17): no report output (plain `render`, `render --tipo`, and the `.md`/`.html`/`.imprimir` and the web sheet) MUST carry the KB reference (`kb`, «KB: …»): the KB is internal and does not reach the client; the ledger keeps the field and the web's Registro tab shows it. `render_report` MUST carry a cover (classification, eyebrow «INFORME TÉCNICO/EJECUTIVO / <workspace>» ("TECHNICAL/EXECUTIVE REPORT / <workspace>"), title, subtitle, Preparado para/por ("Prepared for/by") grid, Período ("Period"), Versión ("Version"), with default values, and the review notice), numbered sections, counts by severity and severity/status in the document's language; the Technical one without a draft is NO LONGER `render_md` (the plain `render_md` keeps its form except for the KB). Cases: «a row with kb set renders no KB line…», cover, sections and localized labels. `render --workspace` sets the eyebrow's name (by default, the ledger's folder).
- Spec 024: `render`/`render --all` (`render_md`) also project the `remediated` rows with corroboration and note, after the open ones and under «Remediated (N)»/«Remediados (N)», with «Retest»/«Nueva prueba»; the `blocker` of a remediated row does not come out; without `remediated` rows the baseline stays byte for byte (REQ-LED-011).

### REQ-LED-003 — `confirmed` requires corroboration, `needs_validation` requires a blocker, and `add` accepts neither
`set` MUST refuse to move a row to `confirmed` without `corroboration` and to `needs_validation` without `blocker`; a blank text (only spaces) MUST count as absent; the refusal MUST NOT write anything, nor the new fields the same call carries. `add` MUST refuse to create a row in `confirmed`, `needs_validation` or `remediated` (spec 024: the `remediated` rule is REQ-LED-011).
- **WHEN** `set --status confirmed` without corroboration and `set --status needs_validation` without a blocker, alone, accompanied by fields of REQ-LED-001 and with a one-space corroboration or blocker; the same `set` with their corroboration or blocker; and `add` with each of those two statuses.
- **THEN** the refusals exit ≠ 0 and the row stays as it was (status and fields); with corroboration or blocker the status changes and the text stays in the row; `add` exits ≠ 0 and the ledger gains no rows.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `cmd_set`, `cmd_add`, `_blank`
- Limit: the corroboration is non-empty text: it is not checked that it describes a second independent signal. A hand-written row can say `confirmed` without corroboration; the report module flags it (REQ-RPT-004) with the same `_blank` rule, the ledger does not prevent it. The web uses `set_row` and shares these same rules; it does not make them stricter.

### REQ-LED-011 — `remediated`: only from `confirmed`, with a retest note, and apart in the report
`set` MUST refuse (writing nothing) to move a row to `remediated` from a status that is not `confirmed` or `remediated`, without `corroboration` or, on entering, without a non-blank `retest` in that same call (a note from a previous cycle does not count); `add` MUST refuse to create a row in `remediated`; `remediated` → `confirmed` MUST be valid and `remediated` → `remediated` without `retest` MUST keep the note; `retest` MUST be written only if the target status is `remediated`. `included` MUST take the remediated rows with corroboration and note after the open ones; the count by severity MUST count only the open ones; without `remediated` rows every output MUST stay byte for byte the same.
- **WHEN** `set --status remediated` from corroborated `confirmed` with and without a note (also blank), from `triaged`/`hypothesis`, without corroboration; the same status over `remediated`; `set --status confirmed` over a remediated row; `add --status remediated`; `list --status remediated`; the real CLI with `--retest`.
- **THEN** the refusals exit ≠ 0 naming the rule and the ledger's sha256 does not change; with a note the status changes and the note stays; going back to `confirmed` leaves the note as history; `add` gains no rows; `list` filters.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `STATUSES`, `set_row`, `cmd_set`, `cmd_add`, `included`, `report_fields`, `render_md`, `report_model`
- Limit: the note is text: it does not prove the retest happened; a single note per row (re-entering `remediated` replaces it); a model can call `findings.py set` (`ledgerCliOnly`) with the same rules as for `confirmed`.

### REQ-LED-004 — Corrupt ledger: a line that is not a UTF-8 JSON object stops `list`, `render`, `add` and `set`
The reader (`_scan`) MUST split the file only on `\n` breaks and classify as bad, with its line number, every non-empty line that does not decode as UTF-8, is not JSON, is JSON that is not an object, carries an integer exceeding the digit limit or a nesting the parser rejects, uses the constants `NaN`, `Infinity` or `-Infinity`, or a number that overflows to infinity (`1e400`); it MUST NOT throw on any line. `list`, `render`, `add` and `set` MUST exit ≠ 0 with a message containing `corrupt ledger` and that number, without a traceback, and MUST NOT write.
- **WHEN** a ledger with one valid row followed by, in turn: text that is not JSON, `42`, `[]`, `null`, `"x"`, a line with non-UTF-8 bytes, a 5000-digit integer, a 100000-level nesting, `{"status":"confirmed","cvss":NaN}`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity` and `{"id":"F-2","cvss":1e400}` (with a `set` over another row, which rewrites nothing); each of the four commands over it; and a file with a stray `\r` between two objects.
- **THEN** each command exits with `corrupt ledger` and the bad line's number; the file does not change; the stray `\r` does not split the line.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `_scan`, `_scan_bytes`, `_finite_float`, `_load`
- Limit: a single bad line leaves the ledger without additions until it is repaired by hand; the report module keeps showing the other rows and warns about it (REQ-RPT-004). A valid JSON object with wrong types (numeric title, `evidence` as text) is not a bad line. The message says `not a JSON object` for all the reasons.
### REQ-LED-005 — Simultaneous additions: 20 `add` processes leave 20 rows with 20 distinct ids
`add` and `set` MUST take an exclusive lock before reading the ledger and release it after writing it.
- **WHEN** the selftest launches 20 real `add` processes at once over a temporary ledger and waits for all of them.
- **THEN** the ledger has 20 rows with 20 distinct ids.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `_locked`, `cmd_add`, `cmd_set`
- Limit: the lock is `flock` over the ledger's directory: Linux only and only between processes that use `findings.py`; whoever writes the file on their own does not respect it. Proven on btrfs and tmpfs, not on a network file system. Without the lock the same test left 17 rows.

### REQ-LED-006 — Row integrity: ids, duplicate id, partial or wrong-typed rows and saving that does not follow links
`add` MUST assign the id `F-<n+1>` starting from the highest numeric id present. `set` MUST refuse when the requested id appears zero or more than one time. `list` and `render` MUST NOT fail on partial rows or on rows with wrong types. Saving MUST NOT leave the temporary file, MUST NOT follow a symbolic link planted at its path and MUST NOT fail on a lone surrogate: it writes it as a JSON escape and reads it back the same. A new ledger MUST be born with mode `0660` when its folder has a default ACL (the toolbox workspace; approved by the operator on 2026-11-17 when merging 013/014 with 016: with `0600` the workspace ACL's mask stays at `---` and the host cannot read a ledger the container created) and with `0600` in any other folder, whatever the umask (bounded after finding 4 of the 013/014 review); an existing ledger keeps its mode (REQ-LED-007). Every CLI read (`add`, `set`, `list`, `render`) MUST go through `_read_regular` with the cap `LEDGER_MAX_BYTES` (64 MiB): a `findings.jsonl` that is a symbolic link or a FIFO MUST NOT be followed, read or block (`not_regular`), and one over the cap is refused (`too_large`); in toolbox the container's root can plant either of the two and the CLI runs on the host.
- **WHEN** two `add` in a row; non-numeric ids (`F-`, `F-x`) next to `F-0007`; a ledger with the same id in two rows and a `set` over it; `list` and `render --all` with a row that carries only `id` and with a wrong-typed row (`id` null, title a list, `evidence` text, `repro` text, `kb` with numbers and objects, severity non-text); the existence of `<ledger>.tmp` after saving; a symbolic link planted at `<ledger>.tmp` toward a victim file followed by an `add`; and an `add` whose title carries a lone surrogate; a new ledger saved with umask `022` and with umask `077`, in a folder without ACL and in one with a default ACL (`setfacl -m d:u:<uid>:rwx`); `add`, `list` and `render` over a `findings.jsonl` that is a link to another engagement's JSONL, a FIFO and a file over the cap (lowered to 64 bytes in the case).
- **THEN** ids `F-0001` and `F-0002`; the next id is `F-0008`; the `set` exits ≠ 0; `list` and `render` do not throw; no `.tmp` remains; the victim file keeps its bytes and the ledger has the row; the `add` exits 0, `_scan` returns the same title and `list` and `render --all` do not throw; the new ledger ends up `0600` without ACL and `0660` with a default ACL, with both umasks; the three verbs refuse (`not_regular` for link and FIFO, `too_large` over the cap) without blocking, without printing the foreign content and without touching the link or its target.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `_next_id`, `cmd_set`, `cmd_list`, `cmd_render`, `_save`, `_default_acl`, `_scan`, `main`
- Limit: the gate pins the mode, not the ACL: without `setfacl` or on a file system without ACL the ACL case prints `skip … NOT checked`. A ledger born `0600` before this change keeps its mode (REQ-LED-007), so an old one in a toolbox workspace still has no mask until the operator changes it by hand. The detection only looks at whether some default ACL exists on the folder, not which users it names. That the host reads and writes the ledger the container created is proven by `tools/toolbox-test.sh` with real Docker. `0660` lets the file's owning group read and write (on the host, the user's private group; inside the container, `root`). The atomic saving is not tested against a real cut midway through a write; a process dead between creating the temporary and renaming it leaves it on disk until the next save. Two contiguous lone surrogates that form a pair are read back as a single character. Saving is only safe with the lock taken (REQ-LED-005).

### REQ-LED-007 — `set_row` compares the row seen before writing
`fingerprint(row)` MUST produce the same SHA-256 for the same JSON object even if the order of its keys changes, and a different one if its content changes. `set_row(..., expect=...)` MUST compare that fingerprint with the row while holding the lock and MUST reject as `stale` without saving if it differs. `set_row(..., check=...)` MUST execute `check(row)` with the lock held and, if it throws `Refusal`, MUST NOT save anything (the band and score rule of REQ-LED-008). `cmd_set` MUST keep the current messages and exit code. `set_row(..., drop=...)` MUST require `expect` and that `drop` be a subset of the narrative fields (`summary`, `impact`, `remediation`, `cwe`, `cve`; if not, `Refusal("invalid")` before touching the file) and MUST remove those keys from the row when saving it. `set_fields(...)` MUST validate each change with `validate_field`, accept only fields from the `allowed` list that are also narrative, and resolve the row's id by its physical line (`not_unique` if it does not exist or repeats) before calling `set_row`. With `max_bytes` (the report module's call, REQ-RPT-008) `set_row` MUST read the ledger without following a symbolic link, without blocking on a FIFO and with `O_NOCTTY` (a terminal node is not adopted as the controlling terminal; cases with a recorded `open`) and MUST reject without saving a ledger that is not a regular file (`not_regular`) or that exceeds `max_bytes` (`too_large`).
- **WHEN** the same row with keys in a different order; a row with a changed value; `set_row` with an old fingerprint after changing a row; the refusals for corroboration, blocker, non-unique id and corrupt ledger, also through the `set` command; `set_row(..., max_bytes=100)` over a link to a file outside, over a FIFO and over a ledger of more than 100 bytes; `drop` without `expect`, with `status`, `id` or an unknown field; `set_fields` with fields outside `allowed`, with an invalid value and over a nonexistent line; and `web_open`/`web_unlink` over a directory, a file and a component that are symbolic links.
- **THEN** the fingerprints are equal only for the same row; the stale row and the other refusals throw `Refusal` with their `kind` and change no bytes; the `set` command keeps exit 1 and the existing refusal text; invalid uses of `drop` and `set_fields` throw `Refusal("invalid")` or `not_unique` without changing bytes; `web_open` creates 0700 directories and 0600 files and rejects (`not_regular`) every link without following it; the three with `max_bytes` throw `not_regular`, `not_regular` and `too_large` in under 2 s, the link remains a link and neither its target nor the large ledger change.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `fingerprint`, `set_row`, `set_fields`, `web_open`, `web_unlink`, `Refusal`, `cmd_set`, `_load`, `_scan`, `_read_regular`
- Limit: the fingerprint does not detect two writes that leave exactly the same row (including `updated` equal to the second); that second submission repeats the same bytes. `set_row` rewrites the whole file under the lock: any writer that does not go through `findings.py` (a manual `>>`, an editor) can lose a line; `findings.py` is the only valid writer. Saving keeps the mode (permissions) of the existing ledger; a text without visible characters (Cc controls, Cf format) counts as empty for corroboration and blocker. Without `max_bytes` (the `set` command and the other CLI ones) the read does not change: it follows a symbolic link, blocks on a FIFO and has no size limit.

### REQ-LED-008 — CVSS v3.1: `findings.py` calculates the score and the severity does not write itself
`cvss_vector` MUST accept only base CVSS:3.1 with the eight metrics (AV, AC, PR, UI, S, C, I, A), of at most 100 characters, and be stored in its canonical form; `cvss` MUST be derived from it with FIRST's formula (`roundup` rounding of appendix A) and a `--cvss` that differs from the computed one MUST be rejected, also when it arrives alone and the row already carries a valid vector (it is checked against that vector, under the lock: `cvss` MUST be derived from it). If the score's band does not match `severity`, the change MUST be rejected (`needs_justification`) unless it carries or the row already has `cvss_justification`. The severity MUST NOT be written from the vector.
- **WHEN** the 2592 vectors against a reference in `Decimal`; `roundup` edge cases; vectors of another version, with repeated, missing, lowercase metrics, of 101 characters and with invisibles; `add` and `set` with a vector, with a different `--cvss` and with a band different from the severity, with and without justification; `set --cvss` alone against a row whose vector gives another score, with an equal score and over a row without a vector.
- **THEN** each vector gives the reference's score and the right band; the invalid ones exit ≠ 0 naming `cvss_vector` without changing bytes; without justification the mismatch writes nothing and with it it does; the lone `--cvss` that contradicts the vector exits ≠ 0 naming `cvss` without changing bytes, the equal one and that of a row without a vector pass; `severity` stays as it was.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `cvss31`, `roundup`, `cvss_check`, `validate_field`, `_optional_fields`, `set_row`
- Limit: it validates the form and the calculation, not that the metrics describe the finding. A lone `--cvss` is checked against the vector the row already carries; if that vector does not parse (hand-written row) there is nothing to check against and it passes, and a row without a vector accepts any `--cvss`. An old justification is not deleted when the vector changes. «Never guess the CVSS» remains a rule of the agents' prompts, not of the code.

### REQ-LED-009 — Comments: append-only events file, with a fingerprint chain and no physical deletion
`findings.py` MUST be the only writer of `.gekko-web/comentarios.jsonl` (next to the ledger): `add`/`edit`/`delete` events appended under lock, each with `prev` (sha256 of the previous event of that comment). The file MUST be opened with `web_open` (not following links, 0700 directories, 0600 file, owner verified), MUST NOT exceed 1 MiB and each text MUST fit in 8192 UTF-8 bytes. Deleting MUST be a tombstone; editing or deleting with an old `expect` fingerprint MUST be `stale`. A bad line MUST make every write fail (`corrupt`). Several processes editing the same comment at once MUST leave a single `prev` chain with no bad lines. A finding id that is not encodable as UTF-8 (a lone surrogate) MUST be rejected in `comment_add` as `invalid` without writing or creating anything, and an event of a hand-written file with such a `finding` MUST count as a bad line. `web_open` MUST look at an existing file with a link-not-following `stat` before opening it, so that a planted FIFO or node is not opened (the later `fstat` covers a change in between).
- **WHEN** add/edit/delete, old fingerprint, double submission with the same id, links and FIFO instead of the file, broken lines in four ways, file at the cap, 20 processes commenting at once and 12 editing the same comment (each process pauses after reading, so that without the lock all read the same state; tested by removing the `flock`), an id with a surrogate in the ledger and in a hand-written file, and a FIFO planted with `os.open` recorded.
- **THEN** the folding shows only what is current; the double submission writes once (the same id with another text is `stale`); links and FIFO are rejected without being followed; bad lines and the cap reject without writing; the 20 writes all remain and the file is read whole; the 12 edits leave 13 lines, none bad, and each `prev` is the sha256 of the previous line recomputed separately; the id with a surrogate is `invalid` without creating the directory and in the hand-written file it is bad line 1; the FIFO is rejected (`not_regular`) without `os.open` receiving its name.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `comment_add`, `comment_edit`, `comment_delete`, `read_comments`, `read_comments_state`, `_comment_append`, `_apply_comment_event`, `web_open`, `owner_ok`, `COMMENTS_PARTS`
- Limit: the «deleted» text stays in the file until the engagement's retention. The `prev` chain carries no key (no HMAC): whoever can write the workspace (e.g. root inside the container, or a writer that does not go through `findings.py`) can rebuild it and forge, reorder, hide or resurrect comments without anything detecting it; the chain only betrays an accidental or partial break. The `stat` pre-check of `web_open` and the later `fstat` leave the race between the two untested. The hand-over of the owner on creation (`fchown`) depends on running as root in the toolbox.
- Spec 024: the `add`/`edit` events admit `images` (0 to 8 distinct sha256; empty in `add` is a bad line) and `gallery_delete` is another writer of the file (through `_comment_append`, with `edit` events of the same text); see REQ-RPT-070.

### REQ-LED-010 — Comments never reach the report or the ledger, and the CLI lists them
`list`, `render` and the downloads MUST NOT read or show comments; `comment add|edit|delete|list` MUST work through the CLI with the same file, and `comment list` MUST print each field (id, finding, date, channel, text) without control or format characters (Cc, Cf): a hostile file does not write terminal sequences (OSC 52, line breaks) to the operator's terminal.
- **WHEN** `render` and `list` with and without the comments file (the same ledger; for `list` `.gekko-web` is set aside and compared); `comment list` after writes from the CLI and over a hand-written file whose `at` and `finding` carry `ESC ]52;…BEL`, a line break and U+202E.
- **THEN** the output of `render` and `list` is byte-for-byte identical and without the comments' text; `comment list` shows the current ones and the ledger does not change; over the hostile file it prints a single line without any control or format character.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `cmd_comment_add`, `cmd_comment_edit`, `cmd_comment_delete`, `cmd_comment_list`
- Limit: in toolbox mode the guard only blocks a `bash` whose command names a scope entry, unless it is exactly `findings.py add|set|list|render` (`ledgerCliOnly`); `comment` is not in that list, so a `comment …` that names a scope host is blocked and one that does not name it passes. The operator keeps it that way: the comments are their notes, and widening `ledgerCliOnly` would loosen the boundary (separate spec-first flow).
- Spec 024: the same with comments with images: neither `render*` nor the report nor the request to the Provider change (REQ-RPT-070).

### 3.15 Report module (RPT)

Cases in `python3 .claude/tools/findings_web.py selftest` (lines `ok REQ-RPT-…`, §2a), except REQ-RPT-013 (the workspace
selftest). The module serves a root (`<root>/engagements/*`) on `127.0.0.1`; the cases use a temporary
root and a free port. Since spec 018 the module opens a single outgoing connection, to the Provider, and only inside an «ask» POST (REQ-RPT-028); the chat cases use a local fake Provider.

### REQ-RPT-001 — Workspace index: same rule as the launcher's menu, without findings data and without a restart
The index MUST list each directory of `<root>/engagements/` that has `scope.txt`, whose name meets the rule of REQ-WS-001 and whose real path falls inside `engagements/`, with its name, total, criticals, highs, confirmed/total and date of the last change, and MUST NOT show the title, target or evidence of any finding. The name rule MUST accept and reject the same as `validWorkspaceName`. A workspace created or deleted with the module up MUST be reflected on the next load.
- **WHEN** a root with valid workspaces (one without `findings.jsonl`), a directory without `scope.txt`, one with an invalid name and a symbolic link that leaves `engagements/`; the index is requested; a workspace is created and deleted between requests; and the name rule is compared with `validWorkspaceName` (via node) over a list of test names.
- **THEN** only the valid ones appear, the one without a ledger with zeros, and no canary of title, target or evidence; the new one appears and the deleted one disappears (its addresses give 404); the two rules coincide over the whole list.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` workspace discovery, index
- Limit: the index shows together the names and counts of all the engagements of the root. The comparison of rules covers the list of test names, not every possible name. One root per process: another worktree is another root.

### REQ-RPT-002 — Each page shows findings of a single workspace
The list, the search and the detail of a workspace MUST NOT contain any data from another's ledger.
- **WHEN** two workspaces with different canaries in each field; the list, a search with the other's canary and a detail of each one are requested.
- **THEN** no response of a workspace contains a canary of the other; the search for the foreign canary returns no rows.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `respond`
- Limit: it isolates by page, not by who is looking: any reader of the module can open any workspace.

### REQ-RPT-003 — List: one row per finding, ordered, with the same counters as the panel
The list MUST show one row per finding in the ledger, in any status, with severity, id and title integrated, target, status and update date; it MUST NOT show CVSS, CWE or summary as default columns. Those technical properties and the summary MUST remain available in the detail. The rows MUST be ordered from `critical` to `info` and, at equal severity, the most recent first. The list's discrete total and the index's authoritative figures MUST come from the ledger; criticals, highs and confirmed/total MUST keep their existing calculations, and confirmed/total MUST match `countFindings` over the same file. The statuses the module knows MUST be those of `FINDING_STATUSES`.
- **WHEN** a ledger with rows of all severities and statuses, an unknown status, a row without a status, lines that are not JSON, one with `NaN` and one with a stray `\r` between two objects; the list is requested; and `countFindings` is run (via node) over the same file.
- **THEN** all the rows are there with the five columns severity, finding, target, status and change in that order; the id stays next to the title; CVSS/CWE are not in the row and remain in the detail; confirmed and total match `countFindings`; `STATUSES` equals `FINDING_STATUSES`.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` list, counters; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `_scan`, `STATUSES`
- Limit: an unknown severity goes last and counts neither as critical nor high. No pagination.
- Spec 024: `STATUSES` and `FINDING_STATUSES` have six statuses (`remediated` last); `countFindings` returns seven integers (with «otros» ("others")).

### REQ-RPT-004 — Faithful state: the unknown, the broken and the uncorroborated are seen as such
A status that is not exactly one of the known ones MUST be shown as `estado desconocido: «<value>»` ("unknown status"), and a severity that is not as `severidad desconocida: «<value>»` ("unknown severity"); a row without a status MUST say «sin estado» ("no status"); none of them MUST carry the confirmed mark or count. A `confirmed` row without `corroboration` (or with only Cc/Cf characters, rule `_blank`) MUST be marked «sin corroboración» ("uncorroborated") in the list and in the detail. For each bad line (REQ-LED-004) the list MUST warn with its number, show its escaped text and say that the ledger accepts no additions or `render` until repaired, and MUST keep showing the other rows.
- **WHEN** a ledger with a row of status `weird`, rows with status `"confirmed "` (trailing space), `["confirmed"]` and `"Confirmed"`, one without a status, one confirmed without corroboration, one confirmed with it and six bad lines (broken text, `42`, `[]`, `null`, one with `NaN` and one with a stray `\r` between two objects).
- **THEN** those of unknown status say `estado desconocido`, do not carry the confirmed class and do not count as confirmed; the one without a status says «sin estado»; only the uncorroborated one carries «sin corroboración», in list and detail; there is one warning per bad line with its number and its text; the valid rows remain in the list.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `status_pill`, `sev_pill`, `unsupported_confirmed`, `bad_line_notice`
- Limit: «sin corroboración» looks at whether the field exists and is not blank, not its content. The uncorroborated row still counts in confirmed/total, same as in the panel. The known statuses are shown with the ledger's word (`confirmed`, `needs_validation`), untranslated.
- Spec 024: a hand-written `remediated` row without a retest note is left out of the report with the reason «sin nota de nueva prueba» ("no retest note") (`report_gap`).

### REQ-RPT-005 — Search and filter: inside the workspace, without changing the counters
With a search text the list MUST keep only the rows that contain it in title, target, CWE or CVE, case-insensitively; with a severity, only those of that severity; with no matches it MUST say so; the counters MUST NOT change.
- **WHEN** searches by a text of the title, of the target, of a CWE and of a CVE, in upper and lower case; a filter by severity; both at once; and a search with no matches.
- **THEN** only the expected rows remain; the page with no matches says so; the counters are those of the full list.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` list
- Limit: literal substring match; it does not search evidence, summary or steps.

### REQ-RPT-006 — Detail: all the row's fields, «—» for the absent, 404 for what does not exist, and an anomalous row does not bring the page down
A finding's page MUST show severity, status, title, target, CVSS, CWE, CVE, summary, evidence (command, artifact path, output, and the screenshots of `evidence.screenshots` with their sha256, the status in which they were attached and their time, expandable with `<details>` and with «Abrir original» ("Open original"); a missing, altered or retired screenshot is seen as «imagen ausente o alterada» ("image missing or altered") with the recorded sha256, never as an image), corroboration, blocker, steps, impact, remediation, KB and dates; an absent field MUST be seen as «—»; a finding or workspace address that does not exist MUST answer 404. A row with unexpected types or with extreme nesting MUST NOT bring down the list, the detail or the index; a row that cannot be drawn MUST be seen in the list as a notice with its line number, without hiding the others, and its detail MUST answer 200 with the same notice. A file date out of range MUST be seen as «—». The detail's `<title>` MUST be fixed text plus the workspace's name.
- **WHEN** the detail of a row with all the fields, of a row that carries only `id`, and of a row with mixed types (title a list, `evidence` text, status a number, `repro` text); addresses of a nonexistent line, of a bad line, non-numeric ones and of a nonexistent workspace; a ledger line with absurd nesting; a row whose title is a list nested 600 levels; a ledger with the modification date set to `4e11`; and, substituting in the test the functions that draw a row with others that throw, the list and the detail.
- **THEN** each value appears, the steps as an ordered list and the output in a `<pre>`; the minimal row shows «—»; the mixed-type one answers 200; the nonexistent addresses answer 404; the absurd line is a bad-line notice; with the nested title the list, the detail and the index answer 200 and the other rows are there; the index shows «—» in the date; the row that throws appears as a notice with its line, the others are seen and its detail answers 200; the `<title>` does not contain the finding's title.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `detail_page`, `list_page`, `text`, `fmt_time`, `captures_section`, `evidence_block`, `state_form`
- Limit: the detail is addressed by the ledger's line number, not by id: a repeated id has one page per row. The artifact path is text: the module does not serve the file. The notice of «a row that cannot be drawn» is tested by forcing the failure: no known row causes it today. The workspace name does go in the `<title>` and in the URL, and the search text in `?q=`: they stay in the browser history.

### REQ-RPT-007 — Hostile content: every ledger field comes out escaped, without scripts, without invisible characters and without external origins
Every value of a row MUST come out as escaped text, without control characters or invisible or text-direction characters: no ledger string MUST appear as a tag, attribute or link destination. Every response MUST carry a content policy that forbids scripts and resources from another origin, and no page MUST reference an external origin.
- **WHEN** a row whose fields (id, title, severity, status, target, summary, impact, remediation, evidence, steps, KB, corroboration, blocker, CWE, CVE) carry `<script>alert(1)</script>`, `"><img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`, `</pre><svg onload=alert(1)>`, `javascript:alert(1)` and an ANSI escape; a row with U+202E, U+200B, U+2066 and U+FEFF in the title; the index, the list, a search and the detail are requested; those same hostile values also go in a comment's text and in each field of edit mode (REQ-RPT-035, REQ-RPT-039), and the detail and `?editar=1` are requested; a valid brand with a hostile name is used in the preview and in «previsualizar» ("preview"); and the headers of each response are read, including those of errors.
- **THEN** no response derived from the ledger or Provider contains `<script`, `<img` or the ESC byte; the only exception for `<img>` is the validated brand's preview: exactly `<img class="dmarca" src="data:image/png|jpeg;base64,…" alt="…">`, with clean bytes and escaped `alt` (REQ-RPT-058), and the Adjuntos screenshots: exactly `<img src="/w/<ws>/galeria/<64 hex>.(png|jpg)" alt="Captura N" loading="lazy">`, without any value of the row or the index except the validated hash (spec 023a, REQ-RPT-061). The only `<svg` of each page are those the module draws (nut, static icons `<use href="#i-…">` and inline sprite); no `<svg` comes from the ledger; no tag carries an event attribute; a ledger `javascript:` does not end up inside a tag; there are no `style` attributes, not even with each theme cookie; the controls come out as U+FFFD and the four invisibles do not appear. The page CSP remains the previous one plus `img-src 'self' data:` (the brand preview and the Adjuntos screenshots do not change it); the HTML's internal CSP authorizes its CSS, without `script-src`, `connect-src` or the Provider's origin, and carries the CSS hash with and without a theme cookie. Gekko's brand (isotype and logotype) remains in four PNGs of the kit embedded as `data:` in CSS, without a generic «G» or an `<svg>` foreign to the module's icons; the bar uses the logotype open and the isotype folded (REQ-RPT-053/054); the drafts remain escaped (REQ-RPT-051). No HTML names another origin.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `esc`, `text`, templates, `response_headers`, `BRAND_PNG`, `token_block`
- Limit: `img-src 'self' data:` allows `data:` images and images from the own path on any page, but no ledger or Provider string reaches a tag (they are escaped, REQ-RPT-031). It tests those values in those fields, not «any XSS». No browser takes part in the gate (the served HTML is inspected); on 2026-11-15 it was looked at in headless Firefox 152 that the style loads with that policy and that the hostile row is seen as text. The search box repeats what the operator typed (`q`), escaped, inside a `value` attribute: it is the operator's input, not the ledger's. The invisibles replaced are U+200B–U+200F, U+202A–U+202E, U+2060–U+2069 and U+FEFF; other similar ones (homoglyphs) pass. The module shows `evidence.output` as it is: a secret stored there is seen.
- Spec 024: «without scripts» except that of REQ-RPT-071 at `/w/<ws>/reporte`: that response's CSP adds exactly `script-src 'sha256-…'`; the others stay the same.

### REQ-RPT-008 — Web writes are status, chat, edit and comments, each through a `findings.py` function
The module MUST keep the index, the list, the detail and their existing actions, plus the per-workspace report and its `.md` and `.html` downloads and the `.imprimir` ("print") view (GET, REQ-RPT-021…024), which do not write. The status action `POST /w/<ws>/f/<line>/estado` MUST use `findings.py` `set_row` and may only change `status`, `corroboration`, `blocker` and `updated` of the selected row; with images (choosing, uploading or removing, spec 023a) the same action uses `set_evidence`, which calls `set_row` under its lock, and additionally changes `evidence.screenshots`. The other only web writes are five (plus, since spec 023a, the upload and deletion of Adjuntos: `gallery_upload` only from `post_gallery` and `gallery_delete` only from `post_gallery_delete`, REQ-RPT-060, and the GET image route, REQ-RPT-061): (1) the finding's chat (REQ-RPT-032 and REQ-RPT-034): applying and undoing a proposal through `set_fields` (only `summary`, `impact`, `remediation` and `updated`) and the history under `.gekko-web/`; (2) the edit `POST /w/<ws>/f/<line>/editar` (REQ-RPT-038…041): `set_fields` with `allowed=EDIT_FIELDS` (`summary`, `impact`, `remediation`, `cwe`, `cve`, `cvss_vector`, `cvss_justification` and `updated`; the score is derived by `findings.py` from the vector and is never sent); (3) the comments `POST /w/<ws>/f/<line>/comentario` (REQ-RPT-035 and REQ-RPT-036): `comment_add`, `comment_edit` and `comment_delete` of `findings.py`, which only write `.gekko-web/comentarios.jsonl` and never the ledger; (4) the report draft `POST /w/<ws>/reporte/borrador` (REQ-RPT-048): `save_draft` writes only `.gekko-web/borradores/<type>.json` (never the ledger) and is the only function of the module that does so; (5) `POST /barra`, which only sets the `gekko_barra` cookie (REQ-RPT-053) and does not write in the workspace. The module MUST call `set_row` only from the status action, `set_fields` only from applying, undoing and the edit, and the `comment_*` functions only from `post_comment`, and MUST NOT name the comments file. Any other route MUST answer 404 and any other method, or a POST to another route, 405. The module MUST NOT call or reference `_save`, `_locked`, `cmd_add` or `cmd_set` (as a name, as attribute `findings._save` or with `import findings`). A `findings.jsonl` that is not a regular file MUST NOT be opened for this action. Every ledger read the module does (pages, downloads, the status action and its `set_row`) MUST open it without following a symbolic link or blocking on a FIFO (`O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`), MUST check with `fstat` on that same descriptor that it is a regular file before reading, and MUST read only from that descriptor; a ledger of more than `LEDGER_MAX_BYTES` (64 MiB) MUST NOT be read beyond that limit plus one byte (unbuffered read with `os.read` on the descriptor) or show rows: the pages say «ledger demasiado grande (máx. 64 MiB)» ("ledger too large (max. 64 MiB)"), the downloads answer 409 and the status action answers 409 without writing. The ledger fingerprint (REQ-RPT-022) MUST be computed over exactly the bytes parsed.
- **WHEN** GET to all routes, including the status action; POST to `/actualizar`, `/tema`, `/barra` and the status action; PUT, DELETE, PATCH, HEAD, OPTIONS and POST to the others; GET to file/artifact routes; ledger and directories are compared before and after the read-only routes, including the report and its three downloads; the module and its write calls are inspected (AST: `set_row` only in `post_state`, `set_fields` only in `apply_proposal`, `undo_proposal` and `post_edit`, `comment_add|edit|delete` only in `post_comment`, counting calls by name and by attribute (`m.comment_add(…)`), and no `comentarios.jsonl` string in production, tested against synthetic fragments); a `findings.jsonl` changed to a link to a file outside, to a FIFO or to a Unix socket after the type check (in the test, `os.lstat` returns that of a decoy regular file) and the list, the `.md` download and the status action are requested; a `findings.jsonl` changed to a link just before `set_row` (in the test, a `set_row` wrapper makes the change); a sparse ledger of 64 MiB + 1 byte; and, with the limit lowered to 4096 bytes in the test, a valid ledger of more than 4096 bytes (list, index, report, download and status action) and another that grows above the limit just after the `fstat` (in the test, an `os.fstat` wrapper enlarges it and another of `os.read` adds up the bytes requested from the kernel); and `editable_reason` over a ledger above the limit and over a socket.
- **THEN** the existing actions keep their behavior; GET to the status action is 404; wrong methods are 405; only the POSTs of status, chat, edit, comment and draft write (`/barra` only sets a cookie), each through its function; the fields other than the four of the status action (`status`, `corroboration`, `blocker`, `updated`) do not change; the artifact remains text, and links/FIFOs/devices are not opened; the one changed after the check shows no rows of the target, the list warns «no se pudo leer el ledger» ("the ledger could not be read"), the download and the status action answer 409, all in under 2 s with the FIFO (and the status action over a FIFO that already was one, also 409), and the link and its target stay intact; the one changed before `set_row` answers 409 «no es un archivo regular» ("not a regular file") without touching link or target; the limit is 64 MiB and the sparse one shows the size notice without rows; the large ledger shows the notice in list, index and report without rows, the download and the status action give 409 and their bytes do not change; the one that grows shows the size notice without rows and the kernel delivers exactly limit + 1 bytes; the socket answers 409 (`not_regular`, never 500) and `editable_reason` says so.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `respond`, `post_state`, `apply_proposal`, `undo_proposal`, `post_edit`, `post_comment`, `post_draft`, `save_draft`, `post_barra`, `detail_page`, `editable_reason`, `read_ledger`, `LEDGER_MAX_BYTES`, `too_large`; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `set_row`, `set_fields`, `comment_add|edit|delete`, `_read_regular`, `_scan`
- Limit: a hard link of the same UID to a regular file outside the workspace is not detected (`O_NOFOLLOW` only sees symbolic links and the target is a regular file); the static test (AST, tested against synthetic fragments) excludes the body of `cmd_selftest`; the module's process keeps writing traces in `<root>/.gekko-state/report.log`. `GET /actualizar` answers 404. A non-regular file that already was one when checked is not opened; one changed afterwards is opened without following the link or blocking and is rejected without reading it. `O_NOFOLLOW` only protects the last component: a workspace directory changed to a link after the REQ-RPT-019 check still opens through the link (same-UID process). Up to 64 MiB each view still reads and parses the whole ledger. Mid-read changes are simulated by substituting `os.lstat`, `os.fstat` and `set_row` in the test, not with a real race.
- Spec 024: the status form also writes `retest` (through `set_row`/`set_evidence`, only with target `remediated`).
- Spec 024: adding a comment can write images (through `comment_add`, which uploads to «Adjuntos» and writes the event all or nothing) and deleting from «Adjuntos» (`gallery_delete`) also writes in the comments file; both remain `findings.py` writes.

### REQ-RPT-018 — The web applies the same `set` rules and keeps the rejected text
The web action MUST share `set_row` with the command and MUST accept or reject with the same rules for status, corroboration, blocker and unique id. A rule rejection MUST answer 422 in Spanish, say what is missing and that nothing was saved, and keep the values typed in the fields. The status the page shows MUST remain that of the saved row. The body MUST be decoded as strict UTF-8; the status must match exactly one of `STATUSES`; only status, corroboration and blocker of the form reach `set_row`; `\r\n` is normalized to `\n`. The action MUST also accept `multipart/form-data` (spec 023a, REQ-RPT-060) and, in urlencoded and in multipart, a repeated required field MUST be 400; without images the path is the usual one (`set_row`) and a multipart form without a file (empty `filename`) accepts and rejects the same as the urlencoded one.
- **WHEN** web/CLI pairs over copies of the same ledger for the five statuses, empty fields, only spaces, existing data, unknown/absent status and corroboration or blocker refusals; invalid UTF-8 values; hostile additional fields; and an accepted save.
- **THEN** both paths accept or reject the same, except `updated`; a web refusal is 422 with no change of bytes, with the operator's text escaped and the current status; additional fields are ignored; a success answers 303 to the validated finding page, located by its id in the reread ledger (saving removes blank lines and shifts later rows); if the id no longer appears, to the workspace list.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `post_state`, `respond`; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `set_row`, `Refusal`
- Limit: `set` keeps the behavior of not deleting corroboration/blocker with an empty value; the corroboration is only validated as non-empty text, same as in REQ-LED-003. The form's text fields come out empty and the current value is shown as escaped text under the field's label («Actual: …» ("Current: …"), only if it exists; never in an attribute) and the placeholder is always a neutral example: empty keeps the value, and a single line «Vacío conserva el valor actual.» ("Empty keeps the current value.") says so. The form has one field per requirement, each with a label and a hint («¿Qué lo confirma?» ("What confirms it?") for Confirmed, «¿Qué falta para validarlo?» ("What is missing to validate it?") for Por validar) and without «actual»/«nueva» lines; only with CSS `:has()` inside `@supports selector(:has(*))` is the field of the chosen status shown (without `:has()` both are seen), without JavaScript or `style=`; the server's validations and messages do not change. Empty keeps the value (a resend without touching the fields does not rewrite corroboration or blocker); only after a 409/422 are they filled with what was sent. A text without visible characters (only Cc controls or Cf format, e.g. U+200B) counts as empty (REQ-LED-007). AC12 of `specs/012-report-module/spec.md` on non-writing is replaced by AC11 and AC12 of `specs/016-estado-desde-web/spec.md`.
- Spec 024: `retest` is optional in the body (a body without it still gives 303), repeated gives 400 and is only written with target `remediated`; the rules of REQ-LED-011 (`not_confirmed`, `no_retest`, corroboration) give 422 in Spanish without writing anything; the web = CLI matrix covers the six statuses (`remediated` starts from a corroborated confirmed row and passes its note). Limit: the note is text, it is not proven that the retest occurred.

### REQ-RPT-019 — The action requires an allowed origin, a valid ticket and a body within the limit
The status route MUST first require the Host and user controls of REQ-RPT-009, POST and the exact route, and `Sec-Fetch-Site` absent or `same-origin`; it must reject a body larger than `MAX_BODY` with 413 (`DRAFT_MAX_BODY` on the draft route, REQ-RPT-048; 20 MiB + 64 KiB only with `multipart/form-data`, where the text parts with their headers over `MAX_BODY` also give 413); the upload to Adjuntos and the deletion carry their own tickets with domain separation, REQ-RPT-060). It must require `estado`, `corroboracion`, `bloqueo` and `visto` (400 if any is missing) and strict UTF-8; an absent `ficha` is an invalid ticket (403). Before writing, workspace and line must exist, the HMAC-SHA256 ticket bound to workspace, line and seen fingerprint must be compared with `hmac.compare_digest`, and the row must be editable: unique id, intact, regular, readable ledger within `LEDGER_MAX_BYTES` (REQ-RPT-008), unlinked directory. An invalid ticket MUST answer 403; absent routes or lines 404; non-editable row/ledger or linked workspace 409; all without writing.
- **WHEN** `Sec-Fetch-Site` cross-site/same-site/none/absent, invalid Host and peer, absent/false/other-finding's/other-workspace's/valid ticket, GET with data in the query, non-POST methods, nonexistent route/line, body `MAX_BODY + 1`, non-regular/corrupt ledger or duplicate id, and linked workspace.
- **THEN** the above codes are obtained without modifying the ledger; the valid ticket works with and without `Sec-Fetch-Site`; the action's GET gives 404 and the other methods give 405.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `Ctx`, `Handler._handle`, `state_ticket`, `post_state`, `respond`, `editable_reason`
- Limit: the ticket lives in memory and is invalidated when the module restarts/updates; same-UID processes that can read the page can obtain a ticket. The HMAC ticket is CSRF protection, not a human-in-the-loop control: a same-UID process can request the form and submit it.
- Spec 024: the «Agregar comentario» ("Add comment") form is `multipart/form-data` and carries the block `<details class="adjuntar">` (checkboxes `elegir` and `archivo`); `elegir`/`archivo` with `editar` or `borrar` give 400.

### REQ-RPT-020 — The fingerprint prevents saving over a modified row
The form MUST include the full fingerprint of the row it showed; `post_state` MUST pass it to `set_row(expect=...)`. If the row changed, the response MUST be 409, must not write and must show the current values keeping the text typed. The 303 after a success and its reload MUST NOT resend the POST.
- **WHEN** the ledger changes after serving the form; the same form is submitted a second time; the success URL is opened repeatedly; and ten saves over different rows run at the same time as ten `findings.py add`, in three rounds.
- **THEN** a stale row receives 409 and keeps the ledger; a stale duplicate does not write again; the notice only appears if the current fingerprint matches; after concurrency all changes and additions remain and `findings.py list` exits 0.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `post_state`, `saved_notice`; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `set_row`, `_locked`
- Limit: if the first write does not change material bytes and `updated` stays equal in the same second, the second submission may save the same row again without altering the ledger.

### REQ-RPT-009 — Loopback only, `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>` only, and only the user running the module
The server MUST listen only on `127.0.0.1`. A connection that does not come from `127.0.0.1`, or whose owner —looked up by its exact source address and port— is not the module's UID, or cannot be determined, MUST be closed on accepting it, without reading a byte or creating a thread. A request whose `Host` is not exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` (also `localhost:<port>` and `[::1]:<port>`) MUST receive a fixed 403 with no data from any ledger, without the name of any workspace and without the header that identifies the module. A request's body MUST be read bounded to `0…MAX_BODY` (`DRAFT_MAX_BODY` only on the draft route, REQ-RPT-048; `MULTIPART_BODY` = 20 MiB + 64 KiB only with `multipart/form-data` on the status route and on the upload to Adjuntos).
- **WHEN** the real server's listening address and a connection to another address of the machine; requests with a foreign `Host`, without `Host`, with `localhost:<port>` and with `[::1]:<port>`; `peer_uid` over test connection tables (own row, absent, with swapped endpoints, of another port, malformed, empty table; and two rows with the same source port, one from `127.0.0.1` of the own UID and another from `127.0.0.2` of another UID); `respond` with `peer_ok` false; a real connection of the same user; a real client bound to `127.0.0.2:P` while the same user keeps open a connection from `127.0.0.1:P`; and a request with `Content-Length: -1` followed by 1 MB.
- **THEN** it listens on `127.0.0.1` and the other address rejects; the `Host` values that are not its own receive the 403 without canaries, without names and without `X-Gekko-Report`; `peer_uid` returns the UID of the exact row —for `127.0.0.2:P`, the other UID, never the own one— or `None`; with `peer_ok` false, 403 without data; the same user's connection is served; the `127.0.0.2` client has its connection closed without a byte of response; the request with `Content-Length: -1` gets a response in under 2 s.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `Server.verify_request`, `is_same_user_peer`, `peer_uid`, `respond`, `Handler`
- Limit: the rejection of another REAL user is not in the gate (it needs docker or sudo). Manual runs of 2026-11-15 with a container on the host's network (UID 0), in `specs/012-report-module/tasks.md`: before the fix it read a ledger by repeating from `127.0.0.2` the source port of a live connection of the operator; after, that attempt and the normal request have their connection closed. It depends on `/proc/net/tcp` (Linux): where it cannot be read, the module rejects everything. There is no login: processes of the same user read it. That includes two cases that add access with respect to the files: an operator proxy that listens outside loopback (Burp, ZAP, mitmproxy) forwards requests to the module and whoever reaches the proxy can read all the ledgers, change the status of any editable row, edit its fields, comment and save report drafts; and a rootless container with the host's network has the operator's UID. If the module runs as root, any root process passes. The ticket is invalidated when the module restarts or updates; such a proxy can also ask the Provider and apply proposals (REQ-RPT-029, REQ-RPT-032). The «other address» case is skipped, with a notice, on a machine without another interface. Same-user connections that send nothing take a thread for up to 10 s each. A question to the Provider (REQ-RPT-029) takes its thread for up to 240 s; the module cuts it if the browser leaves.
- Spec 024: `MULTIPART_BODY` also holds for the comment route with `multipart/form-data`; today's urlencoded body is accepted the same.

### REQ-RPT-010 — Live fidelity: each load rereads the ledger
A change in a ledger MUST be seen on the next load without restarting; a ledger that does not exist or is deleted MUST be seen as «sin hallazgos» ("no findings").
- **WHEN** with the module up a row is added, its status is changed, the file is deleted and created again.
- **THEN** each load reflects the file at that moment; without a file the list says «sin hallazgos».
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` per-request read
- Limit: the list and the detail do not reload themselves (the index does, every 15 s): what is on screen may be old. It reads all the ledgers on each index load, without cache.

### REQ-RPT-011 — New code only on pressing «Buscar actualizaciones» ("Check for updates"), and only from the same origin
All pages MUST carry the button and, when the code on disk differs from the loaded one, MUST warn of it. A POST to `/actualizar` MUST: with no changes, say it is up to date; with changes that compile, answer and replace the process with the new code on the same port; with changes that do not compile, say so and continue with the loaded code. Without a POST the module MUST continue with the loaded code. A POST with `Sec-Fetch-Site` present and different from `same-origin` MUST be rejected without changing code.
- **WHEN** the module runs from a copy of its two files in a temporary directory: POST without changes; the copy is changed and it waits without pressing; POST with `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`; valid POST; and a change that does not compile followed by a POST.
- **THEN** «al día» ("up to date"); after the wait the version header is the old one and the page warns of the new code; the other-origin POST receives 403 and the version does not change; after the valid POST the response arrives whole and in 5 s or less the version is the new one, with the same PID, even with a question hanging on the Provider; with the broken file the page says so and the version does not change.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` update action, header `X-Gekko-Report`
- Limit: it checks that the new code compiles, not that it starts: a file that compiles and fails on startup leaves the module down until the next `pi-hitl.sh` start. It downloads nothing. A browser that does not send `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not covered; the worst that request achieves is that the module takes the code already on disk. The new-code notice is only seen by whoever opens a page. The notice and the check cover `findings_web.py`, `findings.py` and `sanitize_target_content.py`, not everything else Python loads from that directory: a sibling `json.py` or a planted `__pycache__` are loaded at the next start without notice; whoever can write the checkout can already change any script the operator runs.

### REQ-RPT-012 — Coupling: `ensure` uses the module of its root and its user, brings one up if there is none and never adopts another
`ensure` MUST print the base URL and exit 0 when a process of the same UID that answers as the module of its same root listens on the port, without bringing up another; MUST bring it up detached, with a minimal environment, when nobody listens; and MUST exit ≠ 0 without a URL when the port is taken by another program, a module of another root or a process of another UID. `ensure` MUST decide with the headers, without reading the response body. Two simultaneous `ensure` MUST leave a single process. `serve` MUST print the URL and free the port when interrupted.
- **WHEN** `ensure` without a module; a second `ensure` with the module up; `listener_uid` over test tables (own UID, other UID, nobody listening, other port, listening on `0.0.0.0`, a row not in listen state, two owners, malformed UID); a table in which whoever listens is of another UID even though it answers as the module; `ensure` with `GEKKO_LLM_KEY` and `GEKKO_PROVIDER_KEY` set in the environment and the reading of the environment of the process it brought up; two `ensure` at once over a free port; `ensure` with the port taken by a foreign server and by a module of another root; and `serve`, a second `serve` on the same port and an interruption.
- **THEN** URL and exit 0 with a new process; URL and exit 0 with the same PID; `listener_uid` returns the UID of whoever listens on `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `None`; that of another UID is not adopted; the module's environment only carries `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, `LC_*` and `TZ`; a single process and the same URL in both; exit ≠ 0 and nothing on stdout in the two occupied-port cases; the second `serve` exits 1 with one line and the port is free after interrupting.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `cmd_ensure`, `is_ours`, `probe`, `listener_uid`, `cmd_serve`
- Limit: a process of the SAME user that takes the port first and imitates the header is adopted. Whoever occupies the port and delivers the headers drop by drop can delay `ensure`, and with it the start of `pi-hitl.sh`, up to the read cap (1 s per header line). The detached module stays up until `pkill -f 'findings_web.py serve'` or a machine restart. A `serve` launched by hand inherits that shell's environment.

### REQ-RPT-013 — The launcher only publishes the URL of the module that `ensure` confirmed
`tools/pi-hitl.sh` MUST delete `GEKKO_REPORT_URL` before deciding, MUST call `ensure` only with a workspace and with `GEKKO_REPORT` different from `0`, and MUST export the variable only when `ensure` exited 0.
- **WHEN** the selftest reads `tools/pi-hitl.sh` and applies regular expressions over its text.
- **THEN** the `unset GEKKO_REPORT_URL`, the condition over the workspace and `GEKKO_REPORT`, the call to `findings_web.py ensure` and the export conditioned on its exit exist.
- Code: `tools/pi-hitl.sh`
- Limit: only regex over text (like REQ-WS-007). The real behavior —it brings it up, couples, survives Pi, `none`, occupied port, another root, two starts at once, URL inherited from the environment— is proven by `tools/ws-test.sh` with a fake `pi`, outside the gate.

### REQ-RPT-014 — Settings: folded menu with a nut and light or dark theme, without JavaScript and without reflecting the cookie
Every page MUST carry a folded settings menu (`<details>` without `open`) whose button is a nut drawn inside the page with the accessible name «Ajustes» ("Settings"), and which contains «Buscar actualizaciones» and the theme choice. The document's theme class MUST come only from a cookie whose value is exactly `claro` or `oscuro`; any other value MUST be ignored and MUST NOT appear in the response; without a valid cookie the CSS MUST follow the system preference. `POST /tema` MUST set or delete that cookie and redirect to the page of origin only if it is a route of the module's own; it MUST reject a request with `Sec-Fetch-Site` present and different from `same-origin`. Text, links and labels MUST have contrast ≥ 4.5:1 in both themes. The dark theme MUST declare background `#090D0C`, surface `#121816`, text `#F2F6F3` and accent `#C0F425`; the light theme MUST declare background `#F2F5F0`, surface `#FFFFFF` and text `#0B110F`. The CSS MUST declare Space Grotesk and JetBrains Mono first in its stacks, without allowing external resources. The sidebar MUST fold through `POST /barra` with the cookie `gekko_barra` (REQ-RPT-053), with the same mold as the theme cookie.
- **WHEN** the index, the list and the detail are requested without a cookie, with `gekko_tema=claro`, with `gekko_tema=oscuro` and with hostile or unknown values; `POST /tema` with each theme and with an invalid value, with a valid `volver` ("return") and with hostile `volver` (`//otro.example/`, `http://otro.example/`, a path with a line break and header, `/../etc`); the same POST with `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`; `GET /tema`; the code on disk different from the loaded one; and the contrast calculation of each theme's color pairs.
- **THEN** each page has a single folded menu with the nut, its accessible name and the update button inside; without a cookie there is no theme class and the CSS carries the system-preference rule; with `claro` or `oscuro` the corresponding class appears; a hostile value produces no class and does not appear in the response; the POST answers 303 toward the valid `volver`, or toward `/` if it is hostile, with the cookie set (`SameSite=Strict`, `HttpOnly`, `Path=/`) or deleted for `auto` and for an invalid value; the other-origin POST receives 403 without `Set-Cookie`; `GET /tema` answers 404; with new code the notice is outside and inside the menu; all pairs give ≥ 4.5:1; the required base tokens and typographic stacks appear in the served CSS.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `page`, `respond`, `post_theme`, `post_barra`, `safe_back`, `theme_cookie`, `bar_cookie`, `THEMES`, `CONTRAST_PAIRS`
- Limit: the hostile cookie values tested are `claro x`, `claro,oscuro`, `CLARO`, `claroo`, empty, `xgekko_tema=claro`, `%63laro` and `"><script>alert(1)</script>`; `gekko_tema=claro; x` counts as valid (it is a normal cookie list) and, if several arrive, the first valid one wins. A `volver` with `%0d%0a` is accepted as literal text: it is not a real line break. The menu opens and closes only with its button (without JavaScript it does not close when clicking outside). The functional test does not cover the browser or widths other than those captured in T09. The cookie belongs to the host `127.0.0.1`, not the port: another local service on `127.0.0.1` sees it and can change it; it can only be `claro` or `oscuro`. The contrast is computed over the declared colors, not over what a browser draws; it was looked at in headless Firefox in both themes. A browser that does not send `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not covered; the worst that request achieves is changing the theme or folding the bar. `BACK_RE` additionally accepts `/w/<ws>/reporte` (spec 019b) as a return destination, for the theme and the bar.

### REQ-RPT-015 — GET status filter: exact match inside the workspace
The list MUST accept a `status` filter only when its value is exactly one of `findings.STATUSES`; empty or invalid MUST mean «Todos» ("All"). The filter MUST combine with `q` and `sev` over the rows of that same workspace, MUST NOT change its counters and MUST NOT make an unknown status look valid. The list's metric cards (total, confirmed, to validate, highs and criticals) and the review progress MUST always count the whole ledger, also with an active filter. The options MUST come from the same allowlist and the untrusted value MUST NOT be reflected in labels or attributes.
- **WHEN** a temporary ledger with all the valid statuses, unknown status, absent, of non-text type, `confirmed ` and `CONFIRMED`; combined queries `q` + `sev` + `status`, empty, invalid and repeated values, a text search that only appears in the output, and two workspaces with different data.
- **THEN** each combination returns only the expected rows; the invalid value goes back to All, the unknown statuses remain there unclassified, the searches do not read evidence, the counters and metric cards keep the ledger's total even with a filter, and rows of workspaces are not mixed.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `filter_rows`, `list_page`, `list_metrics`, `list_rail`; selftest `REQ-RPT-015`.
- Limit: substring search in title, target, CWE and CVE, without pagination; when `status` is repeated, the first value `parse_qs` delivers is used.

### REQ-RPT-016 — Detail by sections with persistent notices and literal output
The detail MUST expose Summary, Evidence and corroboration, and Log (Registro) as native targets without JavaScript; with no fragment or with an unknown one, Summary MUST stay visible. The severity, the status and the notices MUST remain outside the sections that are hidden. `needs_validation` MUST show its blocker; a `confirmed` without corroboration MUST warn that the signal is empty; a page of a valid row in a ledger with broken lines MUST indicate line numbers. The evidence output MUST remain initially closed, inside `<pre>` and escaped as text; the artifact path MUST remain text. When printing, the detail MUST show all the sections. The detail's «Asistente» ("Assistant") panel (a single `<section id="asistente">` and a link `#asistente`, without JavaScript) is governed by REQ-RPT-025…034; the detail MUST additionally expose the Comments section (`#comentarios`, REQ-RPT-035) and the edit mode `?editar=1` (REQ-RPT-038), and MUST NOT add another write than the status action (REQ-RPT-008 and REQ-RPT-018…020), the chat, the edit (REQ-RPT-039…041) and the comments (REQ-RPT-036); the report and export are governed by REQ-RPT-008 and REQ-RPT-021…024.
- **WHEN** a temporary detail of a normal row, `needs_validation` with a blocker, `confirmed` without corroboration and an output with HTML/control; the same ledger also has a broken line; and the fragments Summary/Evidence/Log are requested.
- **THEN** the three targets exist; notices are before and outside panels; the evidence is not interpreted or includes control characters; a broken line is indicated with its number; the print shows the three sections.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `detail_page`, `respond`, `esc`; selftest `REQ-RPT-016`.
- Limit: the selftest tests HTML and response contracts, not CSS `:target`, tab order or the print result; those states require a real browser. The warning in the detail indicates line numbers; the listing keeps the complete escaped fragments.

### REQ-RPT-021 — Per-workspace report page
The page `/w/<workspace>/reporte` MUST use a single snapshot of `load_templates` per request and `included` to share the selection with the downloaded formats; MUST show included/total, confirmed without corroboration with id and link to the detail, and the summary of the other statuses. It must offer Document/Markdown views, es/en languages and the valid templates (REQ-RPT-045), with the draft and the preview in stacked columns below 1280 px. Document MUST show `report_body_for` inline, without iframe or JavaScript, in `.sheet`, under «Vista previa · A4» ("Preview · A4") and the number of included; the logo is the template's validated clean one or, if it has none, Gekko's brand. The sheet's body shares sections and data with `report_html`, but the preview uses the `<img>` logo of REQ-RPT-058 and the downloads keep their own bytes. The statuses, the provenance and the downloads stay outside the sheet. Unknown parameters go back to Document, English and Technical. An unreadable or broken ledger is warned about without a partial report or download links.
- **WHEN** the page is requested in both views/languages with corroborated findings, confirmed without corroboration, unknown and empty statuses, and with a nonexistent, broken or non-regular ledger.
- **THEN** only the included rows appear in the body, the excluded ones and counts are explained, views/languages keep the other parameter, zero included is distinguished from an empty ledger and from an error, and an invalid ledger delivers no partial body.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `report_route`, `report_page`, `report_body_for`, `draft_form`; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `included`, `report_fields`, `load_templates`
- Limit: a non-empty `corroboration` does not prove an independent second signal. The Markdown view inside the module escapes everything; the residue of the downloaded `.md` is in REQ-RPT-022. The downloaded file ends up outside `engagements/` and outside its retention. `evidence.output` may contain secrets. AC16 only covers the hostile values listed.
- UI-r3: the page MUST show a single row of actions in the header (Hallazgos, Vista previa, Guardar borrador —buttons with `form="borrador"` outside the form, without JS—, Markdown, HTML and a single primary «Exportar PDF» that opens the print view in another tab), a full-width segmented Técnico/Ejecutivo selector with compact Idioma and Vista, «Contenido editable» as `<details open>`, and the provenance, the copy notice and the ledger states in a single `<details>` «Procedencia y estado del ledger»; no bottom bar or separate downloads row. Case «UI-r3: one header row…». UI-r3 review: that `<details>` goes below the sheet (the two columns start aligned), the sheet's header carries «Vista previa · A4» and «N hallazgos incluidos» in two `<span>`, the left panel is sticky from 1280 px, the repro steps are a single ordered list `<ol>` with one label, and the placeholders are read in a faint tone.
- Spec 024: the excluded ones go in a closed `<details class="prov">`, «Fuera del reporte (N)» ("Outside the report (N)"), with a line explaining why and, per row, escaped id, link and reason; no loose `<ul>` and with N = 0 it does not appear.
- Spec 024: the report page (and its Markdown view) carries exactly the pinned script of REQ-RPT-071 and `CSP_REPORT`; nothing else changes.

### REQ-RPT-022 — Downloads bound to the current ledger and without writing
The GET routes `/w/<workspace>/reporte.md`, `.html` and `.imprimir` MUST require `ledger=<sha256>` of the bytes read; if missing, invalid, changed or the ledger is broken/non-regular, they will answer 409 without a report. Markdown MUST be byte for byte `render_report` with the requested template, language and saved draft (without `--tipo`, it follows the compatible route of spec 017). The page additionally binds the template and, when a brand exists, the logo through their sha256 (REQ-RPT-050/057/058). The downloads use fixed MIME and `Content-Disposition` derived from the validated workspace, template and language, `nosniff`, `no-store` and the module's headers; they do not write to the workspace, open connections or read the chat history (REQ-RPT-033).
- **WHEN** the three formats are downloaded after opening the page, with absent/malformed/old digest and with a broken/non-regular ledger; methods other than GET, invalid Host/peer and content with URL and HTML in title, steps and KB are used; files and directories are compared before/after.
- **THEN** Markdown matches `render_md`/CLI and, outside fences and code spans, carries no `<`, `![` or `](`; headers do not reflect ledger content; the old digest and invalid ledger give 409 without a report; other methods give 405, invalid access fixed 403 and there are no writes or non-loopback connections.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `read_ledger`, `report_route`, `response_headers`; `.claude/tools/findings.py` `render_md`, `render_report`, `_code`
- Limit: the downloaded `.md` is `render_md`: inline values go in code spans (REQ-LED-002) and evidence in fences, so a CommonMark/GFM viewer does not execute HTML, load images or link the target's URLs when opening it; a viewer that does not respect spans or fences could, and the target's text is still read literally (a title can say «critical»). A failure of the generator of any format answers 500, never 404. An old report is rejected even if a row that is not included changed. Secrets stored in `evidence.output` appear in files. The gate does not test access from another real user (T28). The downloads stay outside the workspace and its retention.
- UI-r3: neither the `.md`, nor the `.html`, nor the print view, nor the page's sheet MUST contain the KB reference of a row with `kb`; the downloaded `.md` is `render_report(…, workspace)` (not `render_md`). Case «UI-r3: a row with kb set reaches no Markdown, HTML, print view or report page…».

### REQ-RPT-023 — Downloadable HTML, self-contained and without active content
The downloadable HTML MUST be self-contained and wrap `report_body_for`; its `<title>` uses the document's effective title (template text or value saved in the draft), not a fixed label per type. The internal CSP `default-src 'none'; style-src 'sha256-…'; img-src data:` MUST authorize only its own `<style>`. The document includes no scripts, links, `<img>` tags, imports or external resources; its only `data:` URL in CSS is Gekko's horizontal logo or, if there is a `marca`, the clean validated PNG/JPEG bytes (REQ-RPT-052/058). All dynamic values go through `esc`.
- **WHEN** it is generated twice for both languages with the same hostile fields, URLs and invisibles.
- **THEN** both files are deterministic, keep the fields that exist and contain no active content or external resources; MIME and name are those defined by REQ-RPT-022.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `report_html`, `report_body_for`, `report_model`, `finding_html`, `short_html`, `esc`
- Limit: the CSP depends on the viewer respecting it. The gate inspects structure and text, not the behavior of all viewers; `evidence.output` may include secrets.

### REQ-RPT-024 — Print view (PDF from the browser) without active actions
«Exportar PDF» MUST open in another tab (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`) `GET /w/<ws>/reporte.imprimir`, which serves EXACTLY the bytes of the inline `.html` —without `Content-Disposition`— with the same language, template and draft; a change in ledger, draft, template or logo pin gives 409. The security headers are kept except CSP, which MUST be `default-src 'none'; style-src 'sha256-<hash of the document's CSS>'; img-src data:; form-action 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`. The server does not generate PDF and `/reporte.pdf` is 404. `PRINT_CSS` is part of the document's signed CSS and MUST declare A4, numbering `counter(page) " / " counter(pages)` (the only `content:`), `print-color-adjust:exact`, breaks that avoid splitting headings, cover, notice, cards, findings and rows; the repeated footer carries classification and, if there is a brand, escaped name and footer. Only the validated hex and the brand's clean logo can enter the download CSS; no ledger, draft, brand name/footer or workspace texts enter. The `.html` shows the help for Ctrl+P and includes no JavaScript, `style=`, iframes or external resources.
- **WHEN** `.html` and `.imprimir` are requested in both languages, with a hostile classification `</footer><b>…`, with hostile brand name/footer, and `.pdf`.
- **THEN** `.imprimir` = `.html` byte for byte (same sha256), 200, without `Content-Disposition`, a single `Content-Type`, CSP with the document's CSS hash; the footer carries the escaped classification and the CSS only `counter(page) " / " counter(pages)` as `content:`; `.pdf` answers 404.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `PRINT_CSS`, `PRINT_HINT`, `download_css`, `print_csp`, `report_html`, `report_route`; `tools/report-test.sh`
- Limit: the gate does not measure the printed appearance; the sheet must be looked at in Chromium (`page.pdf`) or in the operator's Ctrl+P. `@page` margins with a counter are not supported by Firefox (the footer with the classification is printed). The browser's headers and footers are controlled by the browser (the help asks to uncheck them). A long finding can leave white space at the end of the previous page because of `break-inside:avoid`; long evidence does split.

Later visual correction (2026-11-16), tied to REQ-RPT-001/003:
the name's native link MUST cover the whole card without JavaScript; the link's
focus MUST mark the whole card. Long technical values, including ISO
dates in Cambio, MUST wrap inside their cell without invading another cell or
leaving the table. WHEN one clicks outside the title inside the card and a long
ISO date is shown at 390/760/1024/1500 px, THEN it navigates to the card's workspace
and the text stays contained. Interaction/geometry evidence in a browser with
synthetic fixtures: spec 015 `shots/font-local/correcciones-interaccion.md`.

### REQ-RPT-025 — Each editable finding shows an «Asistente» panel that says what it will send, without JavaScript
The detail of an editable row (unique id, regular and healthy ledger, workspace without links) MUST carry a `#asistente` panel next to the card, with a POST form (`pregunta`, `visto`, `ficha`) and shortcuts that are buttons of the same form. The panel's header MUST be only the title «Asistente» and a status pill («listo» ("ready") / «no disponible» ("not available")). Without expanding anything, the panel MUST show ONE summary line that says that data is sent, how much (`Envía N campos de este hallazgo[ y K turnos] a Provider host:puerto (esquema) · modelo X[ · con salida de evidencia]` ("Sends N fields of this finding[ and K turns] to Provider host:port (scheme) · model X[ · with evidence output]"); never the key or the URL with credentials). Inside a `<details class="sent">` «Ver qué se envía» ("See what is sent"), closed by default and without JavaScript, there MUST be the exact list of fields, the token count, the state of the evidence output (off by default) and the folded text the Provider will receive, byte for byte (system and fenced context, with the previous turns). The log and the composer get more air (without changing their elements). Without a configured Provider or with a non-editable row it MUST say why and not offer a form. Rows without a unique id MUST keep the current markup.
- **WHEN** detail of an editable row with a Provider; without a Provider and with a non-editable row; with the evidence output off and on; with controls and a previous turn in the row; and the folded text is compared with what a fake Provider receives.
- **THEN** the panel carries, visible, the pill, the summary line with host:port, model and number of fields and, inside the closed `<details>`, fields, tokens and output off, plus shortcuts and composer, without the key or the full URL; the folded text is identical byte for byte to what was received; without a Provider or a non-editable row there is no form; the card and the aside coexist in `.detail-main` (content column plus an assistant one at 32 % from 1280 px, own view below) and rows without a unique id do not change.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `chat_section`, `chat_form`, `detail_page`, `chat_context`, `detail_actions`
