Other workspace files (`notes.md`, `loot/`, `scripts/`, the ledger) remain writable by the container, which runs as root with `NET_ADMIN`: it is not containment. The tmpfs masks the path, it does not prevent a container process from writing (in memory, 1 MiB) inside it. Whoever uses docker like the operator is outside this limit.

### REQ-TBX-008 — Identity and names of the shells in toolbox
The sockets directory MUST be resolved with `socketDir` according to the PLACE chosen by the caller (by default, that of `GEKKO_EXEC`): in `toolbox` it MUST always return `<GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR>/.gekko-sockets` and ignore an inherited `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR` (that host directory is not mounted in the container); in `os` it MUST return the inherited `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR` if its real path is outside the workspace's real path, otherwise `<tmp>/gekko-sockets` if that is outside, and if neither MUST throw (the caller refuses the shell with its own socket). «Inside» is decided by real paths (a directory that does not exist yet is resolved by its nearest existing parent), not by the name `engagements`. `shell-session` MUST refuse, before listing or using it, a sockets directory that is a symbolic link. In toolbox mode `session_new` MUST reject a name outside `^[A-Za-z0-9_@][A-Za-z0-9_@-]{0,63}$` without creating anything, because the name is printed in a copyable attach command, and that command MUST name the container `gekko-tb-<ws>`, not the ID. The identity of an own shell MUST include the tmux server's `#{start_time}` and `ours()` MUST compare it, so that a restarted server with the same pid is not taken for the original.
- **WHEN** `socketDir` with each combination of place, launch mode, `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR` (outside, inside, the workspace itself, sibling with the same prefix, via a symbolic link that points to the workspace or leaves it) and workspace (also given by a link), and with the inherited one and `tmp` inside the workspace; the name pattern with valid and invalid ones; and, in the handler harness with real tmux (`toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts`, both modes): `session_new` with a hostile name in toolbox, a sockets directory replaced by a symbolic link, and a restarted server that reuses the pid.
- **THEN** the path and the names are the expected ones (and it throws when there is no room outside the workspace); the handler fails with a reason without creating a session or listing; a shell whose server restarted is not handled.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `socketDir`, `SHELL_NAME_RE`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `assertSockDirNotLink`, `ours`
- Limit: why it matters: a HOST tmux server with the socket inside the container's writable mount lets the latter execute on the host (`tmux -S <sock> run-shell`, tested). In the gate only the pure part; that `shell-session` chooses the place per shell and refuses the shell when `socketDir` throws is tested by the harness (spec 014 T3), outside the gate. Without `GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR` in the environment there is no «inside» and the inherited one is respected. The real-path check does not cover the race between resolving and using. The symbolic-link check does not cover the race between `lstat` and tmux's use. The panel's shell registry (`gekko-panel.ts`) still identifies the server only by pid.


### REQ-TBX-009 — `session_new` in toolbox only proceeds with the toolbox `lista`, and never creates the shell elsewhere
`execSessionDecision(mode, exec)` MUST return `{ ok: true }` in `os` mode whatever the state, and in `toolbox` only with the state `lista`; in `toolbox` with `levantando`, `caída`, without state (`null`) or with an absent `exec` (an old singleton) MUST return `{ ok: false, note }` with a note in Spanish that names the state (`levantando`, `caída` or «sin estado» ("no state")) and the reason, says that the shell was not created anywhere and sends the model to stop and warn the operator, without creating it on the host or by another route. The reason (it comes from docker) MUST come out without control characters, without tmux style and bounded to 160 characters. `runtime.exec` MUST exist in the shared runtime, with initial value `{ state: null, reason: "" }`. `execStateAtLoad(exec, mode, container)` MUST return `lista` in `toolbox` with a lowercase 64-hex ID and `caída` with a reason otherwise, only when the current state is `null` or missing: it MUST NOT overwrite an already written state (it is idempotent) and in `os` it MUST leave the state as it is. `containerIdentityVerdict(output, id)` MUST accept only the output `<id> true` of `docker inspect --type container --format '{{.Id}} {{.State.Running}}' <id>`: another ID, `false`, empty, with extra fields or on two lines MUST be refused. `session_new` MUST, in `toolbox`, first decide with `execSessionDecision` (FAIL naming the state, without invoking anything) and then check the identity with that `docker inspect`: a mismatch, a stopped container, an error or an expired timeout MUST give FAIL without creating anything either in the container or on the host (not even the sockets directory). It holds with and without TUI.
- **WHEN** `execSessionDecision` in `os` with each state and with `exec` absent; in `toolbox` with `lista`, with `levantando` and `caída` with a reason, with state `null` and with `exec` absent, and with a 500-character reason with escapes, line breaks and `#[bg=red]`; `newRuntime(0).exec`; `execStateAtLoad` in `toolbox` with a valid ID, with five invalid values (absent, empty, a name, uppercase, 63 characters), in `os`, with an already written state and twice in a row; `containerIdentityVerdict` with the expected output and with eight variants (another ID, stopped, empty, without state, with extra text, two lines, prefix, another ID with space); and, in the handler harness (`toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts`), `session_new` in toolbox with each state and with an intermediary `docker` on the `PATH` that records each call and returns another ID, `false`, an error or an unexpected output.
- **THEN** `os` always `ok`; `toolbox` `lista` `ok`; the others `ok` false with the requested note (state, reason, «avisa al operador» ("warn the operator"), «no la crees en el host» ("do not create it on the host")); the hostile reason's note has no controls or `#[` and measures less than 400 characters; initial `exec` is `{ state: null, reason: "" }`; `lista` only with a valid ID and `caída` with a reason without it, without touching a written state; only the exact output passes the identity; in the harness, FAIL naming the state or the cause, with `docker` called zero times (state) or just once with the `inspect` argv (identity), and no sockets directory or session created.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `execSessionDecision`, `execStateAtLoad`, `containerIdentityVerdict`, `PanelRuntime.exec`, `newRuntime`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `session_new`
- Limit: the gate tests only the pure functions (spec 014, D3). That `session_new` calls them before invoking anything and in that order is tested by the harness with the intermediary `docker`, outside the gate; with real docker the harness tests the FAIL with a 64-hex ID that is not the container's. `shell-session.ts` initializes the state on loading the extension with `execStateAtLoad`; the guard does the same with the same function (T4), and the periodic check and the `levantando`/`lista`/`caída` transitions belong to `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` (spec 014 T4): the next paragraph. The identity is checked on CREATING: an already created shell is handled with its stored place (REQ-TBX-011) and a container replaced afterwards fails closed in `ours()`. Between the `docker inspect` and the `docker exec` that creates there remains an uncovered race: the container can stop or be replaced; the second step uses the ID and cannot reach another container, but the creation fails or the state changes. That an absent or `null` `exec` in toolbox fails closed is a decision of this function: a caller that does not yet write the state will see FAIL in toolbox.

Periodic check and state at load (spec 014 T4, D1 and D4). On loading, the guard MUST set `runtime.exec = execStateAtLoad(runtime.exec, mode, GEKKO_TOOLBOX)` and nobody MUST reread `exec` afterwards: `session_start` MUST NOT change `GEKKO_EXEC`, `GEKKO_TOOLBOX` or `runtime.exec`. With `ctx.mode === "tui"`, the guard's 3 s timer MUST call `checkToolbox()` BEFORE the early return that `refreshShellsLive` takes when there are no shells, only in `toolbox` mode with state `lista`: an asynchronous call `docker inspect --type container --format '{{.Id}} {{.State.Running}}' <validated ID>` with a 3 s timeout, judged by `containerIdentityVerdict`; if it is not the exact output `<ID> true` (another ID, stopped, docker error, expired timeout, empty output, or a `GEKKO_TOOLBOX` that is not a 64-hex ID, in which case docker is not invoked) the state MUST pass to `caída` with the reason without control characters and bounded to 160, and the guard MUST redraw. `checkToolbox` MUST NOT go back from `caída` to `lista`, MUST NOT query docker outside `toolbox`+`lista` or with `ctx.mode` other than `tui`, MUST NOT overlap with itself, MUST discard the result if the mode changed while it ran, and the timer MUST NEVER throw (a stale `ctx` is discarded as in the rest of the refreshes).
- **WHEN** `checkToolbox` with a fake `docker`: `<ID> true`; `<ID> false`; another ID; error with hostile text (escapes, `#[bg=red]`, 400 characters, two lines); expired timeout; empty output; already in `caída` with a good output; in `os` and in `levantando`; two overlapping calls; a `changeToOs()` while it runs; and a malformed `GEKKO_TOOLBOX`. The real guard mounted against a fake `pi` with the timers captured: loaded in `toolbox` with and without ID; with the `exec` file rewritten and a `session_start`; the timer with TUI and zero shells, with `ctx.mode` `rpc`, and with a `ctx` whose `mode` throws.
- **THEN** only `<ID> true` leaves `lista` and the call is one, with the exact argv and timeout 3000; every other case gives `caída` with a reason ≤160 without controls; from `caída` it neither queries nor leaves; in `os`/`levantando` zero calls; the second overlapping call does not call; the in-flight mode change is not overwritten; on loading, `toolbox` with ID gives `lista` and without ID `caída`; after rewriting `exec` and `session_start` the mode, the ID and the state do not change; with TUI and no shells the timer goes to `caída` and redraws; with `rpc` it does not query; a stale `ctx` does not make the timer throw.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `checkToolbox`, `checkToolboxLive`, `refreshShellsLive`, guard loading (`execStateAtLoad`)
- Limit: the gate uses a fake docker (`execDeps.run`); real docker is tested by the harness (`GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1`: `docker kill` of the container and the check goes to `caída`), outside the gate. A docker error or an expired timeout count as «not ok»: a hung or permissionless docker leaves the toolbox `caída` even though the container lives, and one gets out by retrying from the overlay (it adopts the one that runs). Worst case of detection: 6 s. Without TUI there is no check (`session_new` fails closed by ID on creating). Operations on shells that already exist do not repeat the check (REQ-TBX-003, Limit). A `docker inspect` that answers at one instant proves nothing about the next.

### REQ-TBX-010 — The execution mode is changed with Pi open only from the overlay and `/gekko-toolbox`, lives in memory and is stored only when the change succeeds
`changeToToolbox()` MUST, without a workspace (`GEKKO_WORKSPACE` absent, `none` or with an invalid name, or without `GEKKO_WORKSPACE_DIR`), refuse without invoking docker or the CLI and say how to create one; with a workspace MUST, in this order: set the state `levantando`, `GEKKO_EXEC=toolbox` and delete `GEKKO_TOOLBOX`; create `exec` with `os` if it does not exist (the container mounts it and requires a regular file); run, asynchronously and with a 120 s timeout, the same CLI as the launcher (`node --no-warnings --experimental-strip-types <gekko-workspace.ts> toolbox <ws>`, which validates the workspace and runs `ensureToolbox`); accept its output only if the code is 0 and it is a 64-hex ID; on success set `GEKKO_TOOLBOX=<ID>`, the state `lista` and ONLY then store `toolbox` with `writeExecMode`. On any failure (CLI error, expired timeout, absent program, output that is not an ID, exception) it MUST leave the state `caída` with the first line of the error without control characters and bounded to 160, `GEKKO_EXEC` in `toolbox` (it never goes to `os` on its own) and the stored mode UNCHANGED. There MUST NOT be two starts at once, and an epoch counter MUST discard the result of a start that is no longer the current one. If the mode cannot be stored, it MUST stay `lista` in memory and say so, without writing through a link. `changeToOs()` MUST set `GEKKO_EXEC=os`, delete `GEKKO_TOOLBOX`, leave the state at `null` and store `os`, without invoking docker (the container stays up and the shells it already has stay where they were born, REQ-TBX-011). The mode of a running Pi lives in memory (`GEKKO_EXEC`, `GEKKO_TOOLBOX` and `runtime.exec`, always together): Pi MUST NOT reread `exec` after launch, and only writes that file on reaching `lista` and on confirming the return to `os`. The functions that change the mode or stop the container (`changeToToolbox`, `changeToOs`, `toolboxOff`) MUST be reachable only from the `/gekko-policy` overlay and from `/gekko-toolbox`, and the two MUST refuse when `ctx.mode` is not `tui`; no tool registered for the model (`registerTool`) MUST reach them, nor MUST `shell-session.ts` import them, write `GEKKO_EXEC`/`GEKKO_TOOLBOX` or store the mode.
- **WHEN** `changeToToolbox` and `changeToOs` with a fake CLI/docker (`execDeps.run`) and a temporary workspace: without a workspace, with `none`; the happy path without an `exec` file (the environment, the state and the file are looked at INSIDE the call to the CLI, the argv and the timeout), and repeated over the existing file (the inode); six failures (CLI error with hostile 400-character text with escapes and `#[`, output that is not an ID, empty output, expired timeout, absent program, the executor throws); two overlapping starts; a `changeToOs()` while the start is in flight; `exec` turned into a symbolic link; the return to `os` with the container `lista`; the text of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` and `toolbox/shell-session.ts` passed through the contract (`execPathViolations`) and nine bent copies (a guard tool that changes the mode; `changeToOs()` in `tool_call`; a write of `GEKKO_EXEC` in `tool_call`; `toolboxOff()` in `tool_result`; the overlay and `/gekko-toolbox` without the `ctx.mode` check; `shell-session.ts` that imports the guard, that writes `GEKKO_EXEC` or that stores the mode); and, with real docker (`GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1`, temporary root and workspace of unique name, `GEKKO_OVPN` undefined): the step from `os`, the `docker kill` of the container, the retry, a start with a nonexistent `GEKKO_TOOLBOX_IMAGE` and `off`.
- **THEN** without a workspace nothing is invoked and the message says how to create one; during the call the state is `levantando`, `GEKKO_EXEC` is `toolbox`, there is no `GEKKO_TOOLBOX` and `exec` says `os` (it was created); the CLI is `toolbox <ws>` with timeout 120000; on finishing there is `lista`, the ID, and `exec` says `toolbox` with the same inode; each failure gives `caída` with a non-empty reason, ≤160 and without controls, the mode in toolbox, without ID and `exec` intact; the second start is refused without calling the CLI; the obsolete result is discarded (state `null`, mode `os`); with `exec` a link it stays `lista` in memory, says so and the link's target is not written; returning to `os` changes memory and file with zero calls; the contract gives no violations on the real text and gives at least one per bent copy; with real docker the step reaches `lista` with the container's ID and only then `exec` says `toolbox`, the `kill` leads to `caída` in the check, the retry returns to `lista`, the nonexistent image ends in `caída` with `exec` as it was, and `off` leaves `os` in memory and file.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `changeToToolbox`, `changeToOs`, `activeWorkspace`, `execDeps`, `execPathViolations`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `writeExecMode`, CLI `toolbox`; `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` (real docker)
- Limit: the gate tests the functions with a fake CLI/docker and the overlay with a component driven from the selftest; what only real docker proves is the actual start, the adoption and the container dying; what nothing proves is a real Pi TUI delivering the keys. The contract is regex over the text (regions by indentation; without a dynamically built path) and does NOT test WHO presses the key: the model can type into Pi's TUI with `tmux send-keys` from its host `bash` and answer the confirmation (GAP-78), and from its `bash` it can write `exec` (changes the mode of the next launch, REQ-TBX-001) or use `docker`; what is guaranteed is that the running Pi's mode does not change because of that (nobody rereads `exec`) and that a replaced container is only used if the operator activates again and passes the adoption check. Moving to toolbox with the container already running ADOPTS it after the check of REQ-TBX-003; a `docker run` by the model between the confirmation and the start is adopted under a text that said «crear» ("create"). The epoch discards results, it does not cancel the in-flight CLI (the container may end up created). A workspace without `.pi-sessions` makes the start fail with the CLI's reason. The state of the inherited `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR` is not touched (the sockets directory comes from the place, REQ-TBX-008).

### REQ-TBX-011 — Each shell remembers where it was born and is only handled there; the sockets directory comes from that place
`session_new` MUST read the place (`{ mode, container? }`) from the environment before the first `await`, store it in the own shell and in the panel's registry, and every operation on an own shell (`ours`, `alive`, `session_send`, `session_read` on each poll, `session_kill`, the `kill-session` of the failure paths and the `mouse` option) MUST pass that shell's place: a shell born in one place MUST NOT be handled through the other, however the mode changes. The sockets directory MUST come from `socketDir` with the shell's place on each call: in `toolbox` `<ws>/.gekko-sockets`, in `os` outside the workspace; when `socketDir` throws, a request with a label MUST give FAIL in Spanish without creating anything, and the workspace directory MUST be validated BEFORE creating the sockets directory (a deleted one is not recreated). The rejection of a sockets directory that is a symbolic link is kept. `session_list` MUST query the current place plus each distinct place of the own shells, label each entry with its place and consider as own only the one that matches in (socket, name) AND place. `session_new` MUST refuse, without creating anything, a name that already belongs to a live own shell in the OTHER place (`otherPlaceShells`); in the same place the usual collision is kept. The result of `session_new` MUST include `lugar=os` or `lugar=toolbox`. A replaced container is another place (`samePlace` compares the ID).
- **WHEN** `samePlace` with the same place, `os` against `toolbox` and two different containers; `placesToProbe` with duplicates and in both orders; `otherPlaceShells` with the same name in the other place, in the same one and with another name; and, in the handler harness (`toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts`): an `os` shell with a label with `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR=<ws>/.gekko-sockets` inherited, a workspace with no safe place outside it, a deleted workspace, and with real docker (`GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1`) the mode change under live shells in both directions with the same name in the other place.
- **THEN** the place is the mode plus the container; the list queries each place once, the current one first; only the other place counts as a conflict; in the harness the `os` socket falls outside the workspace, with no safe place there is FAIL without creating anything and the deleted directory does not reappear; the shell born in the toolbox keeps answering `session_send`/`session_read`/`session_list`/`session_kill` inside the container after moving to `os` (and the host's after returning), the new one is born in the current place with `lugar=` and the same name in the other place is refused.
The panel refresh (spec 014 T4, D2) MUST poll each registry shell where it was born: `tmuxRO(args, place?)` uses the place it receives and only without it the current mode, and the live driver looks up each shell's place in the registry (server + session id or name); an entry without a place is treated as the host's.
- **WHEN** the real guard with a fake `tmux` and `docker` on the `PATH` that record their argv, three registered shells (host, toolbox with ID, and one without a place) and the 3 s timer fired, with the current mode `os` and with `toolbox`/`lista`.
- **THEN** the host's shell is polled with `tmux -S <sock> has-session`, the toolbox's with `docker exec <ID> tmux -S <sock> has-session`, the one without a place on the host, and none through the wrong place, in both current modes.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `samePlace`, `placesToProbe`, `otherPlaceShells`, `ShellPlace`, `Shell.place`; `toolbox/shell-session.ts` `placeInForce`, `tmux`, `sockPath`, `sockDirFor`, `ours`, `alive`, `session_*`; `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `socketDir`; `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `tmuxRO`, `placeOf`, `liveDriver`
- Limit: the gate tests only the three pure functions; that each operation passes its shell's place, the sockets directory per place, the FAIL without a safe place, the workspace validation before creating and the label of `session_list` are tested by the harness (cases with an intermediary `docker` in `os` and mode change with real docker), outside the gate. Nothing tests that a new operation that forgets to pass the place fails: `tmux(args, timeout, socket, place?)` falls back to the current mode without it, and that is the probable cause of an omission. The place is that of the environment at the time of the call (the guard rewrites it when changing mode, T4). The sockets directory of an already created shell is recomputed on each call with the environment of that moment; the path is not stored. The name is refused only against a LIVE own shell of the other place: a dead or foreign one does not count, and after `session_start` (which forgets the own ones) the name is free.

### REQ-TBX-012 — `/gekko-toolbox off` stops only this workspace's container, with confirmation, and always leaves a way out of `caída` inside Pi
`/gekko-toolbox` MUST refuse without touching anything when `ctx.mode` is not `tui`; with another argument (or none) MUST show the mode, the state and the usage without calling docker. `toolboxOff(confirm)` MUST, without a workspace, say how to create one without invoking docker; in `levantando` refuse; otherwise, make a NEW `docker inspect --type container --format '{{.Id}} {{.State.Running}}' gekko-tb-<ws>` by name (timeout 3 s; the name comes from the already validated workspace) and: if this session validated an ID and the `inspect` one is another, NOT touch that container; if the `inspect` fails (no docker, permission, timeout) or the container does not exist, stop nothing and say so; in those three cases, with the mode in `toolbox`, still offer —with confirmation— to leave the mode at `os` (from `caída` there is always a way out inside Pi), and with the mode at `os` say there is nothing to do. With the own container: already stopped and mode `os` → say so without confirming or calling `docker stop`; running, or with mode `toolbox`, MUST ask for confirmation with the name, the short ID, that ALL the shells it has inside die (and how many this session knows: those in the registry whose place is that container and are not closed), that the VPN inside it goes down and that the mode goes to `os` and the `bash` block against scope hosts stops applying. After accepting it MUST run `docker stop <ID>` by ID only if it was running (if it fails, say so and do NOT change the mode), mark closed the shells of that place in the registry and go to `os` in memory and stored (`changeToOs`). A refusal changes nothing. No call MUST address a container other than `gekko-tb-<ws>` or the ID its `inspect` returned.
- **WHEN** the real command mounted against a fake `pi`, with a fake docker (`execDeps.run`) that records each call and a `confirm` that keeps the text: with `ctx.mode` `rpc`; with another argument and none; without a workspace; in `levantando`; with the container running and two known shells (text, exact argv of the two calls and later state); already stopped and in `os`; with the operator refusing; with `docker stop` failing with a long error; with an `inspect` that returns another ID; with `inspect` failing (absent program) and with a nonexistent container, both from `caída`; in `os` without a container; in `os` with the container running; and, with real docker (`GEKKO_TEST_TOOLBOX=1`), `off` on the container created by the step to toolbox with a second disposable container alive, and a second `off`.
- **THEN** each refusal calls nothing; the confirmation contains name, short ID, «TODAS las shells» ("ALL the shells"), the count, the VPN, the step to `os` and the `bash` block; the calls are exactly one `inspect` by name and one `stop <ID>`; after accepting the mode is `os` in memory and file and the shells of that container are shown closed; already stopped in `os` makes a single call and asks nothing; refusing neither stops nor changes; a failed `stop` leaves the mode and says so in under 400 characters; with another ID there is no `stop` and moving to `os` is offered; with a failed `inspect` or nonexistent container there is no `stop` and from `caída` one reaches `os` with confirmation; no call names another container; with real docker `off` stops the workspace's container and only that one (the disposable one keeps running), leaves `os` in memory and file, and the second `off` does not call `docker stop`.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `toolboxOff`, `lookupContainer`, `markShellsClosed`, `toolboxStatusText`, command `gekko-toolbox`; `toolbox/tests/handler-harness.ts` (real docker)
- Limit: `docker stop` without `rm`: the container stays and is reused or started at the next step to toolbox. The shells of the container that this session does not know die (after a session change or with another Pi on the same workspace, which will see it `caída` at its next check): that is why the text says ALL. If the session validated no ID (launched in `os`) the ID returned by the `inspect` by the name `gekko-tb-<ws>` is accepted: a foreign container with that name would be stopped after the confirmation that names it. The confirmation does not authenticate whoever presses the key (GAP-78). The `docker` message is third-party text: it comes out without controls and bounded. The gate tests the command with a fake docker; the real `docker stop`, only the harness with real docker.



### REQ-TBX-013 — A new workspace is not created if its container already exists
The menu's creation flow and `gekko-workspace.ts new` (when the workspace does not exist yet) MUST ask, after validating the name and before creating anything, whether the container `gekko-tb-<name>` already exists; if it exists, MUST NOT create the workspace and MUST say how to delete it (`docker rm -f gekko-tb-<name>`): the menu MUST ask for the name again and `new` MUST exit ≠ 0. `new` on a workspace that already exists MUST NOT query it (its container is its own). Without docker data (not installed, no permission or no response) it MUST create anyway.
- **WHEN** the flow with injected I/O receives a name whose container exists and then a free one; the same name followed by end of input; and a name with docker without data; `dockerContainerExists` with a fake `run` that gives 0, «No such object», 127 and another error; and in `tools/ws-test.sh`, with a fake `docker`, `new clash` with the container present and `new boxed` on an existing workspace with its container.
- **THEN** only the second name is created and the text carries `docker rm -f gekko-tb-<name>`; with end of input nothing is created; without docker data it is created; `dockerContainerExists` gives `true`, `false`, `null` and `null`; `new clash` exits ≠ 0 without creating the folder and with the command to delete it; `new boxed` continues with rc 0.
- Code: `toolbox/gekko-workspace.ts` `createFlow`, `PickIo.containerExists`, `realPickIo`, `dockerContainerExists`, `containerClashText`, `createWorkspace`; `tools/ws-test.sh`
- **Limit:** the launcher still rejects afterwards the foreign container of a workspace that already exists (`ensureToolbox`). If docker is slow, the creation waits up to the `timeout` of `realRun` (120 s).
### REQ-RPT-070 — Images in comments
Adding a comment MUST be able to attach live «Adjuntos» images of that workspace or uploaded ones (validated as in REQ-RPT-058/059), all or nothing under the ledger's lock: at most 8, distinct, the text remains mandatory, and an uploaded image MUST NOT remain if the comments file is broken or full; a double submission (same id, text and images) MUST NOT write and the same id with other images MUST be `stale`. A malformed `images` in the comments file (not a list, invalid or repeated hash, more than 8, empty in `add`) MUST be a bad line. Deleting from «Adjuntos» MUST remove the image from the comments (`edit` events with the same text, written first; removal entry with `comentarios`; fingerprint with the comments file's size; file deleted last), a broken, over-cap or full comments file MUST reject with `comments_state` without writing anything, an Adjuntos index with no room for the removal entry MUST reject with `gallery_state` before touching the comments, and a failure at any step MUST leave at most an unused image or a file with its removal entry, never a comment or a row pointing to a deleted file. Comments with images MUST NOT change `render*`, the report or the request to the Provider.
- **WHEN** `comment_add` with one chosen and one uploaded, repeated, with other images, with a foreign hash, absent, with 9, with blank text, with the comments file broken and at its cap; hostile hand-written `images`; `gallery_delete` with one row and two comments, with a new comment after showing the gallery, with broken or at-cap comments, with the Adjuntos index full and with an injected failure at each step; two threads × 20 of deletion and comment with image; `render*` with and without those comments.
- **THEN** the event carries the union without repeats and the upload remains with its entry; the refusals write nothing and do not leave the upload; each comment loses the image through an `edit` and the removal entry names the comments; the old fingerprint is `stale`; no state leaves a reference to a deleted file; the threads finish; the outputs are byte for byte equal.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings.py` `comment_add`, `_comment_append`, `_comment_images`, `_apply_comment_event`, `_fold_gallery`, `delete_stamp`, `gallery_delete`
- Limit: fixed lock order: ledger → comments; `comment_add` without images only takes the comments one. Deleting from «Adjuntos» marks the comment as edited (it is an `edit` event).

### REQ-RPT-071 — A single script, pinned by hash, only on the report page
`/w/<ws>/reporte` (GET, «Vista previa» ("Preview") and its 409/422) MUST carry exactly one `<script>` whose content is the constant `UNSAVED_JS` (sha256 `c31444541cf6a6db69acd108173ec0ec556d49cd0d38122805f269ad45187bde`, pinned in the test), right before `</body>`, and the CSP of the other pages plus `; script-src 'sha256-<that hash in base64>'` and nothing else; every other response (index, list, detail, gallery, image, 4xx/413, status, edit, comment and chat rejections) and the `.md`/`.html`/`.imprimir` downloads MUST NOT carry `<script` or `script-src`. The script MUST touch only the `#borrador` form and `beforeunload` (no network, storage, cookies, `eval` or imports) and the form MUST carry `data-sin-guardar` only when the page shows unsaved values. `markup_problems(page, report=True)` MUST remove exactly one occurrence of that script and fail with any other.
- **WHEN** GET of the report; «Vista previa», a save with an old draft (409) and one with a field over 4096 bytes (422); and a sweep of the index, the list, the detail, the edit, the output, the gallery, the chat, two 404s, three rejections and the three downloads; `markup_problems` with the pinned script, an altered one, two and a foreign one.
- **THEN** the report carries a single script equal to `UNSAVED_JS`, the exact CSP and `data-sin-guardar` only in the three responses with unsaved values; no other response carries `<script` or `script-src`; `markup_problems(report=True)` passes only with the pinned script.
- Code: `.claude/tools/findings_web.py` `UNSAVED_JS`, `UNSAVED_JS_HASH`, `CSP_REPORT`, `response_headers(csp=)`, `page(script=)`, `report_page`, `draft_form`, `markup_problems(report=)`
- Limit: `beforeunload` is decided by the browser (it may omit the dialog without prior interaction and a download link may trigger it): it is an aid, not a control. That the script warns in a real browser is shown by an agent's Playwright run (spec 024 AC6), not the gate.

## 4. Known gaps

These are not requirements (ids `GAP-n`, never the requirement prefix). Closing a defect
follows the reverse reconciliation of §2b: failing test → fix → requirement, in the
same commit. A closed defect leaves this section and becomes a requirement; whatever
its fix still does not cover goes in that requirement's "Limit" line, not here. The repros come
from an adversarial review on copies of the tree and throwaway repos; those of
exported pure functions were re-run when this section was written.

### A. Confirmed defects

Reproduced behavior that contradicts what CLAUDE.md, a code comment, this very spec or a
feature spec reconciled here promise. Each one: file and function, and a one-line repro.

- GAP-39 — **Short dotted IPv4 and an IP inside a prefixed hostname get past the scope stage.** `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `TARGET_RE` / `VERSION_PREFIX` / `decide`. Contradicts the case 'IP embedded in hostname still counts'. Repro: `decide({command:'curl http://app.10.6.6.6.nip.io/'}, scope={192.0.2.10})` → `block:false`; same for `curl http://10-6-6-6.nip.io/`, `ping 10.6.1542`, `ping 10.394758`, `curl http://192.0.2.10/Chrome/10.6.6.6`, `curl -A Chrome/10.6.6.6 http://192.0.2.10/`, `nc ==10.6.6.6 80` (the last two because of `VERSION_PREFIX`: the prefix exempts the IP even when it is a real argument).
- GAP-42 — **Destructive patterns evadable with trivial variants, and false positives on data.** `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `DESTRUCTIVE` / `destructiveReason`. Contradicts CLAUDE.md ('blocking before execution … destructive actions') and the comment 'the word as DATA … must not block'. Repro: `decide({command:'rm -rfv /'}, s)` → `block:false`; same for `rm -rf "/"`, `rm -rf /;`, `echo hi\nreboot`, `systemctl poweroff`, `sudo -n reboot`, `dd if=/dev/zero of=/dev/dm-0`, `tee /dev/sda < x`, `ip6tables -F`. In the opposite direction `decide({command:'grep -r mkfs kb/'}, s)` → `block:true` (also `man wipefs`, `git commit -m 'fix; reboot handling'`, `iptables -f -L`).
- GAP-46 — **Loot read through a parent directory is only fenced, not sanitized.** `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` `touchesLoot` / `resultPolicy`. Contradicts the FIX-B comment ('any input string pointing there … MUST pass through the firewall'). Repro: `resultPolicy('grep', {pattern:'x', path:'engagements'})` → `'fence'`; same for `{path:'../Gekko/engagements'}`, `{pattern:'x', path:'.'}` and `{pattern:'x'}`. In the opposite direction `{pattern:'engagements/', path:'kb/'}` → `'sanitize'`.
- GAP-47 — **Classifier false positives that the code says it avoids.** `toolbox/gekko-panel.ts` `BRUTEFORCE_RE` / `LATERAL_RE` / `CRED_DUMP_RE` / `SERVICE_CTRL_RE`. Contradicts the comments 'plain CME enumeration does not FP' and the anti-FP anchor for reads. Repro: `classifyChanges('nxc smb 10.0.0.9 -u admin -p Password1 --shares')` → `['bruteforce']` (block in all modes; the `i` flag makes `-p` and `-P` equal); `classifyChanges('grep psexec.py notes.md')` → `['lateral']`; `classifyChanges('cat /usr/bin/hydra')` → `['bruteforce']` (also `cat ./hydra`, `ls /opt/hydra/hydra`: the `BFP` path prefix does not distinguish executing from reading); `classifyChanges('grep secretsdump notes.md')` → `['cred-dump']` (also `cat secretsdump.out`, `ls impacket/examples/secretsdump.py`, `less security.md | grep 'reg save sam'`: those `CRED_DUMP_RE` alternatives do not carry the `CMD` anchor); `classifyChanges('iisreset /status')` → `['service-control']`.
- GAP-78 — **The model can type into the Pi TUI, and with that answer the operator's confirmations.** `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` (`tool_call`, no rule for `tmux` in `bash`) and everything that asks for a key: the confirmation to add a host to the scope (`ctx.ui.confirm`, REQ-SCP-002), the per-mode admission, the `/gekko-policy` overlay (AGGRESSIVE mode, pre-authorizations, mirror check) and, since spec 014, the execution-mode change and `/gekko-toolbox off`. Pi runs inside the operator's tmux and the model's `bash` is a child of Pi, with the same user. Contradicts CLAUDE.md ('an ADDED host needs the operator') and the premise of every confirmation. Repro: `tmux send-keys -t "$TMUX_PANE" '/gekko-policy' Enter` from `bash` → the guard lets it through without asking, and a child process that writes like this into its parent's pane is read by the parent as typing (independent review of 2026-11-16, at the tmux level and of the real guard handler in three configurations; not exercised against a live Pi). With the default socket the pane is found even if the environment carries no `TMUX_PANE`. A pattern veto of the `tmux` that writes into foreign terminals stops it in its tested forms but does not close it: with `bash` as the same user there are other routes to the terminal. The hard guarantee remains the container and the egress list.

### B. No case

What the boundary does or declares but no executed case pins down yet.

- GAP-1 — `scope.txt` hot-reload: in the TUI branch (`ctx.ui.confirm`) accepting has no case; rejecting is exercised in REQ-SCP-003 and the headless branch in REQ-SCP-002. A process restart loads the file as-is, without confirm (`gekko-guard.ts:425`): it is a known limit, not a case.
- GAP-2 — `loadScope` without a case: parsing of lines/comments/CIDR, missing file = fail-closed, and the ALWAYS in-scope entries it adds by itself (IPv4 of local interfaces, `127.0.0.0/8`, `0.0.0.0`).
- GAP-3 — Fail-closed of the `tool_call` handler: only the exception injected by `GEKKO_GUARD_FAULT` is exercised (REQ-HLT-002); a real failure of `reloadScope` or of a stage has no case. `session_start` sets guard `ok=true` again: a guard that failed is considered healthy in the next session of the same process.
- GAP-4 — `LOCAL_TOOLS` exemption (read/edit/write/ls/grep/find skip DoS/destructive/scope/admission but remain subject to limits): no case. It is the decision that allows writing `scope.txt` and the per-file laundering (writing a script with an out-of-scope IP or a flood and running it with `bash x.sh`). Brute-force laundered through a shell script run directly was observed in a real run on 2026-11-16 with a valid authorization (it passed without the authorization being consulted) and since then REQ-ADM-014 covers those forms; the out-of-scope IP and flood forms remain open.
- GAP-5 — Brute-force deny in the `tool_call` handler (classification over `scanInput` + `authorizedFor` over the command field, in all modes including headless, except with a named valid authorization): only the classifier and the pure logic are tested, not the wiring.
- GAP-6 — Firewall wiring in `tool_result`: sanitization failure ⇒ guard not healthy; `structuredContent` is forwarded WITHOUT sanitizing or fencing if there was no failure and is omitted if it failed; non-text blocks pass as they are; empty content returns untouched. No case. (The earlier exception in the firewall with a malformed element: closed, REQ-FW-008.)
- GAP-7 — DoS patterns: only `hping3 --flood` has a case. No case: `hping3 -i uN`, t50, mhddos, slowloris, goldeneye, torshammer, loic, hoic, hulk, `ping -f`. The list is not exhaustive (limit of REQ-BRK-005).
- GAP-8 — Injection patterns without a case: directive header (`[STRIPPED-DIRECTIVE-HEADER]`), config mimicry (CLAUDE.md / `.claude/rules`), labeled block (```` ```system ````); and no case tests parity with the canonical Python `sanitize_target_content.py` (the code says "mirror").
- GAP-9 — Configuration parsing without a case: `GEKKO_ALLOW_DESTRUCTIVE` only accepts 1/true/yes; `intEnv` falls back to the default on an invalid or negative value; defaults `maxRepeat=3`, `maxCalls=0`, `maxTokens=0` (caps off by default).
- GAP-10 — Per-session limits wiring: `session_start`→`resetState`, `session_compact`→`forgetLoops`, tokens from `tool_result.usage`, `recordResult` only for execution tools, and that a call already blocked by deny/scope does not inflate counters.
- GAP-11 — Destructive, variants that the pattern DOES cover but without a case: rm on home (`~`, `$HOME`), `rm -rf /*`, `dd of=/dev/nvme…`, `iptables --flush`. (The ones it does not cover: GAP-42.)
- GAP-12 — Scope non-guarantees (limit, NOT a guarantee): only 4-octet dotted IPv4 is gated; hostnames, IPv6 and integer/hex forms of an IP (e.g. `http://167772165/`) are not evaluated. This is delegated to the container's egress (header of `gekko-guard.ts`). Short dotted forms and an IP inside a prefixed hostname are not gated either: GAP-39.
- GAP-14 — Per-session reset in `session_start`: authorizations (`runtime.auth = []`), mode, KB log, activity and SESSION counters (`runtime.counts`) are reset; only `resetMode` has a case.
- GAP-15 — "The model has no tool to change the mode; only operator callbacks" is asserted by construction in comments; no case checks it.
- GAP-16 — `requestMode` treats an exception from `apply` as a failure (catch → ok=false): only an `apply` that returns false is tested, not one that throws.
- GAP-17 — `targetIp`: a command with several distinct IPv4s is not attributable (null) and a per-IP authorization does not cover it; there is only a case with zero IPs.
- GAP-18 — Authorization expiry only tested for binary-upload in AGGRESSIVE. No case: brute-force with an expired authorization goes back to 'block'; `policyChecked`/`toggleTechnique` with an expired `*` authorization.
- GAP-19 — Classifier patterns without a case: password (chpasswd, an isolated `net user <u> <pw>`, Set-LocalUser -Password, usermod -p), account (userdel/deluser, New-LocalUser, net localgroup /add, dscl), brute-force (patator, netexec, smb_login/ssh_login, --password-spray), persistence (systemctl enable, sc create, reg add …\Run, New-Service, at), cred-dump (mimikatz/pypykatz/lsassy/procdump lsass, lsadump::/sekurlsa::, comsvcs MiniDump, nxc --sam/--lsa/--ntds), service-control (sc stop/config/delete, net stop, Stop-Service), log-cleanup (Clear-EventLog, journalctl --vacuum, history -c, auditpol /clear), binary-upload (put *.exe, rsync, wget, Invoke-WebRequest -OutFile).
- GAP-20 — Admission of persistence and cred-dump: only their classification; no case of `admitChange` (confirm in DEFAULT, allow in AGGRESSIVE+auth).
- GAP-21 — Accepted and documented brute-force FP: the tool name as a bare read argument (`grep hydra`, `man medusa`) blocks. Deliberate behavior without a case.
- GAP-22 — `kbAttribute`: a symlink inside `kb/` that resolves OUTSIDE `kb/` must not be attributed, and a realpath that throws returns null; no case.
- GAP-23 — `recordKb` with `opts.error` (type 'error') and `recordKbFromCommand` skipping wrappers (sudo/timeout/nice/VAR=) or verbs invoked by path: no case.
- GAP-24 — Decision trace: which decisions are written ('bloqueado', 'confirmado', 'auto-aprobado') and that a write failure never alters the decision is wiring without a case; only the pure `auditLine`/`redactCommand` are tested.
- GAP-26 — `compactView` redacts secrets and controls via `redactSummary` with `keepWs` (keeps LF and TAB) before truncating; the cases only test bounding and non-mutation, not the redaction nor which whitespace remains. It has no caller in live code.
- GAP-27 — The real ownership gate of `session_send`/`read`/`kill` (`notOwned` + `ours()`: same server PID and session id before acting, re-verified on every poll, and not killing if a stranger re-took the name) lives in `toolbox/shell-session.ts`, with no selftest. `captureOwn` is not used in live code.
- GAP-28 — `send-keys` uses `--` so that an input that starts with `-` (e.g. `-tVICTIM`) does not redirect the keystroke to a foreign session; no case.
- GAP-29 — Socket rule wiring: rejection via `badSocket` in every tool, resolution of the label to a path under `SOCK_DIR` with mode 0700, `GEKKO_TMUX_DIR` as override; only the pure regex is tested and the mode of a pre-existing directory is not verified.
- GAP-30 — Mapping of raw tmux errors to `Probe` in the `liveDriver`: any failure that is not ENOENT/timeout/stderr 'denied|permission' falls into 'gone' and therefore into closed — the false-closed that REQ-TMUX-005 forbids; the input to the pure function is not tested.
- GAP-31 — `captureOwn` cleans the pane output (`stripControl` + `redactSummary`, both with `keepWs`: keeps LF and TAB); the only case uses the harmless string 'output'. It also captures by name, not by session id like the refresh.
- GAP-32 — `refreshShells`: single concurrent refresh (`reg.refreshing`), not re-probing shells already closed, and reading the current process by session id: no case.
- GAP-33 — `stripControl` does not remove the body of OSC sequences: from `\x1b]0;pwned\x07` what remains is `]0;pwned` as inert text, with no case pinning it down. Removing it would delete printable text up to the terminator, also from the decision trace: it is an operator decision, not a mechanical fix. (C1, CR and CSI: REQ-PNL-001.)
- GAP-34 — `panelDetailLines`: only the last block and the scope have a case with a hostile value (REQ-PNL-008); shell names, authorization targets and the activity label go through the same two reads (`oneLine` + `redactSummary`, and `redactSummary` alone), but without a case. The widget that sanitized nothing was retired with spec 008. The rail and bar that replace it do not draw this view but the cards of `panel.json`: controls are stripped from them and their rows and `bar.lastBlock` are redacted on publish (REQ-TUI-020: with a case for the last block, a scope host and a shell name; without a case for an authorization's target; the branch, the model and the states of other extensions are not redacted).
- GAP-35 — The `/gekko-mode` handler TOGGLES the mode when `parseModeCommand` returns null (invalid or empty argument); the operator runs it, not the model; no case.
- GAP-36 — The launcher contract is tested only by regex over the text: `gekko-guard.ts` in a comment satisfies it, and `noDupGuard` accepts 0 `-e` loads. It is not tested that the guard is actually passed to pi.
- GAP-53 — Limits of the mechanical trace: any executed `ok()` counts, including `ok(true)`, and a `008-AC<n>:` case counts for `REQ-TUI-0nn` by the prefix of its message even if it is about something else (REQ-TUI-010, REQ-TUI-011). A whitespace-only change in this spec satisfies the hook rule. `.githooks/pre-commit` (`touched` / `spec_staged` loop) only checks that the spec's path is staged and does not run the gate: with `M toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` staged, the spec truncated to 0 bytes (`: > specs/SECURITY-GUARDRAILS-SPEC.md`, status `M`) or replaced by a symlink (`ln -sf /dev/null …`, status `T`) → hook `exit=0`. The gate would fail in both cases, but nothing runs it on its own (there is no CI or hook that invokes `GEKKO_GUARD_SELFTEST`).
- GAP-54 — The gate depends on the cwd: REQ-PNL-005 reads the launchers with `process.cwd()`, and a `008-AC1` case reads `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts` the same way, with a `readFileSync` without `try`. From `toolbox/` 2 panel cases fail and that `readFileSync` throws `ENOENT`: the process aborts (`exit=1`) after `gekko-guard selftest: all passed`, without printing the panel line or the trace. Separately: the `REQ trace: …` line is printed even if cases that do not throw have failed; that line alone does not indicate a green gate.
- GAP-62 — A case that cannot fail through the path it names: `008-AC20: un bloqueo hostil partido en filas no produce una segunda fila ● guard`. Changing the `│` glyph of the split rows to `●`, or raising `BLOCK_WRAP` to 6, does not make it fail (re-run on a copy: with `●` two `008-AC12` cases fail, not this one); the hostile text of its fixture falls in the fifth row, which is cut off. It only fails if the genuine `● guard` row disappears.
- GAP-63 — Sub-assertions that cannot fail inside cases that can: in `008-AC14: los reductores devuelven solo {open,cursor}…`, `JSON.stringify(rtR) === uiBefore` compares the fixture's runtime, which the reducers never receive; in `008-AC4: ningún hit apunta fuera…`, the first half compares the list of hits with itself filtered. Also, the snapshot of the shared `runtime` of the other `008-AC14` case is a `JSON.stringify`, which does not see `activity.inflight` (a `Map`).
- GAP-64 — AC1 of spec 008, publication: of the `panel.json` write, only the file's existence after a `session_start` in `tui` mode has a case (REQ-TUI-021); the content, the tmp + rename of `writePanelFile`, the 2 s timer (`refreshPanelLive`) and the SESSION counters have no executed case. The only case of the counters is a regex over the text of `gekko-guard.ts`, which passes with the two lines inside a comment (re-run on a copy). "The rail keeps the last good frame and indicates it" (`gekko-sidebar.mjs` `draw`, line `! último estado válido`) has no case in the gate; since 2026-11-12 four checks of `tools/tui-test.sh` cover it (REQ-TUI-001, Limit). The live smoke that replaced AC15 has an opt-in version in `tools/tui-test.sh --live`: it starts a real Pi with the guard and checks that it publishes `panel.json` and that SESIÓN says `turno ≥ 1` (it asks for endpoint, key and model via environment; not a gate; run on 2026-11-12 with a single model: `47 passed, 0 failed` in two runs, `tasks.md` of 008). That the bar goes from turn N to N+1 and that SESIÓN adds a tool remains manual.
- GAP-65 — The `toolbox/gekko-sidebar.mjs` process is not run in the gate: reading and validating `panel.json`, writing each line to the terminal, raw stdin → `parseMouse` / `toggleAt` / `handleKey` (`KEYS` table, `rest` chaining), `persistUi` and reading `panel.ui.json` at startup. Only `tools/tui-test.sh` exercises it (nested tmux; it is not a gate and no hook runs it), and it has no AC5 or AC20 checks. The pre-commit hook does not run the gate either: that both are green when closing a task is a convention (`specs/008-tui-panel/spec.md` §7), not a mechanism. AC7, AC14, AC19 and AC20 of spec 008 assert properties of that process; the gate tests its pure functions.
- GAP-66 — AC6 of spec 008: nothing it asserts is executed in the gate (no case runs tmux). The gate pins the argv of `sidebarBindings`; the capture of the previous binding, `install_bindings` and `--restore` (`tools/gekko-sidebar.sh`, `tools/pi-hitl.sh`), the focus that does not leave Pi's pane and the identical `list-keys` on exit are only in `tools/tui-test.sh`; the triple click outside the sidebar, not even there.
- GAP-67 — Status bar, wiring without a case: the `railVisible` decision (`sidebarLive`: mtime of `panel.json.alive` under 1500 ms; the 140-column cutoff lives in `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh`), the mount with `ctx.ui.setFooter` and the fallback widget, and the origin of branch, model, ctx % and `(auto)` (`publish`). Any process that touches `panel.json.alive` sets the bar to `railVisible=true`: with guard `ON` it stops showing `guard ON`, scope, shells and the block mark (a guard other than `ON` remains visible) — read in the code, not executed against a live guard. The branch, the model and the states of other extensions enter as segments with no filter other than stripping controls: `renderBar` with an extension state `guard ON` and guard `FALLÓ` gives `… · guard FALLÓ · guard ON`. No process test looks at the bar: the four AC10 checks of `tools/tui-test.sh` only check whether the rail pane exists at 180, 139, 140 and 80×24; outside tmux and with `GEKKO_SIDEBAR=0` nothing runs.
- GAP-69 — AC21 of spec 008: the guard wiring has a case with a fake ctx for rpc and for tui (REQ-TUI-021), and no gate case starts Pi in `-p`, `--mode json` or rpc: the three were run by hand with Pi 1.5.0 on 2026-11-12 (result in the Limit of REQ-TUI-021), not in the gate. No case: `render` and `refreshPanelLive` with `hasUI=false`, the 2 s timer and the git `execFile`.
- GAP-70 — AC16 of spec 008: the comparison of the major number in `ver_ge` (`setup.sh`) is not tested: replacing it with "minor ≥ threshold" passes the 7 cases (the `node 21.9` case does not distinguish it: 9 < 19). No case: `tmux 2.9`, `tmux 4.0`, `v21.20.0`, `v23.0.0`; `npm`, `git` or `python3` missing; the message for a missing binary; and that check-only mode does not install or write.
- GAP-71 — AC17 of spec 008: only `missingAcs` has a case. The wiring that prints the count per AC and fails if an expected one has zero (`acCounts`, `EXPECTED_008_ACS`, the `bad++`) does not: a selftest does not measure itself. The second net is the trace of §2c (an AC reconciled here without cases makes it fail).
- GAP-72 — AC8 and AC9 of spec 008: reading the real theme has no case — `NO_COLOR` from Pi's environment → `noColor` (`publish`: `!!process.env.NO_COLOR`), the 7 tokens of `ctx.ui.theme.getFgAnsi` and the `appearance` inferred from the theme name containing `light` (the selftest's fake `pi` returns `""`). A light theme whose name does not say `light` gets the dark palette in the roles that are missing. `ROLE_RE` accepts any foreground color: a valid role equal to the background leaves an alert unreadable; contrast is only tested in the fallback palette.
- GAP-73 — AC18 of spec 008: the real reader of the panel ledger is `readPanelLedger` (with cases: link, FIFO, cap, nonexistent) and that the guard uses it is checked by text, not by running the extension. The comparison of `FINDING_STATUSES` with `STATUSES` of `.claude/tools/findings.py` is already made by a case of REQ-RPT-003 (via node).
- GAP-74 — Real width in the terminal: `cw` counts 1 cell for emoji and wide symbols outside its ranges (`🚀` `U+1F680`, `⚡`, `✅`): a host of 60 `🚀` at 36 columns measures 36 by `vlen` and carries 31 `🚀` (about 67 real cells). The width oracle of the cases is the module's own `vlen` except in one case, and no test measures the real tmux pane.
- GAP-75 — `setup.sh`, node installation: the branch that offers to install nvm + node 22 when the system node does not run TypeScript has no case and was not run end to end on any machine. It downloads `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvm-sh/nvm/v0.40.1/install.sh` and passes it to `bash` without verifying a checksum (pinned by tag, not by hash), the installer modifies the operator's shell startup file, and `nvm.sh` is loaded with `set +eu`. It asks for confirmation (Enter = yes) and does not run in check-only, without a terminal or without `curl`. No case: that after installing it requires `strip`, that `NVM_DIR` ends up at `$HOME/.nvm` even if inherited from another user, and the final notice to restart the shell.
- GAP-76 — `tools/pi-hitl.sh`, startup without a case in the gate (only manual probes with a fake `pi`): (a) outside tmux, with an interactive terminal and `GEKKO_SIDEBAR` other than `0`, it relaunches itself with `exec tmux new-session …` and, if the launcher fails inside, waits for an Enter instead of closing the session; (b) it loads `.gekko-state/env` (the 0600 file written by `setup.sh`, with the Provider key in plain text) with `.`, that is as shell code: whoever can write that file runs code as the operator on the next launch, just as with `.gekko-state/pi-hitl.env`; (c) what is already exported wins over what is saved (`export -p` before, `eval` after), so an old value in the environment of a tmux server that was already running wins over the file. Probes: with no exports it takes what is saved; with `GEKKO_LLM_KEY` exported the shell's wins; with no file and no exports it exits with the message that points to `setup.sh`.
- GAP-77 — `/gekko-sidebar` and `tools/gekko-sidebar.sh`, without a case in the gate (only `tools/tui-test.sh`: 4 checks of `--toggle` and 4 of narrow window and width): the command toggles (rail open → `kill-pane` and mark `@gekko_sidebar_hidden` on the window; hidden → opens it and clears the mark), `--fit` does not reopen while the mark is there, and the `window-resized` hook is installed before opening and also when the window is under 140 columns. It is not a new route for the model: with `bash` it could already close the rail pane, and it can also run `--toggle`. Read in the code, not executed against a live guard: when the rail closes its `.alive` is deleted and the bar goes back to showing guard, scope, shells and the block mark. Live, only the operator saw it (toggling and resizing), with no automatic record.
- GAP-79 — **No egress allowlist in toolbox mode.** `gekko-tb-<ws>` (REQ-TBX-003) uses the default bridge network: the model reaches any destination the host reaches, and the hostnames, IPv6 and non-dotted forms that the guard does not gate (GAP-12, GAP-39) have no backstop. The text that calls the container + egress allowlist the «hard guarantee» (header of `toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md) assumes a filter that the operator sets up; no case tests it.
- (spec 024) The `beforeunload` warning of `/w/<ws>/reporte` is an aid, not a control: the browser may omit it (REQ-RPT-071).

## 5. Note on the mechanical trace

The line `REQ trace: N requisitos, M casos` proves exactly this: each
`### REQ-…` heading of this file has at least one EXECUTED `ok()` under its
`req("REQ-…")` marker (or, for `REQ-TUI-0nn`, with a message that starts with
`008-AC<n>:`), every marker names an id that exists here and no `ok()` ran without a
marker. It does NOT prove that the case is sufficient for the requirement, nor that
the `ok()` checks anything (an `ok(true)` counts), nor that the requirement's text
describes what the case exercises: that is checked by reading, and the "Limit" lines and
§4 say where it is already known not to be enough. The line is printed even if other cases
have failed (GAP-54);
green gate = `exit 0` with the three lines `all passed` / `all passed` / `REQ trace`.

The trace covers only the guard's gate (`GEKKO_GUARD_SELFTEST=1 node --experimental-strip-types toolbox/gekko-guard.ts`,
run from the repo root, which includes the panel selftest, the workspace selftest and, since
spec 012, the Python selftests of the ledger and the report module: their `ok REQ-…` lines count
as cases, §2a). The hooks are covered by `.githooks/test.sh` and the Python sanitizer by its
own selftest; they are not counted here. The process cases of `tools/report-test.sh`
(the standalone command and the `grep` of the prompts) and those of `tools/ws-test.sh` (the real launcher with a
fake `pi`) do not count either. `.githooks/pre-commit` runs `tools/report-test.sh` when the commit touches
`.claude/tools/findings.py`, `.claude/tools/findings_web.py`, `.claude/tools/sanitize_target_content.py` or a file of `.claude/tools/report-templates/` (spec 021 T12, «plantilla sola» cases of `.githooks/test.sh`) and rejects if it fails or if it is missing: it runs
against the working tree, not against what is staged, depends on the hooks being active
(`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) and is skipped with `--no-verify`; there is no CI that repeats it. The rule of `.githooks/pre-commit` checks that this file is
staged in the same commit as a touched file of §1 (added, modified,
deleted, renamed, copied; override: exact `GEKKO_SPEC_UNCHANGED=1`); deleting or
renaming this spec is blocked with no override. It does not require that a requirement change, nor
that the file keep its content or remain a regular file (GAP-53).
